Companion points and locally analytic socle conjecture for Steinberg case
Yiqin He

Abstract
In this paper, we will modify the Breuil-Hellmann-Schraen’s (more generally, resp., Breuil-Ding’s) local model for the trianguline variety (resp., Bernstein paraboline variety) to certain semistable (resp., potentially semistable) non-crystalline point with regular Hodge-Tate weights. Then we deduce several local-global compatibility results, including a classicality result, and the existence of expected companion points on the (definite) eigenvariety and locally analytic socle conjecture for such semistable non-crystalline Galois representations, under certain hypothesis on trianguline variety and the usual Taylor-Wiles assumptions. Moreover, we also discuss slightly the coherent sheaves obtained by patching argument and the coherent sheaves which are constructed from local models and Bezrukavnikov functor, under the route of the recently work of Hellmann-Hernandez-Schraen.



1
Introduction

Let
p
be a prime number and
n\geq 2
an integer. The aim of this paper is to prove several results in the problem of companion form or locally analytic socle conjecture when the
p
-adic Galois representation is semistable non-crystalline with regular Hodge-Tate weights and full monodromy rank (that we call Steinberg case, which is an extreme case in semistable case).

The socle phenomenon and the Breuil’s locally analytic socle conjecture in
[
10
]
and
[
11
]
are some local-global compatibility results in the locally analytic aspect of the
p
-adic local Langlands program. This conjecture is closely related to the existence of companion points, see
[
42
]
for a statement of the conjecture on the existence of all companion forms for finite slope overconvergent
p
-adic automorphic forms of
{\rm GL}_{n}
(in the language of determining the set of companion points on the eigenvariety that are associated with the same
p
-adic Galois representation but with possibly different weights). These problems were discussed by Breuil-Hellmann-Schraen in
[
17
]
(resp., Z. Wu in
[
61
]
and
[
62
]
, resp., Breuil-Ding in
[
12
]
) when the
p
-adic Galois representation is
generic
crystalline with regular Hodge-Tate weights (resp.,
generic
crystalline with non-regular Hodge-Tate weights, resp., certain
generic
potentially crystalline with regular Hodge-Tate weights).

The Steinberg case is previously explored for
{\rm GL}_{2}(L)
case in
[
28
]
. In this paper, we talk about the local model for the trianguline variety (resp., paraboline deformation ring) at certain semistable non-crystalline (or say Steinberg) point (note that the Steinberg case is
not generic
), then we discuss the existence of companion points and companion constituents on the eigenvariety (resp., Bernstein eigenvariety). Furthermore, we also discuss the coherent sheaves obtained from patching functors and the coherent sheaves constructed from local models and the Bezrukavnikov functor, by following the route of
[
44
]
. The relationship of such two coherent sheaves is predicted by the categorical
p
-adic Langlands correspondence.

Before stating our main results, we briefly give the global setup of the paper (in the setting of definite unitary groups as Breuil). Let
F^{+}
be a totally real field and
F
be a quadratic totally imaginary extension of
F^{+}
. Let
{\mathbf{G}}_{U}
be a unitary group attached to the quadratic extension
F/F^{+}
such that
{\mathbf{G}}_{U}\times_{F^{+}}F\cong{\rm GL}_{n}
and
{\mathbf{G}}_{U}(F^{+}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}}{\mathbf{R}})
is compact. Let
S_{p}
be the set of places of
F^{+}
above
p
, and assume that each place in
S_{p}
is split in
F
. Fix a place
{\mathfrak{p}}
of
F^{+}
above
p
and a place
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{p}}}
of
F
over
{\mathfrak{p}}
. Let
E
be a sufficiently large finite extension of
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. Let
k_{E}
be the reside field of
E
. From now on, we write
L=F_{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
for simplicity.

Let
U^{p}=\prod_{v\nmid p}{\mathbf{G}}_{U}(F_{v}^{+})
(resp.,
U_{p}^{\mathfrak{p}}=\prod_{v|p,p\neq{\mathfrak{p}}}U_{v}
) be a compact open subgroup of
\prod_{v|p,p\neq{\mathfrak{p}}}{\mathbf{G}}_{U}(F_{v}^{+})
(resp.,
\prod_{v\nmid p}{\mathbf{G}}_{U}(F_{v}^{+})
). These give a prime-to-
{\mathfrak{p}}
level
U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}:=U^{p}U_{p}^{\mathfrak{p}}\subseteq{\mathbf{G}}_{U}({\mathbf{A}}_{F^{+}}^{\infty,{\mathfrak{p}}})
. Let
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)
be the space of certain
p
-adic automorphic forms (roughly speaking, the space of
p
-adic algebraic automorphic forms over coefficient field
E
of tame level
U^{p}
, of fixed type
\sigma_{0}
(a smooth representation of
{\rm GL}_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
) at the place
S_{p}\setminus\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}
, full level at
p
, and whose weight is
0
at places above
{\mathfrak{p}}
, and given by some fixed weight at each of the places in
S_{p}\setminus\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}
). This space is an unitary Banach space representation of
{\mathbf{G}}_{U}(F_{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{p}}}})={\rm GL}_{n}(L)
(so that its locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic vectors
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm an}}
forms an admissible locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation of
{\rm GL}_{n}(L)
). This space is also equipped with a faithful action of a certain commutative global Hecke algebra
{\mathbf{T}}^{S_{p},{\rm univ}}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
which is generated by some prime-to-
p
Hecke operator.

Let
\overline{{\rho}}:{\rm Gal}_{F}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(k_{E})
be a
{\textrm{mod}}\;p
irreducible representation. We can associate to
\overline{\rho}
a maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\rho}}
of
{\mathbf{T}}^{S_{p},{\rm univ}}
. Let
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm an}}_{\overline{\rho}}
be the localization of
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm an}}
at
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\rho}}
(with respect to the
{\mathbf{T}}^{S_{p},{\rm univ}}
-structure). There is a rigid analytic variety
Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})
over
E
(called the Hecke eigenvariety) that parametrizes the systems of Hecke eigenvalues of finite slope in the representation
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm an}}_{\overline{\rho}}
(more generally, Bernstein Hecke eigenvariety
[
12
]
, which parametrizes the systems of certain Hecke eigenvalues which are not of finite slope).

Suppose that
E
is sufficiently large, i.e.
\Sigma_{L}:=\{\sigma:L\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\}=\{\sigma:L\hookrightarrow E\}
. Let
d_{L}:=|\Sigma_{L}|
. Put
q_{L}:=p^{f_{L}}
, where
f_{L}
denotes the unramified degree of
L
over
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. Let
{\rm GL}_{n}
be the general linear group over
L
. Let
{\mathbf{B}}
(resp.,
{\mathbf{T}}
) be the Borel subgroup of upper triangular matrices (resp., the diagonal torus). Let
{\mathfrak{g}}
(resp.,
{\mathfrak{t}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{b}}
) be the
E
-Lie algebra of
{\rm GL}_{n}
(resp.,
{\mathbf{T}}\subseteq{\mathbf{B}}
). Let
{\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}}
be the parabolic subgroup of
{\rm GL}_{n}
opposite to
{\mathbf{B}}
. Let
{\mathbf{G}}_{L}:=(\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\rm GL}_{n})\times_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
(resp.,
{\mathbf{T}}_{L}:=(\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{T}})\times_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E\subseteq{\mathbf{B}}_{L}:=(\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{B}})\times_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
. Let
{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}
(resp.,
{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}\subseteq{\mathfrak{b}}_{L}
) be the
E
-Lie algebra of
{\mathbf{G}}_{L}
(resp.,
{\mathbf{T}}_{L}\subseteq{\mathbf{B}}_{L}
). We have
{\mathbf{G}}_{L}\cong\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}{\rm GL}_{n}
and
{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}\cong\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}{\mathfrak{g}}
, etc. Let
\mathscr{W}_{n,L}\cong S_{n}^{d_{L}}
be the Weyl group of
{\mathbf{G}}_{L}
. We put
G={\rm GL}_{n}(L)
. Let
w_{0}
(resp.,
\underline{w}_{0}:=(w_{0})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
) be the longest element in
\mathscr{W}_{n}
(resp.,
\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
).

A point
y\in Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})
can be uniquely described by a pair
(\rho_{y},\underline{\delta})
, where
\rho
is a Galois deformation of
\overline{\rho}
on
E
and
\underline{\delta}=\delta_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\delta_{n}
is a locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic character of
L^{n}={\mathbf{T}}(L)
. We are interested in point
y=(\rho_{y},\underline{\delta})
that are semistable non-crystalline with full monodromy rank (we say that
y
is of
Steinberg type
), this means that the local Galois representation
\rho_{L}:=\rho_{y}|_{{\rm Gal}_{F_{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}}=\rho_{y}|_{{\rm Gal}_{L}}
is semistable non-crystalline, and the monodromy operator
N
on
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho_{L})
satisfies
N^{n-1}=0
(thus the associated smooth representation of
G
is the Steinberg representation via the classical local Langlands correspondence).

Under this assumption, we can associate to
y
two permutations
w_{y}=(w_{y,\tau}),w_{\mathcal{F}}=(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau})\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
. The first one measuring the relative positions of the weights
{\rm wt}(\delta_{i})
of
\delta_{i}
with the dominant order, and the second one measuring the relation position of two flags coming from the
p
-adic Hodge theory (i.e., the unique
(\varphi,N)
-stable flag and the Hodge filtration, see Section
2.2
). More precisely, let
{\mathbf{h}}:=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,1}>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,2}>\cdots>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,n})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
be the Hodge-Tate weights of
\rho_{L}
(we fix such
{\mathbf{h}}
throughout this paper). For
1\leq i\leq n
, put
{{\mathbf{h}}}_{i}=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
. Then
w_{y}
and
w_{\mathcal{F}}
are given as follows.

•

Note that
D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{L})
is trianguline. Assume that
D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{L})
admits a triangulation
\mathcal{F}
with parameters
{\rm unr}({\alpha})z^{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}}_{1})},\;\cdots\,\;{\rm unr}({\alpha q_{L}^{i-1}})z^{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}}_{i})},\;\cdots\,\;,{\rm unr}({\alpha q_{L}^{n-1}})z^{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}}_{n})}
for some
\alpha\in E
, where
w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}}_{i})=({\mathbf{h}}_{\tau,(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0})^{-1}(i)})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L},1\leq i\leq n}
.

•

{\rm wt}_{\tau}(\delta_{i})={\mathbf{h}}_{\tau,\;w_{y,\tau}^{-1}(i)}
for
1\leq i\leq n
and
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
.

If
w=1
, we say
y
is strictly dominant. Recall that
y^{\prime}=(\rho_{y},\underline{\delta^{\prime}})\in Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})
is called a companion point of
y
if
\underline{\delta}^{-1}\underline{\delta^{\prime}}
is a
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic character.

We state our main result on companion points and companion constituents under the assumption
L\neq{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. We need the following so-called “Talyor-Wiles hypothesis”.

Hypothesis 1.1
.

(1)

p>2
;

(2)

the field
F
is unramified over
F^{+}
,
F
does not contain a non-trivial root
\sqrt[p]{1}
of
1
and
G
is quasi-split at all finite places of
F^{+}
;

(3)

U_{v}
is hyperspecial when the finite place
v
of
F^{+}
is inert in
F
;

(4)

\overline{\rho}
is absolutely irreducible and
\overline{\rho}({\rm Gal}_{F(\sqrt[p]{1})})
is adequate.

We first have the following classicality result. let
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}\subset{\mathbf{T}}^{S_{p},{\rm univ}}_{\overline{\rho}}[1/p]
be the maximal ideal associated to
\rho
and
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm an}}_{\overline{\rho}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\rho}}]
be the subspace of
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm an}}_{\overline{\rho}}
annihilated by
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}
.

Theorem 1.2
.

(Classicality, See Theorem
5.3
) Assume Hypothesis
1.1
and Hypothesis
1.12
. If the Galois representation
\rho:{\rm Gal}(\overline{F}/F)\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(E)
comes from a Steinberg type point
y=(\rho,\underline{\delta})\in Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})
, then
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm lalg}}_{\overline{\rho}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\rho}}]\neq 0
, i.e.,
\rho
is associated to a classical automorphic representation of
{\mathbf{G}}_{U}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+}}^{\infty})
.

It is conjectured in
[
42
, Conjecture 1.2.5]
and
[
11
, Conjecure 6.5]
(note that Breuil only states such conjecture for potentially (generic) crystalline case) that the companion points of
y
are parametrized by
w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
such that
w^{\prime}\underline{w}_{0}\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
(the usual Bruhat order in
\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
). We write
y_{w^{\prime}}
for the conjectural companion point associated to
w^{\prime}
.

Theorem 1.3
.

(Theorem
5.18
) Assume Hypothesis
1.1
and Hypothesis
1.13
below (certain hypothesis on trianguline variety). If
\rho:{\rm Gal}(\overline{F}/F)\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(E)
comes from a Steinberg type strictly dominant point
y=(\rho,\underline{\delta})\in Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})
(and thus
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm lalg}}_{\overline{\rho}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\rho}}]\neq 0
by Theorem
1.2
), then all companion points of
y
are
y_{w^{\prime}}
for
w^{\prime}\underline{w}_{0}\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
.

The existence of companion points is a weaker version of the so-called locally analytic socle conjecture. Put
{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}:=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i}+i-1)_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L},1\leq i\leq n}
, which is a dominant weight of
{\mathbf{G}}_{L}
with respect to
{\mathbf{B}}_{L}
. For
a\in E
, denote by
{\rm unr}(a)
the unramified character of
L^{\times}
sending uniformizers to
a
. By the Orlik-Strauch construction
[
51
, Theorem]
, we consider the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations

{\mathbf{I}}_{w\underline{w}_{0}}:=\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}}(L)}\big(\overline{L}(-w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}),{\rm unr}(\beta)\big)
for any
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
and
\beta:=\alpha q_{L}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}
. Recall that
{\mathbf{I}}_{w\underline{w}_{0}}
admits a unique quotient
C(w\underline{w}_{0})
, which is a locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic irreducible admissible representation.

Theorem 1.4
.

(Theorem
5.15
, Theorem
5.18
, “a special case of locally analytic socle conjecture”) Assume Hypothesis
1.1
and Hypothesis
1.13
. Then
C(w\underline{w}_{0})
is a subrepresentation of
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm an}}_{\overline{\rho}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\rho}}]
if and only if
w\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
.

For
n=2
, Theorem
1.4
was firstly proved by Ding
[
28
]
with the condition
\lg(w\underline{w}_{0})=1
. For
n>2
, such results are previously not known (to the author’s knowledge). For
n=2
and
\lg(w\underline{w}_{0})>1
, we also give an alternative proof of Theorem
1.4
(for
{\rm GL}_{2}(L)
case, without Hypothesis
1.13
) in Appendix
6
, by combining the arguments in
[
28
]
with
[
26
]
, see Theorem
1.16
. The basic strategy is by computing directly the (partial de Rham) cohomology of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules (without using local models) and using some stratifications of trianguline variety and patched eigenvariety to compare different complete local rings.

On the other hand, if
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
,
[
60
, Theroem 5.6.5]
gives an explicit local model for Steinberg case (a variation of Breuil-Hellmann-Schraen’s local model), and show that
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
is normal and Cohen-Macaulay at point
x_{{\rm pdR}}
, by blowing up the schemes given by Grothendieck’s simultaneous resolution. The author use a calculation on the explicit basis of cohomology of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules of rank
1
(done by Colmez) to study the universal cocycle and the universal derivation. But the campanion points and socle conjecture are not discussed in this work. We will treat this problem in the forthcoming work under the results in
[
60
]
.

In the case of
n>2
and
L\neq{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
, we prove the main results by modifying the methods in
[
17
]
and
[
61
]
to our Steinberg case. The method in
[
17
]
was replacing the Hecke eigenvariety
Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})
by the patched eigenvariety
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
in
[
15
]
(constructed from the patching module
[
23
]
). Then the local geometry of the patched eigenvariety at generic crystalline points can be reflected by the corresponding local geometry of the so-called triaguline variety
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
, where
\overline{r}:=\overline{\rho}|_{{\rm Gal}_{F_{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}}=\overline{\rho}|_{{\rm Gal}_{L}}
. The triaguline variety parameterizes local trianguline Galois representations. The new ingredient is that Breuil-Hellmann-Schraen find local models of the trianguline variety at the
generic crystalline and regular points
, by using some varieties studied in geometry representation theory. In precise, the formal completion of triaguline variety at certain generic crystalline points can be reflected, up to formally smooth morphisms, from some algebraic varieties which are related to the Springer resolution. The
generic
assumption is essential for the proof of the formally smoothness. Furthermore, Zhixiang Wu explores the non-regular cases in
[
61
]
and
[
62
]
.

To understanding the local geometry of triaguline variety at our Steinberg type
point for
L\neq{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
case, we also need a variation of Breuil-Hellmann-Schraen’s local model (the main difference is that the parameters of its triangulation are
non-generic
, so the previous local model map (i.e., (
1.1
)) are not necessary formally smooth). We now explain our results explicitly. Indeed, the following discussion on local models are proved in more general situation, i.e., for certain potentially semistable non-crystalline Galois representation
\rho_{L}
such that
D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{L})
admits the so-called critical special
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration (see
[
12
]
and Section
2.2
for more precise statement, which can be viewed as a paraboline analogue of triangulation). For simplicity, we restrict to the trianguline case in introduction.

Let
\overline{r}:=\overline{{\rho}}|_{{\rm Gal}_{L}}:{\rm Gal}_{L}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(k_{E})
. We denote by
R_{\overline{r}}^{\square}
the maximal reduced and
p
-torsion free quotient of the universal
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lifting ring of
\overline{r}
. Let
\widehat{T}_{L}
be the rigid space over
E
parametrizing continuous characters of
{\mathbf{T}}(L)
. The triaguline variety
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
is a closed subspace of
\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times\widehat{T}_{L}
, where

\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}=({\rm Spf}\;R_{\overline{r}}^{\square})^{{\rm rig}}
. For
w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, we define

\delta_{w^{\prime}}:=({\rm unr}({\alpha})z^{w^{\prime}({\mathbf{h}}_{1})},\;\cdots\,\;{\rm unr}({\alpha q_{L}^{i-1}})z^{w^{\prime}({\mathbf{h}}_{i})},\;\cdots\,\;,{\rm unr}({\alpha q_{L}^{n-1}})z^{w^{\prime}({\mathbf{h}}_{n})}),\;w^{\prime}({\mathbf{h}}_{i})=({\mathbf{h}}_{\tau,w_{\tau}^{-1}(i)})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}

For
w^{\prime}=(w^{\prime}_{\tau})\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, we have
x_{w^{\prime}}=(\rho_{L},\delta_{w^{\prime}})\subseteq\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times\widehat{T}_{L}
. We write
x:=x_{1}
(the so-called strictly dominant point). The assumption on
y\in Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})
implies
x_{w_{y}}:=(\rho_{L},\delta_{w_{y}})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
. Let
\widehat{X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})}_{x_{w_{y}}}
be the completion of
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
at point
x_{w_{y}}
.

Let

\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}:=\{(g{\mathbf{B}},\psi)\in{\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{B}}\times{\mathfrak{g}}|\;\mathrm{Ad}(g^{-1})\psi\in{\mathfrak{b}}\}
, and
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{L}:=\{(g{\mathbf{B}}_{L},\psi)\in{\mathbf{G}}_{L}/{\mathbf{B}}_{L}\times{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}|\;\mathrm{Ad}(g^{-1})\psi\in{\mathfrak{b}}_{L}\}
. The projection
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{L}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}
are the so-called Grothendieck’s simultaneous resolution of singularities. Let
X_{L}:=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{L}\times_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}}\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{L}
(resp.,
X:=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}\times_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}
) be the scheme defined in
[
17
, (2.3)]
, which is equidimensional of dimension
d_{L}\dim G
(resp.,
\dim G
). The irreducible components of
X_{L}
are parameterized by
\{X_{L,w^{\prime}}\}_{w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}}
. We have decompositions
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{L}=\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{\tau}
and
X_{L}=\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}X_{\tau}
by
\Sigma_{L}
-components with
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{\tau}\cong\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}
and
X_{\tau}\cong X
.

By the assumption on
x_{w}
and the theory of almost de Rham representations (recall the period ring
B_{{\rm pdR}}
and the associated functor
D_{{\rm pdR}}(-)
), the finite free
L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-module
D_{{\rm pdR}}(\rho_{L})
of rank
n
is equipped with a nilpotent endomorphism
N
and two flags
\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}
(comes from the triangulation
\mathcal{F}
) and
{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{H}
(comes from the Hodge-filtration), so that we can define a point
x_{{\rm pdR}}:=(\mathcal{D}_{\bullet},{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{H},N)\in X_{L}(E)
by choosing some basis of
D_{{\rm pdR}}(\rho_{L})
. By using the theory of
[
17
, Section 3]
, there exists a natural morphism (the so-called local model map)

\Upsilon:\widehat{X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})}_{x_{w_{y}}}\rightarrow\widehat{X}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}\times_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}}}\widehat{T}_{L,\delta_{w_{y}}}:=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}\cong\widehat{X}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}\times\widehat{{\mathfrak{t}}},

(1.1)

where
\widehat{X}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
is the completion of
X_{L}
at point
x_{{\rm pdR}}
and
\widehat{{\mathfrak{t}}}
is the completion of
{\mathfrak{t}}
at
0
.

Let
{\rm pr}_{1}:\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}\rightarrow\widehat{X}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
be the natural formally smooth projection. For generic crystalline case studied in
[
17
]
, the composition
{\rm pr}_{1}\circ\Upsilon
(the previous local model map in
[
17
, Theorem 3.4.4]
) is formally smooth
[
17
, Theorem 1.6]
. But in our case, it is not true that
{\rm pr}_{1}\circ\Upsilon
is formally smooth. In Section
3.3
, we show that:

Proposition 1.5
.

We construct a formal scheme
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
(a variation of
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
which pro-represents certain groupoid) such that:

(a)

there is a natural morphism
\iota:\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
;

(b)

the natural morphism
\Upsilon:\widehat{X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})}_{x_{w_{y}}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
factors through
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
;

(c)

\widehat{X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})}_{x_{w_{y}}}
is formally smooth over
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
.

We give the reader a comparison of
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
and the original formal completion
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
(equivalently,
\widehat{X}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
, up to formally smooth morphism). By definition, the point
x_{{\rm pdR}}\in\widehat{X}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
splits into the product of its
\Sigma_{L}
-component, i.e.
x_{{\rm pdR}}=(x_{{\rm pdR},\tau})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\in\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\widehat{X}_{\tau,x_{{\rm pdR},\tau}}
. For
J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
, put
x_{{\rm pdR},J}=(x_{{\rm pdR},\tau})_{\tau\in J}
and
\widehat{X}_{J,x_{{\rm pdR},J}}:=\prod_{\tau\in J}{\widehat{X}}_{\tau,x_{{\rm pdR},\tau}}
.

Proposition 1.6
.

(Proposition
3.10
) For any
J\subsetneqq\Sigma_{L}
, the composition
\iota_{J}:\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}\twoheadrightarrow\widehat{X}_{J,x_{{\rm pdR},J}}
) is formally smooth.

Remark 1.7
.

Our observation in Proposition
1.6
shows that the local model is not so complicate. In Section
3.3.3
, we want to describe
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}

through some formal power series, which mix variables coming from different
\Sigma_{L}
-components of
\widehat{X}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
and
\widehat{{\mathfrak{t}}}_{L}
-component of
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
). This is why our local models are established though
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
, which is different from the
previous generic crystalline case (the local models are established only though
\widehat{X}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
).

For any
w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
such that
x_{{\rm pdR}}\in X_{L,w^{\prime}}(E)
, we put
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,w^{\prime},x_{{\rm pdR}}}:=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}\times_{\widehat{X}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}}\widehat{X}_{L,w^{\prime},x_{{\rm pdR}}}
. We finally have:

Theorem 1.8
.

(Proposition
3.22
, Theorem
4.3
) Keep the above notation and situation.

(1)

We have
w_{y}\underline{w}_{0}\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
.

(2)

There exists a formal scheme
X^{\Box,w\underline{w}_{0}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
over
E
such that
\big(X^{\Box,w\underline{w}_{0}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\big)^{\mathrm{red}}
(the associated reduced formal scheme) is formally smooth over
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,w\underline{w}_{0},x_{{\rm pdR}}}
and formally smooth of dimension
n^{2}d_{L}
over
\widehat{X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})}_{x_{w}}
:

\textstyle{\big(X^{\Box,w_{y}\underline{w}_{0}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\big)^{\mathrm{red}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})}_{x_{w_{y}}}}
\textstyle{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,w_{y}\underline{w}_{0},x_{{\rm pdR}}}.}

(1.2)

(c)

\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}
is unibranch at point
x_{{\rm pdR}}
, and
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
is irreducible and Cohen-Macaulay at point
x_{w_{y}}
.

Let
X_{\rho_{L}}
be the formal scheme associated to the universal deformation ring of
\rho_{L}
. We consider a subscheme
X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}:=\Spec R_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
of
X_{\rho_{L}}
. The irreducible components of
X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
are indexed by
X^{w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
for
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
such that
w\underline{w}_{0}\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
. Then
X^{\Box,w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
is a “framed” version of
X^{w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
.

We have two morphisms
\kappa_{1}:X_{L}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
(resp.,
\kappa_{2}:X_{L}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
) by sending

(g_{1}{\mathbf{B}},g_{2}{\mathbf{B}},\psi)\mapsto\overline{\mathrm{Ad}(g_{1}^{-1})\psi},\;\text{resp.,\;}(g_{1}{\mathbf{B}},g_{2}{\mathbf{B}},\psi)\mapsto\overline{\mathrm{Ad}(g_{2}^{-1})\psi}

where
\overline{\mathrm{Ad}(g_{i}^{-1})\psi}
is the image of
\mathrm{Ad}(g_{i}^{-1})\psi\in{\mathfrak{b}}_{L}
via
{\mathfrak{b}}_{L}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
. Let
\kappa_{w,i}
be the restriction of
\kappa_{i}
on
X_{w}
. They induce natural morphisms
\kappa_{i}:\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
(resp.,
\kappa_{w,i}:\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,w,\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
) on our local models, where we view
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,\widehat{y}}
,
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,w,\widehat{y}}
and
\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
as schemes. We suspect that

Conjecture 1.9
.

(Conjecture
3.27
)
The morphism
\kappa_{i}:\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
(resp.,
\kappa_{w,i}:\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,w,\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
) is flat.

In Proposition
3.28
, we use miracle flatness to shows that:

Proposition 1.10
.

The natural morphism
\kappa_{\underline{w}_{0},i}:\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,\underline{w}_{0},\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
is flat.

Moreover, the projection
{\rm pr}_{1}:\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}\cong\widehat{{\mathfrak{t}}}\times\widehat{X}_{L,x_{{\rm pdR}}}\rightarrow\widehat{{\mathfrak{t}}}
gives projections
\kappa:X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}
and
\kappa:\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}
, they are not flat. Let
\Spec\overline{R}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
(resp.,
\Spec\overline{\overline{R}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
) be the fiber of
\Spec{R}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
(resp.,
\kappa_{i}\times\kappa:\Spec{R}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}\times\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}
) over
0
(resp.,
(0,0)
). In Proposition
4.15
, we give the local model of
\Spec\overline{\overline{R}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
, which has a simple description, this ring will be used in the formulation of locally analytic “Breuil-Mezard type” conjecture.

Remark 1.11
.

The above discussion on local model is proved in more general situation, i.e., for certain potentially semistable non-crystalline Galois representation
\rho_{L}
such that
D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{L})
admits the so-called critical special
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration.

We now explain the proof of the existences of (local and global) companion points and companion constituents on eigenvariety. We first describe the local companion points of
x:=x_{1}\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
. We need a hypothesis on local companion points of
x
.

Hypothesis 1.12
.

The point
x_{w_{\mathcal{F}}w_{0}}
lies in
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
.

Denoted by
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}
the closed analytic subspace associated to (framed) semistable deformations of
\overline{r}
with Hodge-Tate weights
{\mathbf{h}}
, and let
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
be the locally closed subspace of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
consisting potentially semistable deformations with full monodromy rank (see Section
2.3
for this notation). For
\rho^{\prime}_{L}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
, there exists a unique
a_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}}\in k(\rho_{L})
such that
{a_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}}},\cdots,{a_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}}q_{L}^{i-1}},\cdots,{a_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}}q_{L}^{n-1}}
are
\varphi^{f_{L}}
-eigenvalues of the
(\varphi,N)
-module
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho^{\prime}_{L})
. The unique
(\varphi,N)
-stable complete flag on
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho^{\prime}_{L})
determine a element
w_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
measuring the relation position of this complete flag and Hodge filtration. Put
\delta_{\rho^{\prime}_{L},w}=({\rm unr}(a_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}})z^{w({\mathbf{h}}_{1})},\cdots,{\rm unr}({a_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}}q_{L}^{i-1}})z^{w({\mathbf{h}}_{i})},\cdots,{\rm unr}({a^{\prime}_{\rho_{L}}q_{L}^{n-1}})z^{w({\mathbf{h}}_{n})})
. We make the following stronger Hypothesis (which is also predicted by
[
37
, Conjecture 5.3.13]
). Hypothesis
1.12
is contained in Hypothesis
1.13
.

Hypothesis 1.13
.

(Hypothesis
4.5
) For any
\rho^{\prime}_{L}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
,
(\rho^{\prime}_{L},\delta_{\rho^{\prime}_{L},w_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}}w_{0}})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
.

Remark 1.14
.

The above hypothesis are natural. In generic crystalline case (see
[
17
]
), the generic crystalline point
(\rho^{\prime},\delta)
such that
\delta
gives the right parameters of triangulation on
D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho^{\prime})
lies in
U_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})\subseteq X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
automatically. But in semistable case, the author does not know whether it is true in general. It is also predicted by
[
37
, Conjecture 5.3.13]
. See Remark
4.6
for more precise statements. The Hypothesis
1.13
are true by replacing
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
with a larger space
X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
, see
[
60
]
.

Under Hypothesis
1.13
, we prove the following result on the local companion points of
x
and
x_{w_{\mathcal{F}}w_{0}}
. Recall that the local companion points of
x
are those
x^{\prime}=(\rho_{L},{\delta^{\prime}})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
such that
{\delta^{\prime}}{\delta}^{-1}
is a
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic character.

Theorem 1.15
.

(Proposition
4.9
) Assume Hypothesis
1.13
, then
x_{w}\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
if and only if
w\underline{w}_{0}\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
.

This theorem is proved by some Zariski-closure argument on semistable deformation rings and a study of the relation between semistable deformation spaces and trianguline variety (they are considered firstly for the crystalline case in
[
15
, Section 2.2]
, see Proposition
4.7
and Proposition
2.4
for more detail). They are also used in the proof of Theorem
1.17
.

In
{\rm GL}_{2}(L)
case, the main theorem in Appendix
6
(i.e., if
\rho_{L}
comes from some global setup) also implies the following result (without Hypothesis
1.12
and Hypothesis
1.13
).

Theorem 1.16
.

(Restrict the above situation and notation to
{\rm GL}_{2}(L)
case) If
y=(\rho,\underline{\delta})\in Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})
is strictly dominant and of Steinberg type. Suppose
\rho_{y,\widetilde{v}}
is generic for
v\in\Sigma(U^{p})\backslash S_{p}
, where
\Sigma(U^{p})
consists of some “bad” places. Then all companion points of
y
are
y_{w^{\prime}}
for
w^{\prime}\underline{w}_{0}\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
. In particular,
x_{w}\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
if and only if
w\underline{w}_{0}\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
. Moreover,
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
is smooth at each point
x_{w}
.

We now move to global setup. Under the “Talyor-Wiles hypothesis”, we get a continuous Banach representation
\Pi_{\infty}
of
G
, which is equipped with a continuous action of certain patched Galois deformation ring
R_{\infty}
commuting with the
G
-action. See Section
5.1.1
for a brief summary. The proofs of Theorem
1.3
and Theorem
1.4
are related to the existence of companion constituents
{\mathbf{I}}_{w\underline{w}_{0}}
. We prove the following result.

Theorem 1.17
.

(Proposition
5.15
and Theorem
5.18
)
Assume Hypothesis
1.1
and Hypothesis
1.13
. Then
{\rm Hom}_{G}\Big({\mathbf{I}}_{w\underline{w}_{0}},\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm an}}_{\overline{\rho}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{r_{y}}]\Big)\neq 0
if and only if
w\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
. In particular,
y_{w}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
if and only if
w\underline{w}_{0}\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
.

Now Theorem
1.4
is a direct consequence of Theorem
1.17
. Indeed, for any irreducible component
W\ncong C(w\underline{w}_{0})
of
{\mathbf{I}}_{w\underline{w}_{0}}
, we have
{\rm Hom}_{G}\Big(W,\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm an}}_{\overline{\rho}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{r_{y}}]\Big)=0
. We sketch the proof of Theorem
1.17
by using the coherent sheaves obtained from patching functors (resp., constructed from local models).

The first cycles are obtained by patching functor. Firstly, we use
[
17
, Section 5]
and
[
44
, Section 6.1]
to construct sheaves
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(M)
over
{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}=\Spec R_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}[[x_{1},\cdots,x_{g}]]
for some integer
g
given in the patching argument, for (parabolic) verma module (and its dual)
M=L(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}),M_{I}(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}})^{\ast}
and the
J
-deformed Verma modules
\widetilde{M}_{I}^{J}(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}})^{\ast}
(and its subquotient) for
J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
and
\ast\{\emptyset,\vee\}
(discussed in
[
44
, Section 2.3]
and Section
5.3.1
). For the companion constituent
{\mathbf{I}}_{w\underline{w}_{0}}
, we have the cycle
[\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(L(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))]\neq 0
if and only if
{\rm Hom}_{G}\Big({\mathbf{I}}_{w\underline{w}_{0}},\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{\infty}]\Big)\neq 0
(equivalently,
{\rm Hom}_{G}\Big({\mathbf{I}}_{w\underline{w}_{0}},\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm an}}_{\overline{\rho}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{r_{y}}]\Big)\neq 0
),
[\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(M(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))]\neq 0\Leftrightarrow[\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(\widetilde{M}_{I}^{J}(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))]\neq 0
if and only if
y_{w}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
.

The second cycles are obtained from local models. Our new ingredients are combining two types partially de-Rham Galois cycles with respect to the parabolic group (see
[
61
, Section 3.6]
) (resp., the
\Sigma_{L}
-components (see
[
26
, Section 5.2]
), see Section
4.3
for more details. For
J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
, we let
Z^{J}_{L}
be the fiber of
X_{L}
at
0
via the natural projection
X_{L}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{t}}_{J}
(
{\mathfrak{t}}_{J}
is the
J
-component of
{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
). One can show that
Z_{L}^{J}
is equidimensional with reduced irreducible components given by
\{Z_{w^{\prime}}:=X_{L,w^{\prime}}\cap Z^{J}_{L}\}_{w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}}
. We have
x_{{\rm pdR}}\in Z^{J}_{L}
. Put
\widehat{Z}^{J,\flat}_{w^{\prime},x_{{\rm pdR}}}:=\widehat{Z}^{J}_{w^{\prime},x_{{\rm pdR}}}\times_{\widehat{X}_{L,w^{\prime},x_{{\rm pdR}}}}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,w^{\prime},x_{{\rm pdR}}}
if
x_{{\rm pdR}}\in Z_{w^{\prime}}
. Pulling back each
\widehat{Z}^{J,\flat}_{w^{\prime},x_{{\rm pdR}}}
via the natural morphism
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})\rightarrow X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
(see (
5.3
)) defines a cycle
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{J,\flat}_{w^{\prime}}
on
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
. If
J\neq\Sigma_{L}
,
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{J,\flat}_{w^{\prime}}
is irreducible by Proposition
1.6
. If
J=\Sigma_{L}
, we rewrite
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{\flat}_{w^{\prime}}:={\mathfrak{Z}}^{J,\flat}_{w^{\prime}}
, which is equi-dimensional (not necessary irreducible in general) if Conjecture
1.9
holds.

We use the strategy in the proof of
[
61
, Proposition 4.7, Proposition 4.9, Theorem 4.10, Theorem 4.12]
to compare the cycles
[\mathcal{L}(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}})]
with cycles
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{\flat}_{w^{\prime}}
and then prove the main results. The new ingredients in his proof (compare with
[
17
, Theorem 5.3.3]
) are results relating the partially de Rham properties of Galois representations (the de Rhamness of graded pieces along the paraboline filtrations of the associated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
) and the relevant properties of cycles on the generalized Steinberg varieties.

By descending induction and similar discussion (more precisely, some Zariski-closure argument on semistable deformation rings) in the proof of Theorem
1.15
(an easy modification of
[
61
, Proposition 4.9, Theorem 4.10]
or the Step
8
and Step
9
in the proof of
[
17
, Theorem 5.3.3]
), one is reduced to showing the following statement (see Proposition
5.14
), which is the key step in the proof of Theorem
1.17
.

•

if
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
for all
w>w_{\mathcal{F}}
, then
y_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
.

We sketch the proof roughly by using our partial cycle
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{J,\flat}_{w^{\prime}}
and the strategy in the proof of
[
61
, Proposition 4.7]
. We have
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{\flat}_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\neq 0
(equivalently,
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{J,\flat}_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\neq 0
) for all
w>w_{\mathcal{F}}
. There exists a simple reflection
s_{\alpha,\tau}
of
{\mathbf{G}}_{L}
and a parabolic subgroup
{\mathbf{B}}_{\tau}\subset{\mathbf{P}}_{\tau}
of
{\mathbf{G}}
such that
s_{\alpha,\tau}w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}})
is strictly
{\mathbf{P}}_{\tau}
-dominant and
w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}})
is
not
strictly
{\mathbf{P}}_{\tau}
-dominant. The assumption implies that
y_{s_{\alpha,\tau}w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
. Put

\displaystyle[\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(s_{\alpha,\tau}w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0},\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau)]:=\Big[\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}(w_{\mathcal{F},\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}\underline{w}_{0,\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}})\otimes_{E}L(s_{\alpha,\tau}w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))\Big]

(1.3)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0},\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau)]:=\Big[\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}(w_{\mathcal{F},\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}\underline{w}_{0,\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}})\otimes_{E}L(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))\Big].

Then we have an equality of the underlying closed subspaces of cycles:

\displaystyle\cup[\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0},\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau)]={\mathfrak{Z}}^{\{\tau\},\flat}_{s_{\alpha,\tau}w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}\cup{\mathfrak{Z}}^{\{\tau\},\flat}_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}.

(1.4)

Then we show that
[\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(s_{\alpha,\tau}w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0},\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau)]
is
{\mathbf{P}}_{\tau}
-partially de Rham (in the terminology of
[
61
, Section 3.6]
), while the cycle
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau\flat}_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}
is not fully contained in the
{\mathbf{P}}_{\tau}
-partially de Rham locus. Hence
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau,\flat}_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}\not\subseteq[\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(s_{\alpha,\tau}w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0},\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau)]
and then
[\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0},\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau)]\neq 0
. This shows that
y_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
.

We end the introduction with a discussion on coherent sheaves
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(M)
over
{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}
(we follow the route of the recently work of Hellmann-Hernandez-Schraen
[
44
, Section 7.2]
). As in
[
44
, Section 7.2]
, we also consider the “local” functor
\mathcal{B}_{y}

associates, to an object
M
in BGG category
\mathcal{O}_{\chi_{\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}}
, a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{B}_{y}(M)
on
{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}
. The functor
\mathcal{B}_{y}
is induced by the Bezrukavnikov’s functor. The functor
\mathcal{B}_{y}
is not exact in our case, but the derived terms can be described through
\iota^{\flat}:\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\widehat{X}_{L,\widehat{y}}
in diagram (
5.11
). We discuss the schematic supports (together with the multiplicities) of
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash J}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
and
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash J}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee})
when
J\subsetneq\Sigma_{L}
in Proposition
5.6
-Proposition
5.9
and Proposition
5.19
. The coherent sheaf
\mathcal{B}_{y}(M)
should be viewed as the local analogue of
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(M)
(predicted by the categorical
p
-adic Langlands correspondence). The relationship between
\mathcal{B}_{y}(M)
and
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(M)
are given in Section
5.4.1
and Proposition
5.19
, see Remark
5.12
and Remark
5.13
for general argument, under the picture of categorical
p
-adic Langlands program. In the future, we want to give an explicit computation on
{\rm GL}_{3}
-case, which may give more evidence for the categorical
p
-adic Langlands program.

2
Preliminaries

2.1
General notation

Let
L
(resp.
E
) be a finite extension of
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
with
\mathcal{O}_{L}
(resp.
\mathcal{O}_{E}
) as its ring of integers and
\varpi_{L}
(resp.
\varpi_{E}
) a uniformizer. Suppose
E
is sufficiently large containing all the embeddings of
L
in
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
. Put

\Sigma_{L}:=\{\sigma:L\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\}=\{\sigma:L\hookrightarrow E\}.

Let
\val_{L}(\cdot)
(resp.
\val_{p}
) be the
p
-adic valuation on
\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}
normalized by sending uniformizers of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
(resp.,
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
) to
1
. Let
d_{L}:=[L:{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}]=|\Sigma_{L}|
and
q_{L}:=p^{f_{L}}=|\mathcal{O}_{L}/\varpi_{L}|
, where
f_{L}
denotes the unramified degree of
L
over
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. For a group
A
and
a\in A
, denote by
{\rm unr}(\alpha)
the unramified character of
L^{\times}
sending uniformizers to
\alpha
. If
{\mathbf{k}}:=({\mathbf{k}}_{\tau})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{L}}
, we denote
z^{{\mathbf{k}}}:=\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\tau(z)^{{\mathbf{k}}_{\tau}}
. For a character of
\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}
, denoted by
\chi_{\varpi_{L}}
the character of
L^{\times}
such that
\chi_{\varpi_{L}}|_{\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}}=\chi
and
\chi_{\varpi_{L}}(\varpi_{L})=1
.

Let
\mathcal{R}_{L}:={\mathbf{B}}_{{\rm rig},L}^{\dagger}
be the Robba ring. Let
A
(resp.,
X
) be an
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-affinoid algebra (resp. a rigid analytic space), and let
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}:=\mathcal{R}_{L}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A
(resp.,
\mathcal{R}_{X,L}
) for the Robba ring associated to
L
with
A
-coefficient (resp., with
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-coefficient). We write
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}(\delta_{A})
for the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of character type over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
associated to a continuous character
\delta_{A}:L^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
. If
D
is a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
, we denote
D(\delta_{A}):=D\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}(\delta_{A})
for simplicity.

Let
X
be a scheme locally of finite type over
E
, or a locally noetherian formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
whose reduction is locally of finite type over
k_{E}
. Let
X^{\mathrm{rig}}
the associated rigid analytic space over
E
. If
x\in X
, denote by
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
(resp.,
k(x)
) the local ring (resp., residue field) at
x
. Let
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
be the
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X,x}}
-adic completion of
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
, and
\widehat{X}_{x}:=\mathrm{Spf}\;\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
. If
x
is a closed point of
X
, then
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
is a noetherian complete local
k(x)
-algebra of residue field
k(x)
.

Let
\GL_{n}
be the general linear group over
L
. Let
\Delta_{n}
be the set of simple roots of
\GL_{n}
(with respect to the Borel subgroup
{\mathbf{B}}
of upper triangular matrices), and we identify the set
\Delta_{n}
with
\{1,\cdots,n-1\}
such that
i\in\{1,\cdots,n-1\}
corresponds to the simple root
\alpha_{i}:\;(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in{\mathfrak{t}}\mapsto x_{i}-x_{i+1}
, where
{\mathfrak{t}}
denotes the
L
-Lie algebra of the torus
{\mathbf{T}}
of diagonal matrices. Let
\mathbf{P}_{I}
be the parabolic subgroup of

\GL_{n}
containing
{\mathbf{B}}
such that
\Delta_{n}\backslash I
are precisely the simple roots of the unipotent radical
\mathbf{N}_{I}
of
\mathbf{P}_{I}
. Denote by
\mathbf{L}_{I}
the unique Levi subgroup of
\mathbf{P}_{I}
containing
{\mathbf{T}}
, and
I
is equal to the set of simple roots of
\mathbf{L}_{I}
. In particular, we have
\mathbf{P}_{\Delta_{n}}=\GL_{n}
,
\mathbf{P}_{\emptyset}=\mathbf{B}
. Let
\overline{\mathbf{P}}_{I}
be the parabolic subgroup opposite to
\mathbf{P}_{I}
. Let
\mathbf{N}_{I}
(resp.
\overline{\mathbf{N}}_{I}
) be the nilpotent radical of
\mathbf{P}_{I}
(resp.
\overline{\mathbf{P}}_{I}
). We have Levi decompositions
\mathbf{P}_{I}=\mathbf{L}_{I}\mathbf{N}_{I}
(resp.
\overline{\mathbf{P}}_{I}=\mathbf{L}_{I}\overline{\mathbf{N}}_{I}
). Let
{\mathbf{Z}}_{n}
(resp.,
{\mathbf{Z}}_{I}
) be the center of
\GL_{n}
(resp.,
\mathbf{L}_{I}
). Let
{\mathfrak{g}}
,
{\mathfrak{p}}_{I}
,
{\mathfrak{l}}_{I}
,
{\mathfrak{n}}_{I}
,
{\mathfrak{z}}_{I}
and
{\mathfrak{t}}
be the
L
-Lie algebras of
\GL_{n}
,
\mathbf{P}_{I}
,
\mathbf{L}_{I}
,
{\mathbf{N}}_{I}
,
{\mathbf{Z}}_{I}
and
{\mathbf{T}}
respectively. Put
{\mathbf{U}}:=\mathbf{N}_{\emptyset}
(resp.
\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}:=\overline{\mathbf{N}}_{\emptyset}
) and
{\mathfrak{u}}:={\mathfrak{n}}_{\emptyset}
. We put
G:=\GL_{n}(L)
.

For a Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
over
L
, and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, let
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma}:={\mathfrak{g}}\otimes_{L,\sigma}E
(which is a Lie algebra over
E
). For
J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
, let
{\mathfrak{g}}_{r,J}:=\prod_{\sigma\in J}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma}
. In particular, we have
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}}\cong{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
. For any algebraic group
\mathbf{H}
over
L
, let
\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}\mathbf{H}
be the scalar restriction of
\mathbf{H}
from
L
to
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. We write
\mathbf{H}_{L}=(\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}\mathbf{H})\times_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\mathbf{H}}_{\tau}
that is isomorphic to
{\mathbf{H}}^{d_{L}}
as a algebraic group over
E
.

Let
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, and
\pi
be an irreducible smooth admissible representation of
{\rm GL}_{m}(L)
, let
{\rm rec}_{L}(\pi)
be the
m
-dimensional absolutely irreducible
F
-semi-simple Weil-Deligne representation of the Weil group
W_{L}
via the normalized classical local Langlands correspondence (normalized in
[
59
]
). We normalize the reciprocity isomorphism
{\rm rec}_{L}:L^{\times}\rightarrow W_{L}^{\mathrm{ab}}
of local class theory such that the uniformizer
\varpi_{L}
is mapped to a geometric Frobenius morphism, where
W_{L}^{\mathrm{ab}}
is the abelization of the Weil group
W_{L}\subset{\rm Gal}_{L}
.

Let
{{\epsilon}}:{\rm Gal}_{L}\rightarrow{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
be the
p
-adic cyclotomic character (i.e., the character defined by the formula
g(\epsilon_{n})=\epsilon_{n}^{{{\epsilon}}(g)}
for any
n\geq 1
and
g\in{\rm Gal}_{L}
). Then we have
{{\epsilon}}\circ{\rm rec}_{L}={\rm unr}(q_{L}^{-1})\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\tau:L^{\times}\rightarrow E^{\times}
by local class theory. We define the Hodge-Tate weights of a de Rham representation as the opposite of the gaps of the filtration on the covariant de Rham functor, so that the Hodge-Tate weights of
{{\epsilon}}
is
1
. A character
\delta:L^{\times}\rightarrow E^{\times}
is called
special
if
\delta:={\rm unr}(q_{L}^{-1})z^{{\mathbf{k}}}={\epsilon}z^{{\mathbf{k}}-1}
for some
{\mathbf{k}}:=({\mathbf{k}}_{\tau})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{L}}
and
{\mathbf{k}}-1:=({\mathbf{k}}_{\tau}-1)_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{L}}
, where
z^{{\mathbf{k}}}
is the character
z\mapsto\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\tau(z)^{{\mathbf{k}}_{\tau}}\in E
.

Let
A
be an affinoid
E
-algebra. A locally
{\mathbf{Q}}
-analytic character
\delta:L^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
induces a
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-linear map
L\rightarrow A
,
x\mapsto\frac{d}{dt}\delta(\exp(tx))|_{t=0}
and hence it induces an
E
-linear map
L\otimes E=\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}E\rightarrow A
. There exist
{\rm wt}(\delta):=({\rm wt}_{\tau}(\delta))_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
such that the latter map is given by
(a_{\tau})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\mapsto\sum_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}a_{\tau}{\rm wt}_{\tau}(\delta)
. We call
{\rm wt}(\delta)
the weight of
\delta
.

Let
\underline{\lambda}:=(\lambda_{1,\sigma},\cdots,\lambda_{n,\sigma})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}
be a weight of
{\mathfrak{t}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
. For
\underline{I}=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}I_{\tau}\subseteq\Delta_{n}^{\Sigma_{L}}
, let
{\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{I}}:=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\mathbf{P}}_{{I}_{\tau}}
be the parabolic subgroup of
{\rm GL}_{n,L}
associated to the
\underline{I}
. We call that
\underline{\lambda}
is
\underline{I}
-dominant (resp., strictly
\underline{I}
-dominant) if
\lambda_{i,\sigma}\geq\lambda_{i+1,\sigma}
(resp.,
\lambda_{i,\sigma}>\lambda_{i+1,\sigma}
) for all
i\in I_{\tau}
and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
. In particular, if
\underline{I}_{L}:=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}I
for the same
I\subseteq\Delta_{n}
, we denote by
X_{I}^{+}
the set of
\underline{I}_{L}
-dominant integral weights of
{\mathfrak{t}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
. For
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{I}^{+}
, there exists a unique irreducible algebraic representation, denoted by
L(\underline{\lambda})_{I}
, of
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{L}
with highest weight
\underline{\lambda}
with respect to
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{L}\cap{\mathbf{B}}_{L}
, so that
\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda})_{I}:=(L(\underline{\lambda})_{I})^{\vee}
is the irreducible algebraic representation of
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{L}
with highest weight
-\underline{\lambda}
with respect to
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{L}\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{L}
. Denote
\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}:=L(\underline{\lambda})_{\emptyset}
. If
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}}^{+}
, let
L(\underline{\lambda}):=L(\underline{\lambda})_{\Delta_{n}}
. A
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representation of
G
over
E
is the induced action of
G\subset\GL_{n,L}(E)
on an algebraic representation of
\GL_{n,L}
. By abuse of notation we will use the same notation to denote
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representations induced from an algebraic representation of
\GL_{n,L}
. Let
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{I}^{+}
be an integral weight, denote by
M_{I}(\underline{\lambda}):=\text{U}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{\text{U}({\mathfrak{p}}_{I,L})}L(\underline{\lambda})_{I}
(resp.
\overline{M}_{I}(\underline{\lambda}):=\text{U}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{\text{U}(\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,L})}L(\underline{\lambda})_{I}
), the corresponding Verma module with respect to
{\mathfrak{p}}_{I,L}
(resp.
\overline{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
). Let
L(\underline{\lambda})
(resp.
\overline{L}(\underline{\lambda})
) be the unique simple quotient of
M(\underline{\lambda}):=M_{\emptyset}(\underline{\lambda})
(resp. of
\overline{M}_{\emptyset}(\underline{\lambda})
).

Denote by
\mathscr{W}_{n}
(
\cong S_{n}
) the Weyl group of
\GL_{n}
, and denote by
s_{i}
the simple reflection corresponding to
i\in\Delta_{n}
. For any
I\subset\Delta_{n}
, define
\mathscr{W}_{I}
to be the subgroup of
\mathscr{W}_{n}
generated by simple reflections
s_{i}
with
i\in I
(so that
\mathscr{W}_{I}
is the Weyl group of
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}
). For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
, we identity
w
with the permutation matrix corresponding to it. Let
I,J
be subsets of
\Delta_{n}
, recall that
\mathscr{W}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}_{J}
has a canonical set of representatives, which we will denote by
\mathscr{W}^{I,J}_{n}
(resp.,
\mathscr{W}^{I,J}_{n,\max}
), given by taking in each double coset the elements of minimal (resp., maximal) length. The Weyl group of
\GL_{n,L}
is
\mathscr{W}_{n,L}:=\Pi_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\mathscr{W}_{n,\sigma}\cong S_{n}^{d_{L}}
, where
\mathscr{W}_{n,\sigma}\cong\mathscr{W}_{n}
be the
\sigma
-th factor of
\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
. Let
I,J
be subsets of
\Delta_{n}
, and let
\mathscr{W}^{I,J}_{n}
(resp.,
\mathscr{W}^{I,J}_{n,\max}
) be the set of minimal (resp., maximal) length representatives in
\mathscr{W}_{n}
in
\mathscr{W}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}_{J}
. For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, denoted by
w^{\min}\in\mathscr{W}^{I,J}_{n}
(resp,
w^{\max}\in\mathscr{W}^{I,J}_{n,\max}
) the corresponding representative of
\mathscr{W}_{n}
. More general, for subset
S\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
and
\underline{I}=\prod_{\sigma\in S}I_{\tau}\subseteq\Delta_{n}^{S}
and
\underline{J}=\prod_{\sigma\in S}J_{\tau}\subseteq\Delta_{n}^{S}
, we put
\mathscr{W}_{\underline{I},S}:=\prod_{\sigma\in S}\mathscr{W}_{I_{\sigma}}
,
\mathscr{W}^{\underline{I},\underline{J}}_{n,S}:=\prod_{\sigma\in S}\mathscr{W}^{I_{\sigma},J_{\sigma}}_{n}
and
\mathscr{W}^{\underline{I},\underline{J}}_{n,S,\max}:=\prod_{\sigma\in S}\mathscr{W}^{I_{\sigma},J_{\sigma}}_{n,\max}
. In particular, if
\underline{I}=\underline{I}_{S}
and
\underline{J}=\underline{J}_{S}
for
I,J\subseteq\Delta_{n}
, we write
\mathscr{W}_{{I},S}:=\mathscr{W}_{\underline{I}_{S},S}

\mathscr{W}^{{I},{J}}_{n,S}:=\mathscr{W}^{\underline{I}_{S},\underline{J}_{S}}_{n,S}
and
\mathscr{W}^{{I},{J}}_{n,S,\max}:=\mathscr{W}^{\underline{I}_{S},\underline{J}_{S}}_{n,S,\max}
for simplicity. If
S=\Sigma_{L}
, we replace the subscript
S
by
L
. Let
w_{0}
(resp.,
\underline{w}_{0}=(w_{0})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
) be the longest elements in
\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
(resp.,
\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
). Let
w_{I,0}
(resp.,
\underline{w}_{\underline{I},0}=(w_{I_{\sigma},0})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}
) be the longest elements in
\mathscr{W}_{I}
(resp.,
\mathscr{W}_{\underline{I},L}
).

If
V
is a continuous representation of
G
over
E
, we denote by
V^{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}
its locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic vectors. If
V
is
locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations of
G
, we denote by
V^{\mathrm{sm}}
(resp.
V^{\mathrm{lalg}}
) the smooth (resp, locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic) subrepresentation of
V
consists of its smooth (locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic) vectors (see
[
57
]
and
[
34
]
for details). Let
\pi_{P}
be a continuous representation of
P
over
E
(resp., locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations of
P
on a locally convex
E
-vector space of compact type, resp., smooth representations of
P
over
E
), we denote by

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Ind}_{P}^{G}\pi_{P})^{\mathcal{C}^{0}}:=\{f:G\rightarrow\pi_{P}\text{\;continuous},\;f(pg)=pf(g)\},

(2.1)

\displaystyle\text{resp.,\;}(\mathrm{Ind}_{P}^{G}\pi_{P})^{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}:=\{f:G\rightarrow\pi_{P}\text{\;locally ${\mathbf{Q}}_{p}$-analytic representations},\;f(pg)=pf(g)\},

\displaystyle\text{resp.,\;}i^{G}_{P}\pi_{P}:=(\mathrm{Ind}_{P}^{G}\pi_{P})^{\infty}=\{f:G\rightarrow\pi_{P}\text{\;smooth},\;f(pg)=pf(g)\}

the continuous parabolic induction (resp., the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic induction, resp., the un-normalized smooth parabolic induction) of
G
. It becomes a continuous representation (resp., locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation) of
G
over
E
(resp., on a locally convex
E
-vector space of compact type, resp., smooth representations of
G
over
E
) by endowing the left action of
G
by right translation on functions:
(gf)(g^{\prime})=f(g^{\prime}g)
.

2.2
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
with special
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration

Let
k,r
be two integers such that
n=kr
. We put
{\Delta_{n}(k)}=\{r,2r,\cdots,(k-1)r\}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
and
\Delta_{n}^{k}=\Delta_{n}\backslash{\Delta_{n}(k)}
. For a subset
I\subset{\Delta_{n}(k)}
, we put
{\mathbf{L}}_{r,I}:={\mathbf{L}}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}
,
{\mathbf{P}}_{r,I}:={\mathbf{P}}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I},\;{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{r,I}:={\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}
,
{\mathbf{N}}_{r,I}:={\mathbf{N}}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I},\;{\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}_{r,I}:={\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}
and
{\mathbf{Z}}_{r,I}:={\mathbf{Z}}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}
. For example, we have

{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}{\rm GL}_{r}&0&\cdots&0\\
0&{\rm GL}_{r}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&0\\
0&0&0&{\rm GL}_{r}\\
\end{array}\right)\subseteq{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{r,\emptyset}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}{\rm GL}_{r}&0&\cdots&0\\
\ast&{\rm GL}_{r}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&0\\
\ast&\ast&\cdots&{\rm GL}_{r}\\
\end{array}\right)

The parabolic subgroups of
\GL_{n}
containing the parabolic subgroup
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{r,\emptyset}
are given by
\{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{r,I}\}_{I\subseteq\Delta_{n}(k)}
. Let
{\mathfrak{l}}_{r,I}
,
{\mathfrak{p}}_{r,I}
,
{\mathfrak{n}}_{r,I}
,
\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{r,I}

\overline{{\mathfrak{n}}}_{r,I}
,
{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,I}
be the
L
-Lie algebras of
{\mathbf{L}}_{r,I}
,
{\mathbf{P}}_{r,I}
,
{\mathbf{N}}_{r,I}
,
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{r,I}
,
{\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}_{r,I}
and
{\mathbf{Z}}_{r,I}
respectively. For
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}^{+}
, we put
L(\underline{\lambda})_{r,I}:=L(\underline{\lambda})_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I},\;\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda})_{r,I}:=\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda})_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}
. Similarly, for
I\subseteq\Delta_{n}(k)
, we have notation
\mathscr{W}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I,L}
,
\mathscr{W}^{\Delta_{n}^{k},\emptyset}_{n,L,\max}
, etc.

In the sequel, we fix a cuspidal Bernstein component
\Omega_{r}
of
{\rm GL}_{r}(L)
and an irreducible smooth cuspidal representation
\pi_{0}\in\Omega_{r}
over
E
of type
\Omega_{r}
. We put

\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}=\prod_{i=1}^{k}\Omega_{i},\;\Omega_{i}=\Omega_{r}

which is a cuspidal Bernstein component of
{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset}(L)
. Let
{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}
be the (rational) Bernstein centre of
\Omega_{r}
over
E
. Let
{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}}\cong\otimes_{i=1}^{k}{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{i}}={\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}
be the (rational)
Bernstein centre of
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
over
E
(see
[
23
, Section 3.13]
).

Let
\mathbf{WD}_{L^{\prime}/L,E}
be the category of representations
(r,N,V)
of
W_{L}
, on an
E
-vector space
V
of finite dimension such that
r
is unramified when restricted to the
W_{L^{\prime}}
. Let
\mathbf{DF}_{L^{\prime}/L,E}
be the category of Deligne-Fontaine modules, i.e., the category of quadruples
(\varphi,N,{\rm Gal}(L^{\prime}/L),D)
where
D
is an
L^{\prime}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-module free of finite rank, which is endowed with a Frobenius
\varphi:D\rightarrow D
(resp., an
L^{\prime}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-linear endomorphism
N:D\rightarrow D
) such that
N\varphi=p\varphi N
and an action of
{\rm Gal}(L^{\prime}/L)
commuting with
\varphi
and
N
such that
g((l\otimes e)d)=(g(l)\otimes e)d
for
g\in{\rm Gal}(L^{\prime}/L),\;l\in L^{\prime}_{0},\;e\in E,\;d\in D
. Then the Fontaine’s theory asserts that there is a functor
\mathrm{WD}:\mathbf{WD}_{L^{\prime}/L,E}\rightarrow\mathbf{DF}_{L^{\prime}/L,E}
gives an equivalence of categories (
[
20
, Proposition 4.1]
).

Let
\pi\in\Omega_{r}
be any irreducible smooth cuspidal representation over
E
of type
\Omega_{r}
. Then
\pi\cong\pi_{0}\otimes_{E}{\rm unr}(\alpha_{\pi})
for some
\alpha_{\pi}\in E^{\times}
. The irreducible cuspidal representation
\pi
corresponds an
E
-point
x_{\pi}
of
\Spec{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}
, a
r
-dimensional absolutely irreducible
F
-semi-simple Weil-Deligne representation
{\textbf{r}}_{\pi}:={\rm rec}_{L}(\pi)
of
W_{L}
over
E
via the normalized classical local Langlands correspondence (see
[
59
]
), a Deligne-Fontaine module
{\mathrm{DF}}_{\pi}
(by Fontaine’s equivalence of categories as in
[
20
, Proposition 4.1]
), and a
p
-adic differential equation
{\Delta_{\pi}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
(by Berger’s theory
[
6
, Theorem A]
, a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of rank
m
over
\mathcal{R}_{k(x),L}
which is de Rham of constant Hodge-Tate weights
0
such that
D_{\mathrm{pst}}(\Delta_{x})
is isomorphic to
{\mathrm{DF}}_{x}
by forgetting the Hodge filtration). Assume that
{\textbf{r}}_{\pi}
is unramified when restricted to
W_{L^{\prime}}
for some finite Galois extension

of

. Then the associated (absolutely) irreducible Deligne-Fontaine module
{\mathrm{DF}}_{\pi}=(\varphi_{\pi},N=0,\Gal(L^{\prime}/L),{\mathrm{DF}}_{\pi})\in\mathrm{DF}_{L^{\prime}/L,E}
, where

\varphi_{\pi}:{\mathrm{DF}}_{\pi}\rightarrow{\mathrm{DF}}_{\pi}
is the Frobenius semi-linear operator on
{\mathrm{DF}}_{\pi}
.

Keep the notation and terminology in
[
12
, Section 2.3, Section 4.1.2]
. We consider a special case of
\Omega
-filtration which associated to an (absolutely) indecomposable Weil-Deligne representation, that we call the special
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}
be a potentially semistable
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
of rank

. Let

be a finite Galois extension of

such that

is a semistable
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L^{\prime}}
of rank

. We consider the Deligne-Fontaine module associated to

{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}=(\varphi,N,\Gal(L^{\prime}/L),D_{{\rm pst}}({\mathbf{D}}))

where
D_{{\rm pst}}({\mathbf{D}})=D_{\mathrm{st}}^{L^{\prime}}({\mathbf{D}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L^{\prime}})
is a finite free
L^{\prime}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-module of rank

,

(over

, and where the

\Gal(L^{\prime}/L)
-action on

.

We say that
{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
admits an increasing

-filtration
\mathcal{F}
if

admits an increasing filtration

by Weil-Deligne subrepresentations:

\mathcal{F}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\ 0={\rm Fil}_{0}^{\mathcal{F}}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{k}^{\mathcal{F}}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}={\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}},

such that
{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{F}}_{i}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\cong{\textbf{r}}_{\pi}\otimes_{E}|{\rm rec}_{L}^{-1}|^{k-i}
for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant k
and some irreducible smooth cuspidal representation

over

. We further assume that the monodromy operator

to

via the identity map on

for

, and sends

By
[
20
, Proposition 4.1]
, the
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration

on

-filtration on the Deligne-Fontaine module

(still denoted by

) by Deligne-Fontaine submodules

\mathcal{F}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\ 0={\rm Fil}_{0}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{k}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}={\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}},

such that
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
is associated to
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
via

[
20
, Proposition 4.1]
. Then
{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{F}}_{i}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\cong(\varphi_{\pi,i},N_{{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{F}}_{i}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}}=0,\Gal(L^{\prime}/L),{\mathrm{DF}}_{\pi,i})
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant k
, where
{\mathrm{DF}}_{\pi,i}

{\mathrm{DF}}_{\pi}
as a module, endowed with a Frobenius morphism
\varphi_{\pi,i}=p^{i-k}\varphi_{\pi}
(i.e., a twist of
\varphi_{\pi}
by

). The monodromy operator

is zero on
(p^{1-k}\varphi_{\pi},N=0,\Gal(L^{\prime}/L),{\mathrm{DF}}_{\pi})
, and sending
(p^{i-k}\varphi_{\pi},N=0,\Gal(L^{\prime}/L),{\mathrm{DF}}_{\pi})

(p^{(i-1)-k}\varphi_{\pi},N=0,\Gal(L^{\prime}/L),{\mathrm{DF}}_{\pi})
via the identity map on
{\mathrm{DF}}_{\pi}
for
2\leq i\leq k
.

Let
{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}
be the
p
-adic differential equation over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
associated to
{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
. The
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration on

now induces an
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration
{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}=\{{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}\}
on
{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}
by saturated
