-submodules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
, such that
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}
is the
p
-adic differential equation over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
associated to
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
. In particular, we see that
{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{F}}_{i}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}\cong{\Delta_{\pi}}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\rm unr}(q_{L}^{i-k}))
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant k
. Consider

\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}={\mathbf{D}}\Big[\frac{1}{t}\Big]\cong\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}\Big[\frac{1}{t}\Big]

By inverting
t
, the filtration
\mathcal{F}
on
\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}
induces an increasing filtration
\mathcal{F}:={\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}:={\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}\big[\frac{1}{t}\big]
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}
by
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}\big[\frac{1}{t}\big]
. Therefore, the filtration
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}=D\big[\frac{1}{t}\big]
induces a filtration on
{\mathbf{D}}
:

\mathcal{F}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}:\ 0={\rm Fil}_{0}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{k}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}={\mathbf{D}},\;{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}=({\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}})\cap{\mathbf{D}},

by saturated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodules of
{\mathbf{D}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
.

Since
{\mathbf{D}}
is potentially semistable, it is de Rham. Hence we have
D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})\cong(D_{{\rm pst}}({\mathbf{D}})\otimes_{L_{0}^{\prime}}L^{\prime})^{\Gal(L^{\prime}/L)},\;
which is a free
L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-module of rank
n
. The
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
on
{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}

induces a
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
on
D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})
by free
L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-submodules
{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}}):=({\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\otimes_{L_{0}^{\prime}}L^{\prime})^{\Gal(L^{\prime}/L)}
. The module
D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})
is equipped with a natural Hodge filtration. We assume that
D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})
has distinct Hodge-Tate weights
{\mathbf{h}}:=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,1}>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,2}>\cdots>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,n})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
. Denote by
{{\mathbf{h}}}_{i}=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Hence, for each
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
, the natural Hodge filtration can be expressed by the following complete flag:

{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{H}D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})_{\tau}:\ 0\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,n}}^{H}D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})_{\tau}\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,n-1}}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})_{\tau}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,1}}}^{H}D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})_{\tau}=D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})_{\tau}.

For each
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
, we now fix a a basis of
D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})_{\tau}
over
E
. Then the Hodge filtration
{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{H}
(resp.,
\mathcal{F}
) corresponds to an
E
-point
(g_{2,\tau}{\mathbf{B}}(E))_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\in{\rm GL}_{n,L}/{\mathbf{B}}_{L}
(resp.,
(g_{1,\tau}{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}(E))_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\in{\rm GL}_{n,L}/{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset,L}
). For each
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
, there exists thus a unique
w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}\in\mathscr{W}^{\Delta_{n}^{k},\emptyset}_{n,L,\max}
such that

(g_{1,\tau}{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}(E),g_{2,\tau}{\mathbf{B}}(E))\in{\rm GL}_{n}(E)(1,w_{\mathcal{F},\tau})({\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}\times{\mathbf{B}})(E)\subset({\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}\times{\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{B}})(E).

We say that
\mathcal{F}
is
non-critical
if
w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}={w}_{0}
for all
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
.

Now by Berger’s equivalence of categories, we see that
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}
corresponds to the filtered Delingen-Fontaine module
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
equipped with the induced filtration from the Hodge filtration on
D_{{\rm pst}}({\mathbf{D}})
. In this case, we see that the Hodge-Tate weights of
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}
are given by

\{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0})^{-1}(1)},{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0})^{-1}(2)},\cdots,{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0})^{-1}(ir)}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}.\;

This implies that the Hodge-Tate weights of
{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}
are

\{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0})^{-1}((i-1)r+1)},{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0})^{-1}((i-1)r+2)},\cdots,{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0})^{-1}(ir)}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}.\;

We put
w_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{h}})_{j}:=\{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0})^{-1}(j)}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
. In this case, using Berger’s equivalence of categories
[
6
, Theorem A]
and comparing the weight (or see
[
12
, (2.4)]
), we have an injection of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}

\mathbf{I}_{i}:{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}\hookrightarrow{\Delta_{\pi}}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\rm unr}(q_{L}^{i-k})){{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(z^{w_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{h}})_{jr}})={\rm gr}^{\mathcal{F}}_{i}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(z^{w_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{h}})_{jr}}),

(2.2)

for
i=1,\cdots,k
. This implies that the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\mathbf{D}}
admits an
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
.

Let
{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L}}
(resp.,
{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
) be the rigid space over
E
parametrizing continuous characters of
{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset}(L)
(resp.,
{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
). The parameters of
\mathcal{F}
in
{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L}}
or
{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
are given as follows. Recall that
\pi\cong\pi_{0}\otimes{\rm unr}(\alpha_{\pi})
for some
\alpha_{\pi}\in E^{\times}
.

Definition 2.1
.

(Special
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration)

Put
({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L}}
with

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{x}}_{0}=({\mathbf{x}}_{0,i}\cong x_{\pi_{0}})_{1\leq i\leq k},\;{\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}}:=(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}={\rm unr}(\alpha_{\pi}q_{L}^{i-k}){z^{w_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{h}})_{ir}}})_{1\leq i\leq k},\;

(2.3)

or
(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,{\mathbf{h}}},\widetilde{\bm{\delta}}_{\mathbf{h}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
with

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,{\mathbf{h}}}=(\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,i})_{1\leq i\leq k},\;\pi_{\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,{\mathbf{h}},i}}\cong\pi_{0}{\otimes_{E}}{\rm unr}(\alpha_{\pi}q_{L}^{i-k}z^{w_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{h}})_{jr}}(\varpi_{L})),\;\widetilde{\bm{\delta}}_{\mathbf{h}}=(\widetilde{\bm{\delta}}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}={z^{w_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{h}})_{ir}}}|_{\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}})_{1\leq i\leq k}.

(2.4)

We call an
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration on
{\mathbf{D}}
is special with parameter
({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L}}
, (resp.,  with parameter
(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,{\mathbf{h}}},\widetilde{\bm{\delta}}_{\mathbf{h}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
) if
{\mathbf{D}}
admits a
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration with parameter
({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L}}
, (resp., with parameter
(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,{\mathbf{h}}},\widetilde{\bm{\delta}}_{\mathbf{h}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
), and
{\mathbf{D}}^{i+1}_{i}
is non-split for each
ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)
. We say a
p
-adic Galois representation
\rho_{L}:{\rm Gal}_{L}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(E)
admits a special
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration (or special triangulation) if
D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{L})
has this property.

In particular, we can restrict them to classical trianguline case.

Remark 2.2
.

(Special triangulation) If
r=1
(so
k=n
), we have
{\mathbf{L}}_{1,\emptyset}={\mathbf{T}}
and
{\mathbf{P}}_{1,\emptyset}={\mathbf{B}}
. Let
\widehat{T}_{L}
denote the character space of
{\mathbf{T}}(L)
over
E
, i.e., the rigid space over
E
parameterizing continuous character of
{\mathbf{T}}(L)
. Via the isomorphism

\iota_{\Omega_{1}^{\otimes n},{\mathbf{h}}}:\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{1}^{\otimes n}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}\times\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{1,\emptyset},\mathcal{O}_{L}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\widehat{T}_{L},(\underline{x},{\delta^{0}})\mapsto(\boxtimes_{i=1}^{r}\pi_{x_{i}}){\delta^{0}}z^{{\mathbf{h}}}.

if
r=1
and
\pi_{0}=v_{1}^{\frac{1-n}{2}}
, then the
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
on
{\mathbf{D}}
becomes the so-called special triangulation with parameter
{\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}}:=(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}={\rm unr}(\alpha q_{L}^{i-k}){z^{w_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{h}})_{i}}})_{1\leq i\leq n}\in\widehat{T}_{L}
.

Remark 2.3
.

Suppose that
{\mathbf{D}}
is of slope zero, i.e., it comes from a
p
-adic Galois representation
\rho_{L}:{\rm Gal}_{L}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(E)
. If
n=2
, and
w_{\mathcal{F}}\neq\underline{w}_{0}
, then
d_{L}>1
, then
w_{\mathcal{F}}=(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
with
w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}=s_{1}
(resp.,
w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}=1
) if and only if
\tau\in S
(resp.,
\tau\not\in S
), where
S
is a subset of
\Sigma_{L}
. If
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
and
n=3
, the possible choices of
w_{\mathcal{F}}
are only
\{s_{1}{w}_{0},s_{2}w_{0}\}
. Although the non-critical case is the most common, it seems less difficult for an special
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration (in particular, a special triangulation) to be critical as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

2.3
Some preliminaries on potentially semistable deformation ring

The proofs of main theorems need some Zariski-closure argument on semistable deformation rings and the relationship between semistable deformation spaces and trianguline variety. In this section, we make some preliminaries on potentially semistable deformation ring.

Keep the notation in Section
2.2
. Let
\Omega_{n}
be the Bernstein component of
{\rm GL}_{n}(L)
associated to the cuspidal Bernstein component
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
of
{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset}
and let
\tau:I_{L}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(E)
be the associated inertial type (see
[
23
, Section 3.2]
). Recall that
\tau_{0}|_{I_{L^{\prime}}}
is trivial.

Let
\overline{r}:{\rm Gal}_{L}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(k_{E})
be a residual representation of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
. Let
R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
be the unique reduced and
p
-torsion free quotient of the (framed) local deformation ring
R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}
corresponding to potentially semistable deformations of
\overline{r}
with inertial type
\tau
and Hodge-Tate weights
{\mathbf{h}}
(in the sense of
[
46
]
) over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
. By
[
46
, Theorem 3.3.4]
, the space
X_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}:=\Spec R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}[1/p]
is equi-dimensional of dimension
n^{2}+d_{L}\frac{n(n-1)}{2}
. We write
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}:=(\Spf R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}})^{{\rm rig}}
for the closed analytic subspace
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}:=(\Spf R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box})^{{\rm rig}}
. By
[
46
, Theorem 2.5.5]
, there is a universal (coherent) filtered
(\varphi,N)
-module
(\mathcal{D},\Phi,{\mathbf{N}},\mathcal{F}^{\bullet})
that is locally free over
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
such that for all
y\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
, we have
(\mathcal{D},\Phi,{\mathbf{N}},\mathcal{F}^{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}}}k(y)=(D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho_{y}),\;\phi_{y},N_{y},\mathcal{F}_{y}^{\bullet}).

We introduce two stratifications on
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
. For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, we write
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},w}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}\subset{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
for the inverse image of the Bruhat cell

\big(\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}({\mathbf{B}}w{\mathbf{B}}/{\mathbf{B}})\big)^{{\rm rig}}
. Then
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},w}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
is locally closed in
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
and the
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},w}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
for
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
set-theoretically cover
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
. Let
\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},w}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}}
be the Zariski-closure of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},w}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
in
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
.

Another stratification of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
is
given by concerning the sharp of nilpotent operater. For any
x\in X_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
, the Jordan normal norm of the nilpotent operator
N_{x}
is determined uniquely up to conjugacy by a partition
\mathcal{P}_{x}
of
n
. Define a partial order
\leq
on partitions which is the reverse of so-called dominance order, i.e.,
(n_{1},n_{2},\cdots,n_{t})\leq(n_{1}^{\prime},n_{2}^{\prime},\cdots,n_{s}^{\prime})
with
n_{1}\geq n_{2}\geq\cdots\geq n_{t}>0
and
n_{1}^{\prime}\geq n_{2}^{\prime}\geq\cdots\geq n_{s}>0^{\prime}
if and only if
\sum_{i=1}^{l}n_{i}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{l}n_{i}^{\prime}
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{\max}
(resp.,
\mathcal{P}_{\min}
) be the maximal (resp., minimal) partition under this dominance order. Let
\mathcal{N}
be the nilpotent cone of
{\mathfrak{g}}
. Then there is a bijection between the nilpotent orbits of
\mathcal{N}
and the partitions. For partition
\mathcal{P}
, let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}}\subset\mathcal{N}
be the nilpotent orbit associated to the
\mathcal{P}
. In particular, if
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}_{\min}
, then it corresponds to the regular nilpotent orbit
\mathcal{O}_{\reg}:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}_{\min}}
in
{\mathfrak{g}}
. Let
N_{\reg}
be the nilpotent elements with all ones on the superdiagonal, then
\mathcal{O}_{\reg}
is the orbit of
N_{\reg}
.

For partition
\mathcal{P}
, by
[
53
, Definition 4.3]
, there is a reduced,
p
-torsion free quotient
R_{\overline{r},\geq\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}:=R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}/I_{\mathcal{P}}
of
R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
and
X_{\overline{r},\geq\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}:=\Spec R_{\overline{r},\geq\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}[1/p]
such that
x\in X_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}\hookrightarrow X_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
if and only if
\mathcal{P}_{x}\geq\mathcal{P}
. Let
X_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
be the open subspace of
X_{\overline{r},\geq\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
such that
x\in X_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}\hookrightarrow X_{\overline{r},\geq\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
if and only if
\mathcal{P}_{x}=\mathcal{P}
. In particular,
R_{\overline{r},\geq\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
is the potentially crystalline deformation ring
R_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{pcr}}
(resp., potentially semistable deformation ring) if
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}_{\max}
(resp.,
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}_{\min}
).

From now on, we restrict the discussion to the case that
L^{\prime}=L
and
\tau=1
is trivial. We replace the superscript
``\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}"
by
``\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}"
in above notation for simplicity. Let
{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
be the
\big(\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\rm GL}_{n,L}\big)\times_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-torsor of the trivialization of the underlying coherent sheaf of the universal filtered
(\varphi,N)
-module
(\mathcal{D},\Phi,{\mathbf{N}},\mathcal{F}^{\bullet})
. Then sending a semistable deformation with a trivialization of
D_{\mathrm{st}}
to its Frobenius
\Phi
, the monodromy operator
{\mathbf{N}}
and the Hodge filtration define a morphism:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}:{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}\longrightarrow\big((\mathrm{Res}_{L^{\prime}_{0}/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\rm GL}_{n,L^{\prime}_{0}})\times_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E\big)^{{\rm rig}}\times_{\Spm E}

\displaystyle\big((\mathrm{Res}_{L_{0}/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}\mathrm{Mat}_{n,L^{\prime}_{0}})\times_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E\big)^{{\rm rig}}

(2.5)

\displaystyle\times_{\Spm E}\big((\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\rm GL}_{n,L}/\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{B}})\times_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E\big)^{{\rm rig}}.

Keep the notation in
[
43
]
. Let
{\mathscr{D}}_{\phi,\mu}^{\mathrm{ad}}
be the quotient stack of the adic space
\big(X_{\varphi,N}\big)^{\mathrm{ad}}
associated with
X_{\varphi,N}
by the action of
\big(\mathrm{Res}_{L_{0}/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\rm GL}_{n,L_{0}}\big)\times E
. Let
{\mathscr{D}}_{\phi,\mu}^{\mathrm{ad},\mathrm{adm}}
be the open subspace of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\phi,\mu}^{\mathrm{ad}}
such that there is a universal representation of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
on a vector bundle
\mathcal{V}
on
{\mathscr{D}}_{\phi,\mu}^{\mathrm{ad},\mathrm{adm}}
, and
\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}_{\phi,\mu}^{\mathrm{ad},\mathrm{adm}}
be the stack over
{\mathscr{D}}_{\phi,\mu}^{\mathrm{ad},\mathrm{adm}}
trivializing
\mathcal{V}
. By
[
43
]
, we get that
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}
is isomorphic to an open subspace
\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}_{\phi,\mu}^{\mathrm{ad},\mathrm{adm},+}(\overline{r})
of
\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}_{\phi,\mu}^{\mathrm{ad},\mathrm{adm}}
. This shows that
\widetilde{\gamma}
is smooth.

For any partition function
\mathcal{P}
, let
{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
be the fiber of the nilpotent orbit
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}}\subset\mathcal{N}
via the morphism

{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}\longrightarrow\big((\mathrm{Res}_{L_{0}/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}\mathrm{Mat}_{n,L^{\prime}_{0}})\times_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E\big)^{{\rm rig}}
, which is locally closed in
{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
. Let

\overline{{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}}
be the closure of
{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
in
{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
. For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, let
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P},w}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}\subset{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
for the inverse image of the Bruhat cell

\big(\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}({\mathbf{B}}w{\mathbf{B}}/{\mathbf{B}})\times_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E\big)^{{\rm rig}}
. Then
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P},w}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
is locally closed in
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
and the
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P},w}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
for
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
set-theoretically cover
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
.

Proposition 2.4
.

We have

h^{-1}\big(\overline{\big(\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}({\mathbf{B}}w{\mathbf{B}}/{\mathbf{B}})\times_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E\big)^{{\rm rig}}}\big)=\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}}=\coprod_{w^{\prime}\leq w}{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w^{\prime}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}},

where
\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}}
is the closure of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
in
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
(so
\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}}
is equal to the intersection of the closure of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
in
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
with
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
).

Proof.

Since the nilpotent orbit
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}_{\min}}
, i.e., the regular nilpotent orbit is smooth and open dense in
\mathcal{N}
, we get that
{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}\rightarrow\big((\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\rm GL}_{n,L}/\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{B}})\times_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E\big)^{{\rm rig}}
is smooth. By the similar argument as in the proof of
[
17
, Theorem 4.2.3]
or
[
61
, Theorem 4.1]
(
using the fact that smooth morphisms are open), we deduce the closure relations
\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}}=\coprod_{w^{\prime}\leq w}{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w^{\prime}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
when we descent along the map
{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
.
∎

3
Local models on special
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-case

By modifying the methods in
[
12
, Section 6]
, we show that the local geometry of the Bernstein paraboline variety at our potentially semistable non-crysatalline points is closely related to the schemes studied in geometric representation theory.

3.1
Preliminaries

We apply
[
12
, Section 5.1-Section 5.4]
to
{\rm GL}_{n,L}
and get the following schemes. We obtain the affine scheme
{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}
associated to the Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}
. In this paper, we usually add a subscript “
L
” (resp.,
J
) when we consider
\Sigma_{L}
-components (resp.,
J
-components).

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r,L}:=\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r,\tau},\;\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r,\tau}=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r}=\{(g{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset},\psi)\in{\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}\times{\mathfrak{g}}\;|\;\mathrm{Ad}(g^{-1})\psi\in\tau_{{r,\emptyset}}\},\;

(3.1)

where
\tau_{{r,\emptyset}}
is the full radical of
{\mathfrak{p}}_{r,\emptyset}
, i.e.,
\tau_{{r,\emptyset}}={\mathfrak{n}}_{r,\emptyset}\rtimes{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset}
. If
r=1
, we put
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}:=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{1}
(note that
{\mathbf{P}}_{1,\emptyset}={\mathbf{B}}
) and
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{L}:=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{1,L}
. We have natural morphisms
q_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}}:\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
(resp.
q_{{\mathbf{B}}}:\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
) given by
(g{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset},\psi)\mapsto\psi
(resp.,
(g{\mathbf{B}},\psi)\mapsto\psi
). We put

\displaystyle X_{r,L}:=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r,L}\times_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}}\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{L}\cong\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}X_{r,\tau},X_{r,\tau}=X_{r}:=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{r}}\times_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}},

(3.2)

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r}\times_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}=\{(g_{1}{\mathbf{B}},g_{2}{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset},\psi)\in{\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}\times{\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{B}}\times{\mathfrak{g}}\;|\;\mathrm{Ad}(g_{1}^{-1})\psi\in\tau_{{r,\emptyset}},\mathrm{Ad}(g_{2}^{-1})\psi\in{\mathbf{B}}\}.\;

By
[
12
, Corollary 5.2.2]
, the scheme
X_{r}
is equidimensional of dimension
n^{2}-\frac{(r+2)(r-1)k}{2}
(in particular, of dimension
n^{2}
if
r=1
). Let
\pi:X_{r}\hookrightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}\times{\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{B}}\times{\mathfrak{g}}\twoheadrightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}\times{\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{B}}
be the composition. For
w\in\mathscr{W}^{\Delta_{n}^{k},\emptyset}_{n}
, let
X_{r,w}
be the closed subscheme of
X_{r}
defined as the reduced Zariski-closure of
\pi^{-1}(U_{w})
, where
U_{w}:={\rm GL}_{n}(1,w)({\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}\times{\mathbf{B}})
(note that
U_{w}
and thus
X_{r,w}
only depends on the coset
\mathscr{W}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}}w
). The irreducible components of
X_{r}
are indexed by
\{X_{r,w}\}_{w\in\mathscr{W}^{\Delta_{n}^{k},\emptyset}_{n}}
. For
w=(w_{\tau})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, let
X_{r,w}:=\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}X_{r,w_{\tau}}
, this is an irreducible component of
X_{r,L}
which only depends on the coset
\mathscr{W}_{\Delta_{n}^{k},L}w
.

We also need the closed subscheme
q_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}}^{-1}(\tau_{{r,\emptyset}})
of
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{r}}
. Let
U_{w}:={\mathbf{B}}w{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}/{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}
and
V_{w}:=q_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}}^{-1}(\tau_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset},\Sigma_{L}})\cap\pi^{-1}(U_{w})
, where
\pi
is the composition
\pi:\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{r}}\hookrightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}\times{\mathfrak{g}}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}
. Then the projection
V_{w}\rightarrow U_{w}
is a geometric vector bundle of dimension
\dim\tau_{{r,\emptyset}}-\lg(w^{\min})
(the proof is analogous to the proof of
[
12
, Proposition 5.2.1]
). Let
q_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}}^{-1}(\tau_{{r,\emptyset}})_{w}
be the closed subscheme of
q_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}}^{-1}(\tau_{{r,\emptyset}})
defined as the reduced Zariski-closure of
V_{w}
in
q_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}}^{-1}(\tau_{{r,\emptyset}})
. Then we see that the
q_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}}^{-1}(\tau_{{r,\emptyset}})
it is equidimensional of dimension
k+\frac{n(n-r)}{2}
and the irreducible components are given by
q_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}}^{-1}(\tau_{{r,\emptyset}})_{w}
for
w\in\mathscr{W}^{\emptyset,\Delta_{n}^{k}}_{n}
.

Denote by
\kappa_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}}:X_{r,L}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}
(resp.,
\kappa_{{\mathbf{B}}}:X_{r,L}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
) the morphism (recall
[
12
, (5.6)]
)

(g_{1}{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset},g_{2}{\mathbf{B}},\psi)\mapsto\overline{\mathrm{Ad}(g_{1}^{-1})\psi},\;\text{resp.,\;}(g_{1}{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset},g_{2}{\mathbf{B}},\psi)\mapsto\overline{\mathrm{Ad}(g_{2}^{-1})\psi}

where
\overline{\mathrm{Ad}(g_{1}^{-1})\psi}
is the image of
\mathrm{Ad}(g_{1}^{-1})\psi\in\tau_{{r,\emptyset}}
via
\tau_{{r,\emptyset}}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}
and
\overline{\mathrm{Ad}(g_{2}^{-1})\psi}
is the image of
\mathrm{Ad}(g_{2}^{-1})\psi\in{\mathfrak{b}}_{L}
via
{\mathfrak{b}}_{L}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
. For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, let
\kappa_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset},w},\kappa_{{\mathbf{B}},w}
be the restriction of
\kappa_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}},\kappa_{{\mathbf{B}}}
at
X_{r,w}
.

Consider the affine
E
-scheme
\mathcal{T}_{r,L}:={\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}\times_{{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}/\mathscr{W}_{n,L}}{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
. By
[
12
, Lemma 5.2.6]
, the irreducible components of
\mathcal{T}_{r,L}
are given by
\mathcal{T}_{r,w}:=\{(\mathrm{Ad}(w^{-1})z,z):z\in{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}\}
for
w\in\mathscr{W}^{\Delta_{n}^{k},\emptyset}_{n,L}
. We have a natural morphism

\kappa=(\kappa_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}},\kappa_{{\mathbf{B}}}):X_{r,L}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}_{r,L}
. By
[
12
, Lemma 5.2.6]
, we note that
X_{r,w}
is the unique irreducible component of
X_{r,L}
such
\kappa(X_{r,w})=\mathcal{T}_{r,w}
.

The generalized Steinberg variety is defined by
Z_{r,L}=\kappa^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}(0)^{{\rm red}}=\kappa^{-1}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}}(0)^{{\rm red}}
. Let
\mathcal{N}
(resp.,
\mathcal{N}_{L}=\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}
with
\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\cong\mathcal{N}
) be the nilpotent cone in
{\mathfrak{g}}
(resp.,
{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}
). Put

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{r,L}:=\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{r,\tau},\;\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{r,\tau}=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{r}:=\{(g{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset},\psi)\in{\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}\times{\mathfrak{g}}\;|\;\mathrm{Ad}(g^{-1})\psi\in{{\mathfrak{n}}_{r,\emptyset}}\}

(3.3)

If
r=1
, we put
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{L}:=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1,L}
. As in
[
12
, Section 5.4]
, we have the so-called Springer resolution (resp. generalized Springer resolution)
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{L}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}_{L}
(resp.,
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{r,L}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}_{L}
). Then we have

Z_{r,L}:=\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}Z_{r,\tau},\;Z_{r,\tau}=Z_{r}:=(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{r}\times_{\mathcal{N}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}})^{{\rm red}}.\;

(3.4)

We have
Z_{r,L}\cong(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{r,L}\times_{\mathcal{N}_{L}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{L})^{{\rm red}}
. Moreover, by
[
12
, Corollary 5.4.1]
, the irreducible components of
Z_{r}
are index by
\{Z_{r,w}\}_{w\in\mathscr{W}^{\Delta_{n}^{k},\emptyset}_{n}}
. For
w=(w_{\tau})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, we denote
Z_{r,w}=\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}Z_{r,w_{\tau}}\hookrightarrow Z_{r,L}
.

Moreover, we set
\overline{X}_{r,w}:=\kappa_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset},w}^{-1}(0)
. By the argument before
[
12
, Lemma 5.4.4]
,
\overline{X}_{r,w}
is equidimensional of dimension
\dim Z_{r,L}
, and each irreducible components of
\overline{X}_{r,w}
is
Z_{r,w^{\prime}}
for some
w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
.

All above schemes lie in the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{Z_{r,L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{r,L}=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r,L}\times_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}}\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{{\mathbf{B}}}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{r,L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r,L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathfrak{g}}_{L},}

(3.5)

where
q_{{\mathbf{B}}}:X_{r,L}\rightarrow\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r,L}
is the base change of
q_{{\mathbf{B}}}:\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{L}\rightarrow{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{L}
via the morphism
q_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}}:\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r,L}\rightarrow{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{L}
.

For any
J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
and
w\in\mathscr{W}^{\Delta_{n}^{k},\emptyset}_{n,L}
, we put
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r,J}:=\prod_{\tau\in J}\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r,\tau}
,
X_{r,J}:=\prod_{\tau\in J}X_{r,\tau}
and

. We also denote
X_{r,J,w}=\prod_{\tau\in J}X_{r,w_{\tau}}\hookrightarrow X_{r,J}
and
Z_{r,J,w}=\prod_{\tau\in J}Z_{r,w_{\tau}}\hookrightarrow Z_{r,J}
.

In particular, if
r=1
, we omit the subscript

in above notation.

3.2
Recollection of some groupoids

Keep the situation in Section
2.2
. Recall that we have fixed a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\mathbf{D}}
(resp.,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}:={\mathbf{D}}[1/t]
) over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
of rank
n
, which admits an
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
with parameter
({{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L}}
or with parameter
(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,{\mathbf{h}}},\widetilde{\bm{\delta}}_{\mathbf{h}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
. Since our parameter
({{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L}}
is not generic in the sense of
[
12
, (6.5)]
, the groupoids in
[
12
, Section 6]
have different behavior. We study them in this section. Keep the notation in
[
12
, Section 6]
.

3.2.1
Almost de Rham
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules

We recall a little about Fontaine’s theory of almost de Rham representations. Let
B_{{\rm pdR}}^{+}:=B_{{\rm dR}}^{+}[\log t]
and
B_{{\rm pdR}}:=B_{{\rm pdR}}^{+}[1/t]
. The
{\rm Gal}_{L}
-action on
B_{{\rm dR}}
extend uniquely to an action of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
-action on
B_{{\rm pdR}}
with
g(\log t)=\log t+\log({\epsilon}(g))
. Let
v_{{\rm pdR}}
denote the unique
B_{{\rm dR}}
-derivation of
B_{{\rm pdR}}
such that
v_{{\rm pdR}}(\log t)=-1
. Note that
v_{{\rm pdR}}
and
{\rm Gal}_{L}
commute and both preserve
B_{{\rm pdR}}^{+}
.

Let
\mathrm{Rep}_{B_{{\rm dR}}}({\rm Gal}_{L})
(resp.,
\mathrm{Rep}_{B^{+}_{{\rm dR}}}({\rm Gal}_{L})
) be the category of (free of finite rank)
B_{{\rm dR}}
-representations of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
(resp.,
B^{+}_{{\rm dR}}
-representations) of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
. If
W\in\mathrm{Rep}_{B_{{\rm dR}}}({\rm Gal}_{L})
, let
D_{{\rm pdR}}(W):=(B_{{\rm pdR}}\otimes_{B_{{\rm dR}}}W)^{{\rm Gal}_{L}}
, which is a finite-dimensional
L
-vector space of dimension no more than
\dim_{B_{{\rm dR}}}W
. The
B_{{\rm dR}}
-representation
W
is called
almost de Rham
if
\dim_{L}D_{{\rm pdR}}(W)=\dim_{B_{{\rm dR}}}W
. The
B^{+}_{{\rm dR}}
-representation
W^{+}
is called
almost de Rham
if
W^{+}[1/t]
is almost de Rham. Let
\mathrm{Rep}_{{\rm pdR}}({\rm Gal}_{L})
be the category of almost de Rham
B_{{\rm dR}}
-representations
W
of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
.

Let
A\in{\rm Art}_{E}
be a local Artinian
E
-algebra with the maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}_{A}
. Let
\mathrm{Rep}_{{\rm pdR},A}({\rm Gal}_{L})
be the category of almost de Rham
B_{{\rm dR}}
-representations
W
of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
together with a morphism of
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebras
A\rightarrow{\rm End}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{{\rm pdR}}({\rm Gal}_{L})}(W)
such that
W
is finite free over
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A
. Let
\mathrm{Rep}_{A\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}L}({\mathbf{G}}_{a})
be the category of pairs
(V_{A},\nu_{A})
where
\nu_{A}
is a nilpotent endomorphism of a finite free
A\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}L
-module
V_{A}
. Then the functor
D_{{\rm pdR}}
induces an equivalence of categories between
\mathrm{Rep}_{{\rm pdR},A}({\rm Gal}_{L})
and
\mathrm{Rep}_{A\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}L}({\mathbf{G}}_{a})
(see
[
17
, Lemma 3.1.4]
).

3.2.2
Groupoids

We recall some groupoids over
{\rm Art}_{E}
that introduced in
[
12
, Section 6]
. Recall that the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}:={\mathbf{D}}[1/t]
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}[1/t]
admits a
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration
\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}=(\mathcal{M}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k}
with
\mathcal{M}_{i}:={\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}\big[\frac{1}{t}\big]
with parameter
({{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L}}
.

Let
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}=W_{{\rm dR}}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}})
(resp,.
{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:=W_{{\rm dR}}^{+}({\mathbf{D}})
) be the
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm dR}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-representation (resp.,
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm dR}^{+}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-representation) of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
associated to
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}
. Moreover, the
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration
\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}
on
\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
induces a
{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}
-filtration
{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}=({\mathbf{F}}_{i}):=(W_{\rm dR}(\mathcal{M}_{i}))
on
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
with
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm dR}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-subrepresentations of
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
. For
1\leq i\leq k
, we put
{\rm gr}_{i}{{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}:={\mathbf{F}}_{i}/{\mathbf{F}}_{i-1}
, so
{\rm gr}_{i}{{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\cong(B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E)^{\oplus r}
as
{\rm Gal}_{L}
-representation. We recall certain groupoids of deformations of
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
and
{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
.

Let
X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}
be the groupoid over
{\rm Art}_{E}
of deformations
of
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}
, i.e., consists of triples
(A,W_{A},\iota_{A})
where
A\in{\rm Art}_{E}
and
W_{A}\in\mathrm{Rep}_{{\rm pdR},A}({\rm Gal}_{L})
and
\iota_{A}:W_{A}\otimes_{A}L\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
. A morphism
(A,W_{A},\iota_{A})\rightarrow(B,W_{B},\iota_{B})
in
X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}
is a morphism
A\rightarrow B
in
{\rm Art}_{E}
and isomorphism
W_{A}\otimes_{A}B\xrightarrow{\sim}W_{B}
compatible with
\iota_{A}
and
\iota_{B}
. Fix an isomorphism
\alpha:(L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E)^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{\mathrm{pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}})
. Let
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}
be the groupoid over
{\rm Art}_{E}
of
framed deformations of
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}
, i.e., consists of triples
(A,W_{A},\iota_{A},\alpha_{A})
where
(A,W_{A},\iota_{A})\in X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}
and
\alpha_{A}:(A\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E)^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{\mathrm{pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{A})
such that
\alpha_{A}
modulo
{\mathfrak{m}}_{A}
coincides with
\alpha
.

Let
X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
be the groupoid over
{\rm Art}_{E}
of deformations of
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}
together with the filtration
{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}
(see
[
12
, Section 6.1]
), i.e., consists of triples
(A,W_{A},{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet},\iota_{A})
where
(A,W_{A},\iota_{A})\in X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}
and
{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet}=({\mathbf{F}}_{A,i})_{0\leq i\leq k}
is a
{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}
-filtration of
W_{A}
in
\mathrm{Rep}_{{\rm pdR},A}({\rm Gal}_{L})
such that
{\mathbf{F}}_{A,0}=0
and
{\mathbf{F}}_{A,i}/{\mathbf{F}}_{A,i-1}
for
1\leq i\leq k
are free of
r
over
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A
and is isomorphic to
{\mathbf{F}}_{i}/{\mathbf{F}}_{i-1}\otimes_{B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E}\epsilon_{A,i}
for some rank one
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A

\epsilon_{A,i}
and
\iota_{A}
induces
{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet}\otimes_{A}E\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}
. For
1\leq i\leq k
, we put
{\rm gr}_{i}{{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet}}:={\mathbf{F}}_{A,i}/{\mathbf{F}}_{A,i-1}
. Moreover, we put
X^{0}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}:=X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
if the filtration
{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}=(0\subseteq{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}})
(i.e., the trivial one), which is subgroupoid of
X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}
. We put
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}:=X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}}X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}^{\Box}
and
X^{0,\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}:=X^{0}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}}X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}^{\Box}
.

Let
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+}
be the
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm dR}^{+}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-representation
W_{\rm dR}^{+}({\mathbf{D}})
of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
(see
[

, Section 6.3]
). We define groupoid
X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+}}

over
{\rm Art}_{E}
exactly as we define

by replacing
W
,

in

by
W_{\mathbf{D}}^{+}
,
W_{A}^{+}
with
W_{A}^{+}
an almost de Rham
A\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}B_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-representation of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
. We have natural morphism
X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+}}\rightarrow X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}
(by inverting
t
). We put
X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+}}^{\Box}:=X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+}}{\times}_{X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}}X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}^{\Box}
,
X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}:=X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+}}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}}X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
and
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}:=X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}}X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}^{\Box}
.

Now let
\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}=(\mathcal{D}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n}:=(D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{F}}_{j}))_{1\leq i\leq n}
, which is a complete flag of
D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}})
. On the other hand, the

-lattice

induces another complete flag

{\rm Fil}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},\bullet}:=\Big({\rm Fil}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},i}(D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}))\Big)_{1\leq i\leq n},

of
D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}})
(see
[
17
, (3.5)]
), with

\displaystyle{\rm Fil}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},i}(D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}})):=\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}{\rm Fil}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{\tau,n+1-i}}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}}}(D_{{\rm pdR},\tau}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}})):=\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}(t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{\tau,n+1-i}}{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}})_{\tau}^{{\rm Gal}_{L}}

(3.6)

where
D_{{\rm pdR},\tau}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}):=D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}})\otimes_{L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E}(L\otimes_{L,\tau}E)
and
(t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{\tau,n+1-i}}{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}})_{\tau}:=t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{\tau,n+1-i}}\big({\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}}\otimes_{L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E}(L\otimes_{L,\tau}E)\big)
.

Let
y
be the closed point of the
E
-scheme
X_{r,L}
corresponding to the triple
(\alpha^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}),\;\alpha^{-1}({\rm Fil}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},\bullet}),N_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}})

). In the sequel, we write
y_{1}:={\rm pr}_{1}y=(\alpha^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}),N_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}})\in\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r,L}
,
y_{2}:={\rm pr}_{2}y=(\alpha^{-1}({\rm Fil}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},\bullet}),N_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}})\in\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{L}
and
z=(N_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}})\in{{\mathfrak{g}}}
. We have:

•

by
[
17
, Corollary 3.1.6]
(resp.,
[
17
, Theorem 3.2.5]
), the groupoid
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}\cong|X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}^{\Box}|
(resp.,

) is pro-representable, and the functor
|X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}|
(resp.,
|X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+}}^{\Box}|
