\widehat{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}}_{L,z}
(resp.,
\widehat{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}}_{L,y_{2}}
);

•

by
[
12
, Proposition 6.1.2]
(resp.,
[
12
, Proposition 6.3.2 (1)]
), the groupoid
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\cong|X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\Box}|
(resp.,
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\cong|X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}|
) is pro-representable, and the functor
|X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}|
(resp.,
|X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}|
) is pro-representated by the formal scheme
\widehat{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}}_{r,L,y_{1}}
(resp.,
\widehat{X}_{r,L,y}
).

For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
, we define the groupoid
X^{\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}:=X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\times_{|X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}|}\widehat{X}_{r,w,y}
. Then the groupoid
X^{\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
over
{\rm Art}_{E}
is pro-representable. The functor
|X^{\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}|
is pro-represented by the formal scheme
\widehat{X}_{r,w,y}
. Let
X^{w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
be the image of
X^{\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
by the forgetful morphism
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
. By
[
12
, Proposition 6.3.4]
, the morphism of groupoids
X^{w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
,
X^{\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
are relatively representable and are closed immersions.

We then list some groupoids of
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-deformations of
\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
(or
{\mathbf{D}}
).

We say
\mathcal{M}_{A}
over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
is of type
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
if there exists a filtration
\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}=(\mathcal{M}_{A,i})_{0\leq i\leq k}
by
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule of
\mathcal{M}_{A}
over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}[1/t]
such that
\mathcal{M}_{A,0}=0
and
\mathcal{M}_{A,i}/\mathcal{M}_{A,i-1}\cong\Delta_{x_{i}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}(\delta_{A,i})[1/t]
for some continuous character
\delta_{A,i}:L^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
and
x_{i}\in\Spec{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}
. Such a filtration is called a
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration, and
((x_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k},(\delta_{A,i})_{1\leq i\leq k})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L}}
is called a parameter of
\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}
.

As in
[
12
, Section 6.2]
, we define the groupoid
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
over
{\rm Art}_{E}
of
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}
, i.e., consist of triples
(A,\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet},j_{A})
where
\mathcal{M}_{A}
is a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}[1/t]
of type
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
, and
\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}
is an
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration of
\mathcal{M}_{A}
such that
j_{A}:\mathcal{M}_{A}\otimes_{A}E\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}
is compatible with the filtrations. By
[
12
, Lemma 6.2.2]
, for
(A,\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet},j_{A})\in X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
, there exist unique characters
\underline{\delta}_{A}=(\delta_{A,i})_{1\leq i\leq k}:L^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
such that
\delta_{A,i}\equiv\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}\;{\textrm{mod}}\;{\mathfrak{m}}_{A}
and
((x_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k},(\delta_{A,i})_{1\leq i\leq k})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L}}
is a parameter of
\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}
.

Let
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}
be the groupoid over
{\rm Art}_{E}
by forgetting everywhere the
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtrations in
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
. The functor
W_{\rm dR}
defines a morphism of groupoids
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}\rightarrow X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}
and
X_{\mathcal{M},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
. We put
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}^{\Box}:=X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}}X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}^{\Box}
and
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}^{\Box}:=X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}}X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}^{\Box}
.

Let
X_{{\mathbf{D}}}
be the groupoid over
{\rm Art}_{E}
of deformations of
{\mathbf{D}}
(see
[
12
, Section 6.3]
). We have natural morphisms
X_{{\mathbf{D}}}\rightarrow X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+}}
(induced by the functor
W_{\rm dR}^{+}(-)
),
X_{{\mathbf{D}}}\rightarrow X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}
(by inverting
t
). Note that we have a natural morphism
X_{{\mathbf{D}}}\rightarrow X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+}}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}}X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}
, which is an equivalence by
[
17
, Proposition 3.5.1]
. We put

X_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{\Box}:=X_{{\mathbf{D}}}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}}X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}^{\Box}
, and
X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}^{\Box}:=X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}}X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}}^{\star}
. For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
, We put

\displaystyle X^{\star,w}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}:=X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\times_{X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}}X^{\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}},X^{w}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}:=X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}}X^{w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}.\;

(3.7)

By
[
12
, Proposition 6.3.4]
, the morphism of groupoids
X^{w}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
and
X^{\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
are relatively representable and are closed immersions.

Let
\rho_{L}:{\rm Gal}_{L}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(E)
be a continuous group morphism and let
V(\rho_{L})
be the associated representation of
\rho_{L}
. Supppse
{\mathbf{D}}=D_{{\rm rig}}(V(\rho_{L}))
. Recall that
X_{\rho_{L}}
denotes the groupoid over
{\rm Art}_{E}
of deformations of the group morphism
\rho_{L}
. Let
X_{V(\rho_{L})}
be the groupoid over
{\rm Art}_{E}
of deformations of the representation
V(\rho_{L})
. We can identity
X_{\rho_{L}}
with the framed deformations of
V(\rho_{L})
. Therefore the morphisms
X_{\rho_{L}}\rightarrow X_{V(\rho_{L})}
is relatively representable and formally smooth of relative dimension
n^{2}
. We also have an equivalence
X_{V(\rho_{L})}\xrightarrow{\sim}X_{{\mathbf{D}}}
(induced by the
D_{{\rm rig}}(-)
-functor) and
X_{\rho_{L}}\xrightarrow{\sim}|X_{\rho_{L}}|
. We put

X_{V(\rho_{L}),\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}:=X_{V(\rho_{L})}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{D}}}}X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}},\;X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}:=X_{\rho_{L}}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{D}}}}X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}.

Then
X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X_{\rho_{L}}
is a closed immersion by base change. For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, we define

\displaystyle X^{w}_{V(\rho_{L}),\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}:=X_{V(\rho_{L})}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{D}}}}X^{w}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}},\;X^{w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}:=X_{\rho_{L}}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{D}}}}X^{w}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}},\;\hskip 100.0pt

(3.8)

and their
\Box
-versions
X^{\star,w}_{V(\rho_{L}),\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
,
X^{\star,w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
. In next section, in order to study groupoids
X^{\bullet,\star}_{\ast,\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
for
\ast\in\{\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},{\mathbf{D}},\rho_{L}\}
,
\bullet\in\{\Box,\emptyset\}
and
\star\in\{w,\emptyset\}
, we introduce certain groupoid
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
.

3.3
Variation of local models and its geometry

This section aim to study
X^{\Box}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
(for
\star\in\{\Box,{\rm ver}\}
) and find its local model. More precisely, recall that we have a morphism of groupoids over
{\rm Art}_{E}
:

\omega_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}:X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{(\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L})}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}

(3.9)

by setting the triple
(A,\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet},j_{A})
to the parameter of
\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}
given in
[
12
, Lemma 6.2.2]
(in precise,
(A,\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet},j_{A})\in X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
, there exist unique characters
\underline{\delta}_{A}=(\delta_{A,i})_{1\leq i\leq k}:L^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
such that
\delta_{A,i}\equiv\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}\;{\textrm{mod}}\;{\mathfrak{m}}_{A}
and
((x_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k},(\delta_{A,i})_{1\leq i\leq k})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L}}
is a parameter of
\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}
). Let
\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}
be the completion of
{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}
at
0
. By the diagram in
[
12
, Proposition 6.2.3, Theorem 6.2.6]
, we have a morphism, i.e., the so-called
local model map
(induced by
\omega_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}
and the natural morphism
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
):

\Upsilon:X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{(\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L})}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}\times_{\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}}X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}.\;

(3.10)

To analysis this morphism
\Upsilon
, we begin with some computations on the cohomology of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}[1/t]
.

For
1\leq i,j\leq k
, denote by
\mathcal{M}_{i,j}^{0}:={\rm End}(\Delta_{\pi}){{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}^{-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},j})
and
\mathcal{M}_{i,j}:=\mathcal{M}_{i,j}^{0}[1/t]
. In particular, we have
\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}\cong{\rm End}(\Delta_{\pi}){{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}^{-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i-1})[1/t]={\rm End}(\Delta_{\pi}){{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\epsilon})[1/t]
, which is not dependent on
2\leq i\leq k
.

Lemma 3.1
.

(1) For any
j\neq i-1,i
, we have
{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j})={\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j})=0
, and
\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j})=d_{L}r^{2}
.

(2) For
2\leq i\leq k
, we also have
{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1})={\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1})=0
, and
\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j})=d_{L}r^{2}
.

Proof.

Part (1) follows from
[
12
, Lemma 6.2.5]
, since the parameter of
\mathcal{M}_{i,j}
is generic in the sense of
[
12
, (6.5)]
. We prove
(2)
. We deduce from the morphism
t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0}\hookrightarrow t^{-N-1}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0}
a long exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{-N-1}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{-N-1}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0}/t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0})\rightarrow

(3.11)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{-N-1}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{-N-1}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0}/t^{-\sigma_{N}}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0})\rightarrow

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{-N-1}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{-{N-1}}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0}/t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0})\rightarrow 0.

By
[
47
, Theorem 4.7]
, we see that
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{-N-1}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0}/t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0})=0
and

\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{-N-1}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0}/t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0})=\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{-N-1}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0}/t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0})<\infty.\;

By
[
14
, Lemma 5.1.1]
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{-N-1}

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0}/t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0})\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{0}({\rm Gal}_{L},t^{-N-1}W_{{\rm dR}}^{+}(\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0})/t^{-\sigma_{N}}W_{{\rm dR}}^{+}(\mathcal{M}_{i,i-1}^{0}))

(3.12)

\displaystyle\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{0}\big({\rm Gal}_{L},t^{-N-1}W_{{\rm dR}}^{+}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}^{-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i-1}))/t^{-N}W_{{\rm dR}}^{+}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}^{-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i-1}))\big)^{\oplus r^{2}}.

By
[
49
, Lemma 2.16]
, the latter is when
N
is sufficiently large. Then by (
3.11
) and
[
17
, (3.11)]
, we get that
{\mathrm{H}}^{j}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j})={\mathrm{H}}^{j}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,j}^{0})
for sufficiently large
N
. Since the parameter of
t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,j}^{0}
is generic in the sense of
[
12
, (4.13)]
for sufficiently large
N
, Part
(2)
follows from
[
12
, Lemma 4.1.12]
.
∎

Lemma 3.2
.

The morphism
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|\rightarrow|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}|
is relatively representable, and
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|
is a subfunctor of
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}|
. Therefore the morphism
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}
of groupoids is relatively representable and is a closed immersion.

Proof.

We first to show
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|
that is a subfunctor
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}|
, i.e., the
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration
\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}
deforming
\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}
on a deformation
\mathcal{M}_{A}
is unique. This follows from the similar argument in
[
12
, Lemma 4.1.14]
. The proof proceeds by induction on the length of
\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}
, we should show that
\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}
is an
\Omega
-filtration on
\mathcal{M}_{A}
, then
\mathcal{M}_{A,1}
is uniquely determined as a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule of
\mathcal{M}_{A}
. Now suppose that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{A,1}
is another
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule of
\mathcal{M}_{A}
deforming
\mathcal{M}_{1}
. Observe that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{A,1}
(resp.,
\mathcal{M}_{A}/\mathcal{M}_{A,1}
) is a successive extension of
\mathcal{M}_{1}
(resp.,
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}_{1}
), then by Lemma
3.1
, we deduce
{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{A,1},\mathcal{M}_{A}/\mathcal{M}_{A,1})=0
. Therefore, we see that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{A,1}\subset\mathcal{M}_{A,1}
. Then we see that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{A,1}=\mathcal{M}_{A,1}
since they have the same rank. On the other hand, by the same argument as in
[
12
, Proposition 6.2.8]
, we have an equivalence of groupoids over
{\rm Art}_{E}
:

X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\xrightarrow{\sim}X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}\times_{|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}|}|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|,

and
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|\hookrightarrow|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}|
is still relatively representable. The last statement is a direct consequence of the first assertion.
∎

Using the same argument as in the proof of
[
12
, Proposition 6.2.10]
, we deduce:

Proposition 3.3
.

The groupoid
X^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
(for
\star\in\{\Box,{\rm ver}\}
) over
{\rm Art}_{E}
is pro-representable. The functor
X^{\Box}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
(resp.,
X^{\mathrm{ver}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
) is pro-represented by a formally smooth noetherian complete local ring of residue field
E
and dimension
d_{L}\big(n^{2}+k+\frac{n(n-r)}{2}\big)
(resp.,
k+d_{L}\big(k+\frac{n(n-r)}{2}\big)
).

Proof.

By the same argument as in
[
12
, Lemma 6.2.9]
, we can show that
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
(and thus
X^{\Box}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
) is relatively representable. Since
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
is pro-representable, so the first statement follows. For the second assertion, we recall the groupoids
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}
and
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver},\Box}
defined in the proof of
[
12
, Proposition 6.2.10]
. Recall that
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}\cong|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}|
. We first show that
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}|
is pro-representable. It is clear that
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}},1},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}|
is pro-represented by
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{L^{\times}},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},1}}\cong E[[x_{1},\cdots,x_{d_{L}+1}]]
. Now assume that
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}},i-1},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}|
is pro-represented by a formally smooth noetherian complete local ring
R_{i-1}
of residue field
E
and dimension
i-1+d_{L}(i-1+r^{2}\frac{i(i-1)}{2})
. Let
S_{i}
denoted the completion of
R_{i-1}\otimes_{E}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{L^{\times}},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}}
with respect to the maximal ideal generated by the maximal ideal of
R_{i-1}
and the one of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{L^{\times}},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}}
. For any morphism
S_{i}\rightarrow A
with
A\in{\rm Art}_{E}
, let
\mathcal{M}_{i-1,A}
be the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}[1/t]
given by the pull-back along
R_{i-1}\rightarrow S_{i}\rightarrow A
of the universal
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{R_{i-1},L}[1/t]
and let
\delta_{A,i}
be the character
L^{\times}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{L^{\times}},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}}\rightarrow S_{i}\rightarrow A
. Let

N_{i}:=\varprojlim_{S_{i}\rightarrow A}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}\Big(\Delta_{\pi}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}(\delta_{A,i})\Big[\frac{1}{t}\Big],\mathcal{M}_{i-1,A}\Big).

(3.13)

By Lemma
3.1
, we deduce that
N_{i}
is a free
S_{i}
-module of rank
(i-1)d_{L}r^{2}
(use Lemma
3.1
(1) and a dévissage). Then
[\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}},i}]
correspondences to a maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}
with residue field
E
of the polynomial
S_{i}
-algebra
\mathrm{Symm}_{S_{i}}N_{i}^{\vee}
. Let
R_{i}
be the completion of
\mathrm{Symm}_{S_{i}}N_{i}^{\vee}
at
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}
. One can check that
X^{\mathrm{ver}}_{\mathcal{M}_{i},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
is pro-represented by
R_{i}
. In particular,
X^{\mathrm{ver}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
is pro-represented by a formally smooth noetherian complete local ring of residue field
E
and dimension
k+d_{L}\big(k+\frac{n(n-r)}{2}\big)
. By the same argument as in the last paragraph in the proof of
[
12
, Proposition 6.2.10]
, we obtain the dimension of
X^{\Box}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
.
∎

Suppose
y_{A}:=(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}},A},\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet},j_{A})
is an object in
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(A)
, and
\underline{\delta}_{A}=(\delta_{A,i})_{1\leq i\leq k}:L^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}\in\widehat{(\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L})}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}(A)
is a parameter of
\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}
(note that
\delta_{A,i}:L^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
are continuous characters such that
\delta_{A,i}\equiv\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}\;{\textrm{mod}}\;{\mathfrak{m}}_{A}
). Let
\mathcal{M}_{A,i,j}={\rm End}(\Delta_{\pi}){{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\delta}_{A,i}^{-1}{\delta}_{A,j})[1/t]
. For
J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
, recall

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g,J}(\mathcal{M}_{A,i,j}):={\rm Ker}\big[{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{M}_{A,i,j})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR},J}(\mathcal{M}_{A,i,j}))\big].

In particular,
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g,\Sigma_{L}}(\mathcal{M}_{A,i,j})={\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g}(\mathcal{M}_{A,i,j})
.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
1\leq i,j\leq k
and
J\subsetneqq\Sigma_{L}
.

(1)

If
j\neq i-1,i
, the natural morphism
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j}))
is an isomorphism.

(2)

If
j=i-1
, we have
\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j})=1
and
\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g,J}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j})=(d_{L}-|J|)r^{2}
. Moreover, the natural morphism
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR},J}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j}))
is a surjection.

(3)

Then the natural morphism

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{M}_{A,i,j})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}(\mathcal{M}_{A,i,j})),j\neq i-1,i

(3.14)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{M}_{A,i,i-1})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR},J}(\mathcal{M}_{A,i,i-1}))

are surjectvie.

Proof.

When
N
is sufficiently large, it is enough to study the map

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,j}^{0})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}(t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,j}^{0}))\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j})).

In this case, we have
t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,j}^{0}={\rm End}(\Delta_{\pi})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\rm unr}(q_{L}^{j-i})t^{{\mathbf{k}}})
for some
{\mathbf{k}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d_{L}}_{<0}
. So the first one is
[
12
, Proposition 6.2.5 (2)]
. By
[
49
]
, we translate to the language of
E
-
B
-pairs. Let
{{\mathbf{W}}_{\pi,N}^{i,j}}
be the
E
-
B
-pair associated to
t^{-N}\mathcal{M}_{i,j}^{0}
, we have
\widetilde{H}^{2}_{J}({\rm Gal}_{L},{{\mathbf{W}}_{\pi,N}^{i,j}})=0
for
J\subsetneqq\Sigma_{L}
and

\dim_{E}\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\Sigma_{L}}({\rm Gal}_{L},{{\mathbf{W}}_{\pi,N}^{i,j}})=1
. Then
(2)
follows from the dimension formula
\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g,J}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j})=d_{L}r^{2}+\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{0}({\rm Gal}_{L},\mathcal{M}_{i,j})+\dim_{E}\widetilde{H}^{2}_{J}({\rm Gal}_{L},{{\mathbf{W}}_{\pi,N}^{i,j}})-\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{0}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR},J}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j}))
in
[
26
, Proposition A.3]
. Finally, the surjectivity of the first (resp., second) morphism in (
3.14
) follows from
(1)
(resp.,
(2)
) together with the fact that the functor
W\mapsto{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W)
on
W\in\mathrm{Rep}_{{\rm pdR},E}({\rm Gal}_{L})
is right exact.
∎

Remark 3.5
.

If
L\neq{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
and
r=1
, Part
(2)
implies that the natural inclusion
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j})\subseteq{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g,\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}(\mathcal{M}_{i,j})
induces an isomorphism for any
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
.

Lemma
3.4
implies that the local model map
\Upsilon
in (
3.10
) is not longer formally smooth in our case. To overcome this problem, we introduce certain subgroupoid
X^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
of
\widehat{(\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L})}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}\times_{\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}}X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
in Section
3.3.1
. We try to analysis the geometry via some power formall series in Section
3.3.3
.

3.3.1
Main constructions

Let
A\twoheadrightarrow B
be a surjective map in
{\rm Art}_{E}
, and let
K=\ker(A\twoheadrightarrow B)
. Suppose
y_{A}:=(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}},A},\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet},j_{A})
(resp.,
y_{B}:=(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}},B},\mathcal{M}_{B,\bullet},j_{B})
) is an object in
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(A)
(resp.,
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(B)
). Suppose that
x_{A}
is isomorphic to
x_{B}
when modulo
J
.

Let
\underline{\delta}_{A}=(\delta_{A,i})_{1\leq i\leq k}:L^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
(resp.,
\underline{\delta}_{B}=(\delta_{B,i})_{1\leq i\leq k}:L^{\times}\rightarrow B^{\times}
) be the parameter of
\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}
(resp.,
B
) given by
[
12
, Lemma 6.2.2]
. For
2\leq i\leq k
, we see that the exact functor
W_{{\rm dR}}
induces natural morphisms

j_{\underline{\delta}_{\ast},i}:{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{\ast,i,i-1})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}({\mathcal{M}}_{\ast,i,i-1})),\ast\in\{A,B\}.\;

(In particular,
\dim_{E}\mathrm{Im}j_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}},i}=d_{L}r^{2}-1
by Lemma
3.4
(2)). By the proof of
[
17
, Theorem 3.4.4]
and Lemma
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,i-1})\otimes_{A}B\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i,i-1}),

(3.15)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,i-1}))\otimes_{A}B\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i,i-1})).

They fit into a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,i-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{\underline{\delta}_{A},i}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i,i-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{\underline{\delta}_{B},i}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,i-1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{\underline{\delta}_{A},\underline{\delta}_{B},i}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i,i-1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

(3.16)

where the morphism
j_{\underline{\delta}_{A},\underline{\delta}_{B},i}
is induced by modulo
K
. Furthermore, it is easy to see that
\ker j_{\underline{\delta}_{A},\underline{\delta}_{B},i}\cong J{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,i-1}))
(as a
A
-module) and

j^{-1}_{\underline{\delta}_{A},\underline{\delta}_{B}}(\mathrm{Im}j_{\underline{\delta}_{B},i})=\mathrm{Im}j_{\underline{\delta}_{A},i}+J{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,i-1}))
by diagram chasing. From this we deduce

Lemma 3.6
.

Keep the above situation and notation. We have a short exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow J{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,i-1}))\cap\mathrm{Im}j_{\underline{\delta}_{A},i}\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}j_{\underline{\delta}_{A},i}\xrightarrow{j_{\underline{\delta}_{A},\underline{\delta}_{B}},i}\mathrm{Im}j_{\underline{\delta}_{B},i}\rightarrow 0.

(3.17)

Denote by
\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}:=\widehat{(\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L})}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}\times_{\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}}X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\cong{\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset}}\times X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
for simplicity (note that
\widehat{(\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L})}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}\cong\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset}\times\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}
, similar to the argument after
[
17
, (3.17)]
). We define a full subgroupoid
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
of
\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
. Roughly speaking,
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
is certain subgroupoid of
\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
that describes the “image” of the local model map
\Upsilon
in (
3.10
), i.e., the deformations of
({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet})
that come from deformations of
(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet})
.

Definition 3.7
.

The objects of
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
are the
5
-tuples
(A,\underline{\delta}_{A},{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A},{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet},\iota_{A})

where

•

A\in{\rm Art}_{E}
and
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A}
is a
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A
-representation of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
;

•

\underline{\delta}_{A}=(\delta_{A,i})_{1\leq i\leq k}:L^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
such that
\delta_{A,i}\equiv\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}{\textrm{mod}}\;{\mathfrak{m}}_{A}
;

•

{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet}
is a
{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}
-filtration on
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A}
by
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A
-subrepresentation of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
such that
{\mathbf{F}}_{A,0}=0
and
{\mathbf{F}}_{A,i}/{\mathbf{F}}_{A,i-1}
(
1\leq i\leq k
) is free of rank
r
over
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A
and isomorphic to
{\mathbf{F}}_{i}/{\mathbf{F}}_{i-1}\otimes_{B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E}W_{{\rm dR}}(\delta_{A,i})
;

•

\iota_{A}:{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A}\otimes_{A}E\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}
is an isomorphism of
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-representations of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
which induces isomorphism
\iota_{A}:{\mathbf{F}}_{i,A}\otimes_{A}E\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathbf{F}}_{i}
for
0\leq i\leq k
;

•

Put
\mathcal{M}_{\underline{\delta}_{A},i,i-1}:={\rm End}(\Delta_{\pi}){{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}({\delta}_{A,i}^{-1}{\delta}_{A,i-1})[1/t]
. The natural morphism

j_{\underline{\delta}_{A},i}:{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{M}_{\underline{\delta}_{A},i,i-1})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}(\mathcal{M}_{\underline{\delta}_{A},i,i-1}).\;

Then

{\mathbf{F}}_{A,i}/{\mathbf{F}}_{A,i-2}\in\mathrm{Im}(j_{\underline{\delta}_{A},i})
for
2\leq i\leq k
, where we view
{\mathbf{F}}_{A,i}/{\mathbf{F}}_{A,i-2}
as an element in the extension group
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}(\mathcal{M}_{\underline{\delta}_{A},i,i-1}))
.

A morphism
(A,\underline{\delta}_{A},{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A},{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet},\iota_{A})\rightarrow(A^{\prime},\underline{\delta}_{A^{\prime}},{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A^{\prime}},{\mathbf{F}}_{A^{\prime},\bullet},\iota_{A^{\prime}})
is a morphism
A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
in
{\rm Art}_{E}
and an isomorphism
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A}\otimes_{A}A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A^{\prime}}
of
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A^{\prime}
-representation of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
which are compatible with all above structures.

The following proposition is an analogue of
[
12
, Theorem 6.2.6]
.

Proposition 3.8
.

The morphism
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
of groupoids over
{\rm Art}_{E}
factors through the full subgroupoid
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\hookrightarrow\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
. Moreover, the morphism
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
of groupoids over
{\rm Art}_{E}
is formally smooth.

Proof.

The proof of
[
17
, Theorem 3.4.4]
is also suitable for us. Suppose
y_{A}:=(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}},A},\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet},j_{A})
(resp.,
y_{B}:=(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}},B},\mathcal{M}_{B,\bullet},j_{B})
) is an object in
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(A)
(resp.,
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(B)
). Suppose that
x_{A}
is isomorphic to
x_{B}
when modulo
K
. In our case, the sujectivity of
[
17
, (3.23)]
is replaced by the
sujectivity of

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,i-1})\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}j_{\underline{\delta}_{A},i}\times_{\mathrm{Im}j_{\underline{\delta}_{B},i}}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i,i-1})

(3.18)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Im}j_{A}\times_{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i,i-1}))}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i,i-1}).

This is a direct consequence of
[
17
, Lemma 3.4.5]
, Lemma
3.6
, commutative diagram (
3.16
) and (
3.17
). The result follows.
∎

Lemma 3.9
.

\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
is formally smooth over
{\rm Art}_{E}
.

Proof.

Let
A\rightarrow A/I
be a surjection in
{\rm Art}_{E}
with
I^{2}=0
. We show that
|\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}|(A)\rightarrow|\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}|(A/I)
is surjective. Let
(\underline{\delta}_{A/I},{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},{A/I}},{\mathbf{F}}_{A/I,\bullet},\iota_{A/I})\in|\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}|(A/I)
. Since
\widehat{(\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L})}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}
is formally smooth, we can choose a lifting
\underline{\delta}_{A}:L^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
, which is a continuous character such that
\underline{\delta}_{A}\equiv\underline{\delta}_{A/I}\;\mathrm{mod}\;I
. On the other hand, the diagram (
3.16
) and the exact sequence (
3.17
) show that the natural morphisms
\mathrm{Im}(j_{\underline{\delta}_{A},i})\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}(j_{\underline{\delta}_{A/I},i})
is surjective for each
1\leq i\leq k-1
. By induction on
1\leq i\leq k-1
, we can construct a object
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},{A}}
which is a successive extension of
{\mathbf{F}}_{A,i+1}/{\mathbf{F}}_{A,i}\otimes_{B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}}E}W_{{\rm dR}}({\delta}_{A,i})
such that
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},{A}}\equiv{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},{A/I}}\;\mathrm{mod}\;I
. The natural filtration
{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet}
on
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},{A}}
also gives
{\mathbf{F}}_{A/I,\bullet}
when modulo
I
. This completes the proof.
∎

Choose
({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A},{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet},\iota_{A})\in X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}(A)
. We have
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A}\cong\oplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A,\tau}
with respect to the isomorphism
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A\cong\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}B_{{\rm dR},\tau}
(
B_{{\rm dR},\tau}:=B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{L,\tau}A
). It equipped with an
L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-linear operator
N_{A}=\oplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}N_{A,\tau}
. For
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
,
N_{A,\tau}=0
if and only if
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A}
is
\tau
-de Rham.

For any
J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
, we put
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A,J}\cong\oplus_{\tau\in J}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A,\tau}
. Therefore, we let

X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}
(resp.,
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}
) be the
J
-component of
X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
(resp.,
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
), i.e., for
A\in{\rm Art}_{E}
, we define its
A
-points by

X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}(A)=\left\{(A,{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A,J},{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet,J},\iota_{A,J})\right\},\;\big(\text{resp.,\;}X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}(A)=\left\{(A,{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A,J},{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet,J},\iota_{A,J},\alpha_{A,J})\right\}\big).

We can define
X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}
and
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}
in a similar way. Note that
L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A\cong\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}A
, we also have a natural decomposition
D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A})\cong\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}D_{{\rm pdR},\tau}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A})
(note that
D_{{\rm pdR},\tau}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A})\cong D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A,\tau})
by writing
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A\cong B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{L}(L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A)
). For any
J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
, we put

D_{{\rm pdR},J}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A})\cong\prod_{\tau\in J}D_{{\rm pdR},\tau}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}},A})
.
Hence the point
y_{1,J}=(y_{1,\tau})_{\tau\in J}
(resp.,
y_{J}=(y_{\tau})_{\tau\in J}
) lies in
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r,J}
(resp.,
X_{r,J}
). It is easy to see that the groupoid
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}
(resp.,
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}
) over
{\rm Art}_{E}
is pro-representable, and the functor
|X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}|
(resp.,
|X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}|
) is pro-representated by the formal scheme
\widehat{X}_{r,J,y_{J}}
(resp.,
\widehat{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}}_{r,J,y_{1,J}}
).

Put scheme
Y_{r,L}:={{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset}}\times{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}}_{r,L}
. For
J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
, put
Y_{r,J}:={\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset}\times{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}}_{r,J}
. Let
\widehat{y}_{1}=(\underline{0},y_{1})\in Y_{r,L}
, and
\widehat{y}_{1,J}
the corresponding point in
Y_{r,J}
. We define
\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}:=\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset}\times X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}
. Then the functor
|X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}|
is pro-representated by the formal scheme
\widehat{Y}_{r,J,\;\widehat{y}_{1,J}}
.

Proposition 3.10
.

For any
J\subsetneqq\Sigma_{L}
, the composition
{X}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\twoheadrightarrow\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}
and the composition
\iota_{J}:\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\twoheadrightarrow\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}
of groupoids over
{\rm Art}_{E}
are formally smooth. In particular,
{X}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}
and
\iota_{J}:\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}
are also formally smooth.

Proof.

We follows the route of
[
17
, Theorem 3.4.4]
. Let
A\twoheadrightarrow B
be a surjective map in
{\rm Art}_{E}
. Note that for
i\neq j
we have commutative diagram (as in (
3.16
)):

\textstyle{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,j})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{y_{A},i}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i,j})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR},J}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,j}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR},J}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i,j}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

(3.19)

By replacing the
[
17
, Lemma 3.4.3]
with our Lemma
3.4

(3)
, and using
[
17
, Lemma 3.4.5]
, we get the sujectivity of the following map (similar to the sujectivity of
[
17
, (3.23)]
):

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,j})\longrightarrow\;{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR},J}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,j}))\times_{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR},J}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i,j}))}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i,j}).

(3.20)

and thus the surjectivity of

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{1}

\displaystyle{}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i-1}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\delta}_{A,i}^{-1})[1/t])\longrightarrow

(3.21)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR},J}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i-1}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\delta}_{A,i}^{-1})[1/t]))

\displaystyle\times_{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR},J}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i-1}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\delta}_{B,i}^{-1})[1/t]))}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i-1}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\delta}_{B,i}^{-1})[1/t]).

By the same argument as in the proof of

, Theorem 3.4.4]
, we get that
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}
is formally smooth. Note that this morphism factors through

, therefore

is formally smooth (since any object in
