has a preimage in
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
by definition of
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
).
∎

Corollary 3.11
.

For
W\in\{X,\widehat{X}\}
, the natural morphisms
{X}^{\Box}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow W_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}
and
\iota_{J}:\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow W_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}
of groupoids over
{\rm Art}_{E}
are formally smooth.

3.3.2
Representablity

For
1\leq i\leq k
, we fix isomorphisms
\alpha_{i}:L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{\mathrm{pdR}}({\rm gr}_{i}{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet})
(so we fix an isomorphism
\beta:(L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E)^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{\mathrm{pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}})=\oplus_{i=1}^{k}D_{\mathrm{pdR}}({\rm gr}_{i}{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet})
). Consider the groupoids

W_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}:=X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\times_{\prod_{i=1}^{k}X_{{\rm gr}_{i}{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}X^{0,\Box}_{{\rm gr}_{i}{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}},\;W\in\{X,\widehat{X}\}.\;

(3.22)

(where the
\Box
in
X^{0,\Box}_{{\rm gr}_{i}{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
are given by the tuples
\underline{\alpha}:=(\alpha_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k}
) and
W_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\Box}
for
W\in\{X,\widehat{X}\}
(with respect to the isomorphism
\beta:(L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E)^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{\mathrm{pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}})
). For
\star\in\{\Box,\emptyset\}
, the functor
D_{{\rm pdR}}(-)
induce the following natural morphisms:

X^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\ast}\rightarrow X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\ast}.

This section aims to prove the pro-representablity of
\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\star}
for
\star\in\{\Box,{\rm ver}\}
. Note that a object in
X^{\mathrm{ver}}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}(A)
is a
5
-tuples
(A,W_{A},{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet},\iota_{A},\underline{\alpha}_{A})
, by imposing the additional data
\underline{\alpha}_{A}=(\alpha_{A,i})
, where
\alpha_{A,i}:(L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A)^{r}\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{{\rm pdR}}({\rm gr}_{i}{{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet}}W_{A})
.

Proposition 3.12
.

We have a natural morphism
\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\Box}\rightarrow\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}

of groupoids over
{\rm Art}_{E}
and an equivalence of groupoids
X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box}\cong X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\Box}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}}X^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\mathrm{ver}}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
over
{\rm Art}_{E}
. Moreover,
\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\Box}\rightarrow\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}
is relatively pro-representable.

Proof.

Choose
(A,W_{A},{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet},\iota_{A},\alpha_{A})\in X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}(A)
. By the proof of
[
12
, Lemma 6.1.1]
, the induced action of
\nu_{W_{A}}
on
W_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{F}}_{A,i})/W_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{F}}_{A,i-1})
gives the scalars
(\nu_{\epsilon_{A,1}},\cdots,\;\nu_{\epsilon_{A,k}})\in\prod_{i=1}^{k}X^{0,\Box}_{{\rm gr}_{i}{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
. This gives a natural morphism
\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\Box}\rightarrow\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}
. Fix
\eta_{A}:=(A,W_{A},{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet},\iota_{A},\underline{\alpha}_{A})\in X^{\mathrm{ver}}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}(A)
, where
\underline{\alpha}_{A}=(\alpha_{A,i})
with
\alpha_{A,i}:(L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A)^{r}\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{{\rm pdR}}({\rm gr}_{i}{{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet}})
. Denoted by
\widetilde{\eta_{A}}
the groupoid over
{\rm Art}_{E}
it represents. Then for each
A
-algebra
A^{\prime}
in
{\rm Art}_{E}
, the
A^{\prime}
-points of groupoid
(\widetilde{\eta_{A}}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}}X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\Box})(A^{\prime})
consists of objects
(A^{\prime},W_{A^{\prime}},{\mathbf{F}}_{A^{\prime},\bullet},\iota_{A^{\prime}},\underline{\alpha}_{A^{\prime}},\beta_{A^{\prime}},\alpha_{A^{\prime}})
, where
\beta_{A^{\prime}}:(W_{A}\otimes_{A}A^{\prime},{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet}\otimes_{A}A^{\prime},\iota_{A}\otimes 1,\underline{\alpha}_{A}\otimes 1)\rightarrow(A^{\prime},W_{A^{\prime}},{\mathbf{F}}_{A^{\prime},\bullet},\iota_{A^{\prime}},\underline{\alpha}_{A^{\prime}})
is a morphism in
X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}
and
\alpha_{A^{\prime}}:(L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A^{\prime})^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{{\rm pdR}}(W_{A^{\prime}})
such that
\underline{\alpha}_{A^{\prime}}
and
\alpha_{A^{\prime}}
are compatible (so that we can drop the data
\underline{\alpha}_{A^{\prime}}
since it is already determined by
\alpha_{A^{\prime}}
). Recall that we have the natural morphism
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r,L}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L},(g{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}\psi)\mapsto\overline{\mathrm{Ad}(g^{-1})\psi}
. Then the tuple
\underline{\alpha}_{A}
gives a point
z_{A}
in
{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}
. Let
(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}}_{r,L},y_{1}})_{z_{A}}
be the fiber of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}}_{r,L},y_{1}}
over
z_{A}
. Then the functor
|(\widetilde{\eta_{A}}\times_{X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}}X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\Box})|
is pro-represented by the completion of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}}_{r,L},y_{1}}
via the closed subspace
(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}}_{r,L},y_{1}})_{z_{A}}
. This completes the proof. ∎

We first deduce that

Proposition 3.13
.

The groupoids
X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}
and
\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}
over
{\rm Art}_{E}
are pro-representable. The functor
|X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}|
(resp.,
\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}
) is pro-represented by
a formally smooth noetherian complete local ring
R_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}
of residue field
E
.

Proof.

It is clear that
|X_{{\mathbf{F}}_{1},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}|\cong|X_{{\mathbf{F}}_{i}}^{\Box}|
is pro-represented by a formally smooth noetherian complete local ring
U_{1}
of residue field
E
and dimension
d_{L}
. Denoted by
V_{i}
the formal scheme pro-represents the functor
|X_{{\rm gr}_{i}{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{0,\Box}|
for
1\leq i\leq k
. Assume that
|X_{{\mathbf{F}}_{i-1},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}|
is pro-represented by
U_{i-1}
, where
U_{i-1}
is a formally smooth noetherian complete local ring of residue field
E
. Let
T_{i}
(resp.,
T^{\prime}_{i}
) denoted the completion of
U_{i-1}\otimes_{E}V_{i}
(resp.,
U_{i-1}\otimes_{E}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{L^{\times}},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}}
) with respect to the maximal ideal generated by the maximal ideal of
U_{i-1}
and the one of
V_{i}
. For any morphism
T_{i}\rightarrow A
with
A\in{\rm Art}_{E}
, let
{\mathbf{F}}_{A,i-1}
be the
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A
-representation given by the pull-back along
U_{i-1}\rightarrow T_{i}\rightarrow A
of the universal
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-representation-module over
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}U_{i-1}
and let
{\rm gr}_{i}{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet}
be the
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A
-representation given by the pull-back along
V_{i}\rightarrow T_{i}\rightarrow A
of the universal
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-representation-module over
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}V_{i}
. Let

\displaystyle W_{i}:=\varprojlim_{T_{i}\rightarrow A}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L}}\Big({\rm gr}_{i}{\mathbf{F}}_{A,\bullet},{\mathbf{F}}_{A,i-1}\Big),

(3.23)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle W}^{\prime}_{i}:=\varprojlim_{T^{\prime}_{i}\rightarrow A}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L}}\Big(W_{{\rm dR}}\big(\Delta_{\pi}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}(\delta_{A,i})\Big[\frac{1}{t}\Big]\big),{\mathbf{F}}_{A,i-1}\Big)

By definition,
[{\mathbf{F}}_{i}]
correspondences to a maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}
with residue field
E
of the gradded commutative symmetric
T_{i}
-algebra
\mathrm{Symm}_{T_{i}}W_{i}
and
\mathrm{Symm}_{T^{\prime}_{i}}W^{\prime}_{i}
. Let
U_{i}
(resp.,
U_{i}^{\prime}
) be the completion of
\mathrm{Symm}_{T_{i}}W_{i}
(resp.,
\mathrm{Symm}_{T^{\prime}_{i}}W^{\prime}_{i}
) at
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}
. Then
|X_{{\mathbf{F}}_{i},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}|
(resp.,
|\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{i},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}|
) is pro-represented by
U_{i}
(resp.,
U_{i}^{\prime}
). On the other hand, it is clear that
X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{k}X_{{\rm gr}_{i}{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
is formally smooth. Since
\prod_{i=1}^{k}X^{0,\Box}_{{\rm gr}_{i}{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
is formally smooth, we get that
X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}
is formally smooth. This shows that
|X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}|
is pro-represented by a formally smooth noetherian complete local ring of residue field
E
.
∎

We next show the pro-representablity of
\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\mathrm{ver}}
.

Proposition 3.14
.

The groupoid
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\mathrm{ver}}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
is pro-representable. The functor
|\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\mathrm{ver}}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}|
is pro-represented by a formally smooth noetherian complete local ring
R_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver},{\flat}}
of residue field
E
. Moreover, we have a natural ring homomorphism
\iota^{\flat}:R_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}\rightarrow R_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver},{\flat}}
.

Proof.

Keep the notation in the proof of Proposition
3.13
. The exact functor
W_{{\rm dR}}
induces a homomorphism of
S_{i}
-modules
W_{{\rm dR}}:N_{i}\rightarrow W_{i}^{\prime}
(see (
3.13
) and (
3.23
)). Let
W_{i}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}:=W_{{\rm dR}}(N_{i})
, it admits a natural
S_{i}
-module structure (and thus have a
T_{i}^{\prime}
-module structure). It is clear that
W_{i}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}
is a finitely generated module over
S_{i}
or
T_{i}^{\prime}
. Then
[{\mathbf{F}}_{i}]
corresponds to a maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}
with residue field
E
of the gradded commutative
T_{i}
-algebra
\mathrm{Symm}_{T_{i}}(W_{i}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)})
. We prove this proposition by induction on
k
. It is clear that
|\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{1},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\mathrm{ver}}|=|\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{1},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}|
is pro-represented
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{L^{\times}},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},1}}\cong E[[x_{1},\cdots,x_{d_{L}+1}]]
. If
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\mathrm{ver}}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{i-1},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
is already defined and is pro-represented by
U_{i}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\flat}
. Let
T_{i}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\flat}
denoted the completion of
U^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\flat}_{i-1}\otimes_{E}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{L^{\times}},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}}
with respect to the maximal ideal generated by the maximal ideal of
U^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\flat}_{i-1}
and the one of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{L^{\times}},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}}
(note that we have a natural homomorphism
T_{i}^{\prime}\rightarrow T_{i}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\flat}

). We see that
[{\mathbf{F}}_{i}]
correspondences to a maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}
with residue field
E
of the gradded commutative
T_{i}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\flat}
-algebra
\mathrm{Symm}_{T_{i}}(W^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\flat}_{i})
(by assumption, we see that the
T_{i}^{\prime}
-module structure
\mathrm{Symm}_{T_{i}}(W^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\flat}_{i})
factors through the natural homomorphism
T_{i}^{\prime}\rightarrow T_{i}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\flat}
, so
\mathrm{Symm}_{T_{i}^{\prime}}(W^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\flat}_{i})
has a
T_{i}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\flat}
-module structure). We let
U_{i}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\flat}
be the completion of
\mathrm{Symm}_{T_{i}^{\prime}}(W_{i}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)})
at
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}
. This pro-represents
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\mathrm{ver}}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{i},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
. This completes the induction. On the other hand, it is clear that
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{k}X_{{\rm gr}_{i}{{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}}
is formally smooth (similar to the proof of Lemma
3.9
), we see that the functor
|\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\mathrm{ver}}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}|
is pro-represented by a formally smooth noetherian complete local ring
R_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver},{\flat}}
.
∎

Remark 3.15
.

The above proposition is equivalent to the following facts via the module
W_{i}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}
. Note that

N_{i,g}:=\varprojlim_{S_{i}\rightarrow A}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma),g}\Big(\Delta_{\pi}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}(\delta_{A,i})\Big[\frac{1}{t}\Big],\mathcal{M}_{i-1,A}\Big).

(3.24)

is the kernel of the map
W_{{\rm dR}}:N_{i}\rightarrow W_{i}^{\prime}
. Recall the definition of
S_{i}
(resp,
T_{i}^{\prime}
) in the proof of Proposition
3.26
(resp., Proposition
3.13
). For any
J\subsetneqq\Sigma_{L}
, the formally smoothness of
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\mathrm{ver}}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}^{\mathrm{ver}}
has the following explanation. We put

W^{\prime}_{i,J}:=\varprojlim_{T_{i}^{\prime}\rightarrow A}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L}}\Big(W_{{\rm dR},J}\big(\Delta_{\pi}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}(\delta_{A,i})\Big[\frac{1}{t}\Big]\big),{\mathbf{F}}_{A,i-1,J}\Big)

(3.25)

Note that the
W_{{\rm dR},J}
-functor induces a map
N_{i}\rightarrow W^{\prime}_{i,J}
(recall (
3.13
)) of
S_{i}
-modules with kernel
N_{i,g,J}
. By Lemma
3.4
(3), we see that the map
N_{i}\rightarrow W^{\prime}_{i,J}
is surjective. Therefore, we obtain a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow N_{i,g,J}\rightarrow N_{i}\rightarrow W^{\prime}_{i,J}\rightarrow 0
of
S_{i}
-modules, where

N_{i,g,J}:=\varprojlim_{S_{i}\rightarrow A}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g,J}\Big(\Delta_{\pi}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}(\delta_{A,i})\Big[\frac{1}{t}\Big],\mathcal{M}_{i-1,A}\Big).\;

(3.26)

Via the natural projection
W^{\prime}_{i}\rightarrow W^{\prime}_{i,J}
, the image of
W_{i}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}=W_{{\rm dR}}(N_{i})
is equal to
W^{\prime}_{i,J}
.

Proposition 3.16
.

The groupoid
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
over
{\rm Art}_{E}
is pro-representable. The functor
|\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}|
is pro-represented by a formally smooth noetherian complete local ring
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{Y_{r,L},\widehat{y}_{1}}
of residue field
E
. Moreover, we have a natural homomorphism
\iota^{\flat}:\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{r,L},\widehat{y}_{1}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{Y_{r,L},\widehat{y}_{1}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{Y_{r,L},\widehat{y}_{1}}
is a formal power series over the ring
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{r,J},\widehat{y}_{1,J}}
for any
J\subsetneq\Sigma_{L}
.

Proof.

The first assertion follows form Proposition
3.12
and Proposition
3.14
. The second assertion follows from Proposition
3.14
and Proposition
3.10
.
∎

In Section
3.3.3
below, we want to explore the geometry of local models, i.e.
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\mathrm{ver}}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow{\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset}}\times X_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
through formal power series. These power formall series should mix
{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
-component (and its
\Sigma_{L}
-components) and
{\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset}}
-component (and its
\Sigma_{L}
-components). This phenomena is different from the previous generic (potentially) crystalline case, where the local models are established only though
{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
.

3.3.3
Description of local models through formal power series

By (
3.15
), we can choose the cocycle of
{\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,i-1}
and
W_{{\rm dR}}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,i-1})
in a functorial way, so that we can choose the universal cocycle of the universal
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules, this allows us to study the module
W_{i}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}
(see the proof of Proposition
3.14
). If we can find a basis of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules and the associated cohomology groups functorial in
A\in{\rm Art}_{E}
, then the universal cocycle and differential operator
\partial
can be written as matrix forms (we explain them below). This strategy is achieved in
[
60
]
for
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
case. This section is not necessary for our application, we just try our best to explain the structure of
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\mathrm{ver}}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
.
We first assume that
k=2
. For simplicity, we assume
r=1
. Let
\bm{\delta}_{i}={\rm unr}(\alpha q_{L}^{i-1})
for

i=1,2
. By the proof of Proposition
3.26
,
{X}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}
is pro-represented by the ring

R_{2}\cong E[[Y_{1},\{X_{1,\tau}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}},Y_{2},\{X_{2,\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}},\{U_{v}\}_{v\in\Sigma_{L}}]],

the
Y_{i},\{X_{i,\tau}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
correspond to the coordinates of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{L^{\times}},\bm{\delta}_{i}}
for
i=1,2
and
\{U_{v}\}_{1\leq v\leq d_{L}}
correspond to the extension parameters. Recall the argument after
[
37
, Theorem 5.3.26]
. Let
\mathcal{M}^{{\rm univ}}
be the universal
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
R_{2}
. Write
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{{\rm univ}}=\mathcal{M}^{{\rm univ}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}[1/t]}L_{\infty}((t))
for the scalar extension. Then the derivation of the
\Gamma
-action at
1
which gives a derivation

\partial^{{\rm univ}}_{\infty}:\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{{\rm univ}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{{\rm univ}}

above the fixed derivation
t\frac{d}{dt}
on
L_{\infty}((t))
. We can define the sub-
L_{\infty}
vector space
D_{{\rm pdR},\infty}(\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{{\rm univ}})
of
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{{\rm univ}}
such that the canonical map

D_{{\rm pdR},\infty}(\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{{\rm univ}})\otimes_{L_{\infty}}L_{\infty}((t))\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{{\rm univ}}

is an isomorphism. Let
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{{\rm univ}}=W_{{\rm dR}}(\mathcal{M}^{{\rm univ}})
be the corresponding universal
B_{{\rm dR}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}R_{2}
-representation of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
. Then
\partial^{{\rm univ}}_{\infty}
is compatible with the nilpotent operator
\partial^{{\rm univ}}
on
D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{{\rm univ}})
when modulo
t
and descent to
L
.

We remark that the arguments in
[
60
, Sections 5.1-5.4]
are also suitable for general
L
. Similar to the argument before
[
60
, Lemma 5.4.4, Proposition 5.6.2]
, by choosing a basis of the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules and the associated cohomology groups (functorial in
A\in{\rm Art}_{E}
and
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}(A)
), we see that
\mathcal{M}^{{\rm univ}}
corresponds to a universal cocycle
c_{12}\in R_{2}
(and thus get a matrix
H\in\mathrm{Mat}_{2\times 2}(R_{2})
of the
\partial^{{\rm univ}}_{\infty}
, see the result in
[
60
, Lemma 5.4.4]
). Since the universal cocycle
c_{12}
depends only on the quotient
\bm{\delta}_{1}{\bm{\delta}_{2}}^{-1}
, we get
c_{12}=F(Y,\{X_{\tau}\},\{U_{v}\})\in E[[Y,\{X_{\tau}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}},\{U_{v}\}_{v\in\Sigma_{L}}]]
, where
Y=Y_{1}-Y_{2}
and
X_{\tau}=X_{1,\tau}-X_{2,\tau}
. Moreover, the formal power series
F(Y,\{X_{\tau}\},\{U_{v}\})
is symmetric with respect to the subscripts
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
. The above discussion on universal cocycles can be generated to arbitrarily
k
and
r=1
easily.

Remark 3.17
.

If
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
, based on the Colmez’s computations on the cohomology of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules,
[
60
, Proposition 5.6.2]
shows that the universal cocyle
F(Y,X,U)\cong(YU)
. The author does not know how to explain
F(Y,\{X_{\tau}\},\{U_{v}\})
if
L\neq{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
.

Consider the ring
R_{2}^{\prime}:=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{L^{\times}},\bm{\delta}_{1}{\bm{\delta}_{2}}^{-1}}\cong E[[Y,\{X_{\tau}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}]]
. For any
R_{2}^{\prime}\rightarrow A
, by
[
17
, Lemma 3.3.6, Lemma 3.3.7]
, we see that (induced by the
D_{{\rm pdR}}(-)
-functor)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{1}\Big({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}(\delta_{A})\Big)\cong\coker({\rm wt}(\delta_{A})-{\rm wt}(\bm{\delta}_{1}{\bm{\delta}_{2}}^{-1}):A\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}L\rightarrow A\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}L).\;

(3.27)

Let
F_{\tau}(X_{\tau})\in E[[Y,\{X_{\tau}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}]]
be the element which corresponds to the universal
\tau
-weight. Similar to the proof of
[
60
, Lemma 5.6.1]
,
F_{\tau}(X_{\tau})\in E[[X_{\tau}]]
is a uniformizer.

Lemma 3.18
.

As a
R_{2}^{\prime}
-module, we have an isomorphism induced by the
D_{{\rm pdR}}(-)
-functor:

\displaystyle W_{2}^{\prime}:

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{R^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow A}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}\Big({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}(\delta_{A})\Big)

(3.28)

\displaystyle\cong\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}E[[Y_{\tau},\{X_{\sigma,\tau}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}]]=R_{2}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}L/(\{X_{\tau,\tau}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}),\;

where we add the subscript
\tau
to
Y,X_{\sigma}
to indicate that they lies in
\tau
-component. In particular,
W_{2,\tau}^{\prime}\cong E[[Y_{\tau},\{X_{\sigma,\tau}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}]]
. Therefore,
W_{2,\tau}^{\prime}
vanishs in the closed subspace
V(Y,\{X_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\backslash\{\tau\}})
of
R_{2}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We have the following short exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\varprojlim_{R^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow A}A\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}L\xrightarrow{v_{R^{\prime}_{2}}}\varprojlim_{R^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow A}A\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}L\rightarrow W^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow 0,

where
v_{R^{\prime}_{2}}:=\varprojlim_{R^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow A}{\rm wt}(\delta_{A})=\big(F_{\tau}(X_{\tau})\big)_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
. Hence,
W^{\prime}_{2}\cong E[[Y,\{X_{\tau}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}]]\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}L/\big(F_{\tau}(X_{\tau})\big)_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\cong\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}E[[Y_{\tau},\{X_{\sigma,\tau}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}]]
. This completes the proof.
∎

Recall that

N_{2,\ast}:=\varprojlim_{R^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow A}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}\Big({\rm Gal}_{L},R_{A,L}(\delta_{A})\Big)

for
\ast\in\{\emptyset,g\}
. Note that
N_{2}
is a free
R_{2}^{\prime}
-module of rank
d_{L}
. Recall that
W_{2}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}\cong N_{2}/N_{2,g}
as
R_{2}^{\prime}
-module and it is a submodule of
W_{2}^{\prime}
. Let
\big\langle\big(F_{i}(Y_{\tau},X_{\sigma,\tau})\big)_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\big\rangle_{i\in I}
be the generated elements of
W_{2}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}
(as a
R_{2}^{\prime}
-submodule of
W_{2}^{\prime}
). Then Remark
3.15
translates into

Proposition 3.19
.

Assume
L\neq{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
and
J\subsetneq\Sigma_{L}
, then
\big\langle\big(F_{i}(Y_{\tau},X_{\sigma,\tau})\big)_{\tau\in J}\big\rangle_{i\in I}
is equal to the unit ideal of
\prod_{\tau\in J}E[[Y_{\tau},\{X_{\sigma,\tau}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}]]
.

3.4
The case of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules and Galois representations

In this section, we assume
L\neq{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. Consider the scheme
\mathcal{X}_{r,L}:=Y_{r,L}\times_{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{r,L}}X_{L}
, and
\widehat{y}:=(\widehat{y}_{1},y_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{r,L}
(for
J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
, we have the corresponding
J
-component
\mathcal{X}_{r,J}
and the point
\widehat{y}_{J}
). Define formal scheme
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,L,\widehat{y}}:=\widehat{Y}_{r,L,\widehat{y_{1}}}^{\flat}\times_{\widehat{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}}_{r,L,y_{1}}}\widehat{X}_{r,L,y}
. We have natural morphism
\iota^{\flat}:\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,L,\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{r,L,\widehat{y}}
. For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
. let
\mathcal{X}_{r,w}:=Y_{r,L}\times_{{{{\mathfrak{g}}}}_{r,L}}X_{r,w}=\mathcal{X}_{r,L}\times_{X_{r,L}}X_{r,w}
. Consider the formal completion
\widehat{X}_{r,w,y}
, it is empty if
y\not\in{X}_{r,w}(E)
. We put
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,w,y}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,L,\widehat{y}}\times_{\widehat{X}_{r,L,y}}\widehat{X}_{r,w,y}
. Denoted by
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},\widehat{y}}
(resp.,
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,w},\widehat{y}}
) the ring pro-represent

|\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,L,\widehat{y}}|
(resp.,
|\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,w,\widehat{y}}|
).

Put
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}:={X}^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\times_{\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}}\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
and
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}:=\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\times_{|X^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}|}\widehat{X}^{\flat}_{w,y}
for
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
. Similar to
[
12
, Proposition 6.3.2, Corollary 6.3.3, Proposition 6.3.4]
, we see that

Proposition 3.20
.

We have the following facts.

(a)

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},\widehat{y}}
is a noetherian complete local ring of residue field
E
and has a finite number of irreducible components. Moreover, we have a natural homomorphism
\iota^{\flat}:\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},\widehat{y}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},\widehat{y}}
is a formal power series over the ring
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,J},\widehat{y}_{J}}
for any
J\subsetneq\Sigma_{L}
.

(b)

The groupoid
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
(resp.,
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
) over
{\rm Art}_{E}
is pro-representable and the functor
|X^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}|
(resp.,
|\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}|
) is pro-represented by
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,L,\widehat{y}}
(resp.,
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,w,\widehat{y}}
).

(c)

The morphism of groupoids
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
,
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
are relatively representable and are closed immersions.

For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, let
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
be the image of
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
through the forgetful morphism
\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}
. It is clear that

\displaystyle X^{w}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\cong X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\times_{\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}}\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}},\;X^{\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\cong X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\times_{\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}}\widehat{X}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}.

(3.29)

Let
l_{{\mathbf{D}}}:=k+d_{L}\big(k+\frac{n(n-r)}{2}\big)-\dim_{E}\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
. Similar to
[
12
, Corollary 6.3.5]
, we have

Proposition 3.21
.

For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, the groupoid
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
(resp.,
X^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
) over
{\rm Art}_{E}
is pro-representable. The functor
|X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|
is pro-represented by a formal scheme which is formally smooth of relative dimension
l_{{\mathbf{D}}}
over
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,L,\widehat{y}}
(resp.,
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,w,\widehat{y}}
).

Proof.

By Proposition
3.26
and Corollary
3.8
and base change.
∎

As in Definition
2.1
, in the sequel, we fix a
p
-adic potentially semistable non-crystalline Galois representation
\rho_{L}:{\rm Gal}_{L}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(E)
which admits a special
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration with parameter
({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L}}
, (resp.,  with parameter
(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,{\mathbf{h}}},\widetilde{\bm{\delta}}_{\mathbf{h}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
).

Let
w_{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathscr{W}^{\Delta_{n}^{k},\emptyset}_{n,L,\max}
measuring the relative position of the two flags
(\alpha^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}),\alpha^{-1}({\rm Fil}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},\bullet}))
, i.e., it lies in the
{\rm GL}_{n,L}
-orbit of
(1,w_{\mathcal{F}})
in
{\rm GL}_{n,L}/{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset,L}\times{\rm GL}_{n,L}/{\mathbf{B}}_{L}
. We put
\mathcal{S}(y):=\{w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}:y\in{X}_{r,w}(E)\}=\{w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}:\widehat{X}_{r,w,y}\neq 0\}=\{w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}:X^{w}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\neq 0\}
.

The map

\kappa:X_{r,L}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}_{r,L}
induces a morphism
\widehat{X}_{r,L,y}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{r,L,(0,0)}
, thus the pullback
\kappa:\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{r,L,\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}_{r,L,(0,0)}
. Denoted by
\Theta
the composition:

X^{\Box}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{X}^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{r,L,\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}_{r,L,(0,0)}

which factors through the morphism
X^{\Box}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
still denoted by
\Theta:X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}_{r,L,(0,0)}
.

The main proposition of this section is given as follows.

Proposition 3.22
.

Assume
L\neq{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. We have the following facts.

(a)

The groupoid
X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
(resp.
X^{w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
for
w\in\mathcal{S}(y)
) over
{\rm Art}_{E}
is pro-representable. The functor
|X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|
(resp.,
|X^{w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|
) is pro-represented by a ring
R_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
(resp.,
R^{w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
) of residue field
E
and dimension
n^{2}+d_{L}\big(k+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}\big)
.

(b)

For
w\in\mathcal{S}(y)
, the ring
R^{w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
is irreducible and Cohen-Macaulay. The ring
R_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
is equidimensional and
R^{w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\cong R_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}/{\mathfrak{p}}_{w}
for a minimal prime ideal
{\mathfrak{p}}_{w}
of
R_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
. The map
w\mapsto{\mathfrak{p}}_{w}
is a bijection between
\mathcal{S}(y)
and the set of minimal prime ideals of
R_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
. Moreover, the dimension of
X^{w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
is equal to

n^{2}-n^{2}d_{L}+l_{{\mathbf{D}}}+\dim_{E}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{w,y}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
.

(c)

The morphism
|X^{w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|\hookrightarrow|X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|\xrightarrow{\Theta}\mathcal{T}_{r,L,(0,0)}
of groupoids over
{\rm Art}_{E}
factors through
\mathcal{T}_{r,w,(0,0)}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{r,(0,0)}
if and only if
\mathscr{W}_{\Delta_{n}^{k},L}w^{\prime}=\mathscr{W}_{\Delta_{n}^{k},L}w
. This implies that if
X^{w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\neq 0
, then
w\underline{w}_{0}\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
.

Proof.

Note that
X_{\rho_{L}}\rightarrow X_{{\mathbf{D}}}
is relatively representable and formally smooth of relative dimension
n^{2}
, so is the morphism
X^{\Box}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
. Then we get that

\displaystyle\dim_{E}R_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}

\displaystyle=n^{2}+d_{L}\big(n^{2}+k+\frac{n(n-r)}{2}\big)-\dim_{E}\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})+\dim_{E}\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})-n^{2}d_{L}

(3.30)

\displaystyle=n^{2}+d_{L}\big(k+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}\big).

We prove
(b)
by applying the same argument in the proof of
[
12
, Theorem 6.4.1, Proposition 6.4.3]
and using our Proposition
3.10
. In precise, for each
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
, we see that
X^{\Box,w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
is formally smooth over
X^{\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},\{\tau\}}
. Recall that
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{r,w_{\tau}},{y}_{\tau}}
is irreducible, we get that
\Spec R^{\Box,w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
is irreducible too, where
R^{\Box,w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
is the ring pro-represents the functor

|X^{\Box,w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|
. On the other hand, the relative dimension of
X^{\Box,w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
over
X^{\Box,w}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},\{\tau\}}
is equal to the relative dimension of
X^{\Box}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
over
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},\{\tau\}}
. Since
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{r,L},y}
is equidimensional, we deduce that
R_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
is equidimensional too. Part
(c)
also follows easily from
[
12
, Lemma 5.2.7]
and the following argument. Let
\mathcal{P}
be a geometric property of
X_{r}
(resp.,
X_{r,w}
for
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
) that stable under a formally smooth morphism. Then
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,L,\widehat{y}}
(resp.,
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,w,\widehat{y}}
, where we view them as schemes) inherit the geometric property
\mathcal{P}
since
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,L,\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{r,w_{\tau}},{y}_{\tau}}
(resp.,
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,w,\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{r,w_{\tau}},{y}_{\tau}}
) is formally smooth.
∎

Remark 3.23
.

Since
X^{\Box,w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
is also formally smooth over
\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box,w}
, by the proof we see that

\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box,w}\cong|\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{+},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{(\varphi,\Gamma),\Box,w}|
is irreducible, i.e., the scheme
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,w},\widehat{y}}
is irreducible. Therefore,
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},y}
is equidimensional and there is a bijection between irreducible components of
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},y}
and
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},y}
. We suspect that
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},y}
and
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},y}
have the same dimension, and the natural morphism
\iota^{\flat}:\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},y}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},y}
maps the generic point of
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},y}
to the generic point of
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},y}
(in particular,
\iota^{\flat}
is dominant).

Remark 3.24
.

If
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
, the same results as in Proposition
3.22
and the conjecture in
3.23
can be deduced from
[
60
, Theroem 5.6.5]
. The results in
[
60
, Theroem 5.6.5]
indicate an explicit geometric description of
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{y}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{w,y}
. Moreover, the morphism
\iota^{\flat}:\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},y}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},y}
should induced by a composition of blow-ups and open immersions of some algebraic schemes. The author use a calculation on the explicit basis of
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}(\delta))
done by Colmez to study the universal cocycle and the universal derivation. The author do not know how to generate these methods to
L\neq{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-case.

Remark 3.25
.

Our method are suitable for any potentially semistable Galois representation
\rho_{L}
which admits a general
\Omega
-filtration with arbitrarily parameters (with some mild regularity assumptions).

3.4.1
Flatness

Assume
L\neq{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. Write
\kappa_{1}:=\kappa_{{\mathbf{P}}_{r,\emptyset}}
and
\kappa_{2}:=\kappa_{{\mathbf{B}}}
for simplicity. This section aims to prove the flatness of the natural morphism
\kappa_{i}:\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,L},\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{t}}_{L},0}
and
\kappa_{w,i}:\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{r,{w}},\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{t}}_{L},0}
(such results are motivated by the previous local models on generic (potentially) crystalline case and the
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-semistable case in
[
60
, Proposition 2.3.6]
(we remark that such results are closely related to the flatness of the weight map of trianguline variety at Steinberg point). The main tool is miracle flatness
[
58
, Tag 00R3]
.

Proposition 3.26
.

The natural morphism
X^{\Box}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}
is flat, and thus
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{Y_{r,L},\widehat{y}_{1}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L},0}
is flat.

Proof.

Firstly, we prove that
X^{\mathrm{ver}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}
is flat. Let
R^{\mathrm{ver}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
be the formally smooth noetherian complete local ring pro-represents the
X^{\Box}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
. Let
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}}
be the maximal ideal of the complete local ring
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L},0}
. Then the ring
R^{\Box}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}/{\mathfrak{m}}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}}
pro-represents the subgroupoid
X^{\mathrm{ver}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet},0}
of

X^{\mathrm{ver}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
consists of object
(A,\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet},j_{A},\underline{\beta}_{A})\in X^{\mathrm{ver}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(A)
such that
{\rm wt}_{\tau}(\delta_{A,i})={\rm wt}_{\tau}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i})
. We show that
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet},0}^{\mathrm{ver}}|
is pro-representable by a formally smooth noetherian complete local ring with dimension
k+d_{L}\frac{n(n-r)}{2}
. It is clear that
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}},1},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet},0}^{\mathrm{ver}}|
is pro-represented by
E[[x]]
. Now assume that
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}},i-1},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}|
is pro-represented by a formally smooth noetherian complete local ring
R_{i-1}
of residue field
E
and dimension
i-1+d_{L}r^{2}\frac{i(i-1)}{2}
. Let
S_{i}
denote the completion of
R_{i-1}\otimes_{E}E[[x]]
with respect to the maximal ideal generated by the maximal ideal of
R_{i-1}
and the one of
E[[x]]
. For any morphism
S_{i}\rightarrow A
with
A\in{\rm Art}_{E}
, let
\mathcal{M}_{i-1,A}
be the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}[1/t]
given by the pull-back along
R_{i-1}\rightarrow S_{i}\rightarrow A
of the universal
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{R_{i-1},L}[1/t]
and let
\delta_{A,i}
be the character
L^{\times}\rightarrow E[[x]]\rightarrow S_{i}\rightarrow A
. Put
N_{i}:=\varprojlim_{S_{i}\rightarrow A}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}\Big(\Delta_{\pi}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}\delta_{A,i})\Big[\frac{1}{t}\Big],\mathcal{M}_{i-1,A}\Big).

Then
N_{i}
is a free
S_{i}
-module of rank
(i-1)d_{L}r^{2}
by Lemma
3.1
. Then
[\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}},i}]
correspondences to a maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}
with residue field
E
of the polynomial
S_{i}
-algebra
\mathrm{Symm}_{S_{i}}N_{i}^{\vee}
. Let
R_{i}
be the completion of
\mathrm{Symm}_{S_{i}}N_{i}^{\vee}
at
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}
. One can check that
X^{\Box}_{\mathcal{M}_{i},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet},0}
is pro-represented by
R_{i}
. In particular,
X^{\mathrm{ver}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet},0}
is pro-represented by a formally smooth noetherian complete local ring of residue field
E
and dimension
k+d_{L}\frac{n(n-r)}{2}
. Therefore,
R^{\mathrm{ver}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}/{\mathfrak{m}}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}}
has co-dimension
d_{L}k=\dim\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L},0}
in
R^{\mathrm{ver}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
. By the miracle flatness, we see that
X^{\mathrm{ver}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}
is flat. By the proof of
[
17
, Lemma 2.3.1]
and
[
17
, Lemma 2.3.2]
, we see that the morphism
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{L}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{t}}_{L},(g{\mathbf{B}}_{L},\psi)\mapsto\overline{\mathrm{Ad}(g^{-1})\psi}
is flat. By the proof of Proposition
3.12
, the natural morphism
\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\Box}\rightarrow\widehat{X}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{ver}}
is flat. This deduces that
X^{\Box}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset,L}
is also flat.
∎

Conjecture 3.27
.

Assume
r=1
. The natural morphism
X^{\Box}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
(resp.,
X^{\Box,w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
) is flat. Thus
\kappa_{i}:\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L},\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{t}}_{L},0}
(resp.,
\kappa_{w,i}:\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{{w}},\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{t}}_{L},0}
) is flat.

Proposition 3.28
.

Assume that
r=1
, the natural morphism
X^{\Box,\underline{w}_{0}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
is flat, equivalently ,
\kappa_{\underline{w}_{0},i}:\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{\underline{w}_{0}},\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{t}}_{L},0}
is flat.

Proof.

Note that
X^{\underline{w}_{0}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
is Cohen-Macaulay by Proposition
3.22
. Every deformation in
R^{\underline{w}_{0}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}/{\mathfrak{m}}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}}
is semistable by
[
2
, Proposition 2.3.4]
. But each semistable deformation
\rho_{A}
of
\rho_{L}
gives a natural triangulation
\mathcal{M}_{\bullet,A}
. Indeed, we can use the arguments before
[
7
, Theorem 3.3.4]
, let
\rho_{A}\in\widehat{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\overline{r},\underline{w}_{0},\rho_{L}}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(A)
for
A\in{\rm Art}_{E}
, and if
M
is a
(\varphi,N)
-stable subspace of
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho_{A})
, then
(\mathcal{R}_{A,L}[\log(X),1/t]\otimes_{A}M)^{N=0}\cap D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{A})
(resp.,
(\mathcal{R}_{A,L}[\log(X),1/t]\otimes_{A}M)^{N=0}
) is a sub-
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of

D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{A})
(resp.,
D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{A})[1/t]
) which has the same rank as
M
. We obtain the desired triangulation by using this argument step by step. Thus we can identity
R^{\underline{w}_{0}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}/{\mathfrak{m}}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}}
with
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\underline{w}_{0}}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}},\rho_{L}}
, which has dimension
n^{2}+d_{L}\frac{n(n-1)}{2}
(and thus has co-dimension
d_{L}n
in
R^{\underline{w}_{0}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
). By the miracle flatness, we finally deduce that
X^{\underline{w}_{0}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
is flat and thus
\kappa_{\underline{w}_{0},i}:\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{\underline{w}_{0}},\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{t}}_{L},0}
is flat.
∎

Remark 3.29
.

For
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
case, Conjecture
3.27
are obtained in
[
60
, Proposition 2.3.6]
if we consider the local models given in
[
60
, Theorem 5.6.5]
.

Remark 3.30
.

We also have projections
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset},0}
and
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,w},\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset},0}
, which are induced by the composition
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,L},\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{Y_{r,L},\widehat{y}_{1}}\xrightarrow{{\rm pr}_{1}}{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset}
, where
{\rm pr}_{1}:Y_{r}={\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset}\times{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}}_{r,L}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{z}}_{r,\emptyset}
. They are not necessary flat.

4
Local applications

We drive several local consequences of the results of local models: further properties of Bernstein paraboline varieties (in particular, trianguline variety), existence of local companion points, and a locally analytic “Breuil-Mézard type” statement for Steinberg case.

4.1
Local geometry of Bernstein paraboline varieties at special point

We now recall the definition of Bernstein paraboline varieties
[
12
, Section 4.2]
. Let
\overline{r}:{\rm Gal}_{L}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(k_{E})
be a continuous group morphism. The Bernstein paraboline variety
{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
of type
(\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},{\mathbf{h}})
is a subspace of
\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
. It contains a subspace
U^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})
consists of the point
(\rho,\underline{x},{\delta^{0}})
such that

(1)

(\underline{x},{\delta^{0}})\in\big({\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}\big)^{{\rm gen}}
(the set of generic points in
{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
, see
[
12
, Section 4.2]
),

(2)

D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho)
admits an
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho)
such that

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho)\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{k(x),L}}\mathcal{R}_{k(x),L}((\delta_{i}^{0})^{-1}_{\varpi_{L}})\hookrightarrow\Delta_{x_{i}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{k(x),L}}\mathcal{R}_{k(x),L}(z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{ir}})

(4.1)

and the image has Hodge-Tate weights
({\mathbf{h}}_{(i-1)r+1},\cdots,{\mathbf{h}}_{ir})
.

Then
{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
is the Zariski-closure of
U^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})
in
\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
. By
[
12
, Theorem 4.2.5, Corollary 4.2.5]
, we have:

Proposition 4.1
.

(1)

The rigid space
{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
is equidimensional of dimension
n^{2}+\left(\frac{n(n-1)}{2}+k\right)d_{L}
.

(2)

The set
U^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})
is Zariski-open and Zariski-dense in
{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
, and is smooth over
E
.

(3)

Let
x=(\rho_{x},\underline{\pi}_{x},{\delta^{0}})\in{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
, then
D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{x})
admits an
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}=\{{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{x})\}
such that, for all
1=1,\cdots,s
,

{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{x})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{k(x),L}}\mathcal{R}_{k(x),L}((\delta_{i}^{0})^{-1}_{\varpi_{L}})\Big[\frac{1}{t}\Big]=\Delta_{x_{i}}\Big[\frac{1}{t}\Big].

Remark 4.2
.

(Comparison with trianguline variety) If
r=1
(so
k=n
), we have
{\mathbf{L}}_{1,\emptyset}={\mathbf{T}}
and
{\mathbf{P}}_{1,\emptyset}={\mathbf{B}}
. Let
\widehat{T}_{L}
denote the character space of
{\mathbf{T}}(L)
over
E
, i.e., the rigid space over
E
parameterizing continuous character of
{\mathbf{T}}(L)
. Then
[
12
, Remark 4.2.4]
show that
{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
coincides with trianguline variety
X^{\Box}_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
of
[
16
, Section 2.2]
, by using the isomorphism

\iota_{\Omega_{1}^{\otimes n},{\mathbf{h}}}:\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{1}^{\otimes n}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}\times\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{1,\emptyset},\mathcal{O}_{L}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\widehat{T}_{L},(\underline{\pi}_{x},{\delta^{0}})\mapsto(\boxtimes_{i=1}^{r}\pi_{x_{i}}){\delta^{0}}z^{{\mathbf{h}}}.

As in
[
17
, Section 3.7]
, we will drop the
\Box
in
X^{\Box}_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
in Section
5
in order to avoid any confusion with the other kind of framing used in local models.

Let
\rho_{L}
be a Galois representation which admits a special
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration (i.e., We keep the situation in Proposition
3.22
). Suppose that
x=(\rho_{L},\underline{\pi}_{x},{\delta^{0}})
appears on the
{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
. We have a natural morphism of formal schemes (recall that
(\widehat{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}})_{\rho_{L}}
is equivalent to
X_{\rho_{L}}\cong|X_{\rho_{L}}|
)

{\widehat{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}}(\overline{r})_{x}}\rightarrow(\widehat{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}})_{\rho_{L}}\cong X_{\rho_{L}}.

By the argument before
[
12
, Proposition 6.4.6]
, there exists
w_{x}=(w_{x,\tau})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\in\mathscr{W}^{\Delta_{n}^{k},\emptyset}_{n,L}
such that, for
1\leq j\leq n
,
{\mathbf{h}}_{\tau,w_{x,\tau}^{-1}(j)}={\rm wt}_{\tau}(\chi_{i})+{\mathbf{h}}_{\tau,j}
where
i
is the integer such that
(i-1)r<j\leq ir
.

The proof of
[
12
, Proposition 6.4.5, Proposition 6.4.6, Corollary 6.4.7, Corollary 6.4.8]
are also suitable for our case. By an easy variation of the above proofs, we deduce from Proposition
3.22
:

Proposition 4.3
.

Assume
L\neq{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. We have

(a)

The canonical morphism
{\widehat{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}}(\overline{r})_{x}}\rightarrow X_{\rho_{L}}
factors through a morphism

{\widehat{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}}(\overline{r})_{x}}\rightarrow X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}.

(b)

The morphism
{\widehat{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}}(\overline{r})_{x}}\rightarrow X_{\rho_{L}}
and
{\widehat{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}}(\overline{r})_{x}}\rightarrow X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
are closed immmersions.

(c)

Denote by
\Theta_{x}:{\widehat{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}}(\overline{r})_{x}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{r,L,(0,0)}
the composition of fomall schemes:

{\widehat{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}}(\overline{r})_{x}}\hookrightarrow X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\xrightarrow{\Theta}\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{r,L,(0,0)}.

Then morphism
\Theta_{x}
factors through
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{r,w_{x}\underline{w}_{0},(0,0)}
.

(d)

The closed immersion
{\widehat{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}}(\overline{r})_{x}}\rightarrow X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
in
(2)
factors through an isomorphism of groupoids over
{\rm Art}_{E}
:

{\widehat{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}}(\overline{r})_{x}}\xrightarrow{\sim}X^{w_{x}\underline{w}_{0}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}.

Then
w_{x}\underline{w}_{0}\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
. In particular,
{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
is irreducible
and Cohen-Macaulay at the point
x
.

Remark 4.4
.

In the case
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
, see
[
60
, Proposition 6.2.7, Corollary 6.2.9,Theorem 6.2.10]
for related results. The author also shows that trianguline variety is normal and Cohen-Macaulay at the semistable point
x
.

4.2
Local companion points

In the remainder of this section, we restrict to the case
r=1
. By Remark
4.2
, the corresponding point of
(\rho_{L},\underline{\pi}_{x},{\delta^{0}})\in X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{[1,n]},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})
via the isomorphism
X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{[1,n]},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})\xrightarrow{\sim}X^{\Box}_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
is
x=(\rho_{L},\chi)\in X^{\Box}_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
with
\chi:=\iota_{\Omega_{[1,n]},{\mathbf{h}}}(\underline{\pi}_{x},{\delta^{0}})\in\widehat{T}_{L}
.

Recall that
\rho_{L}
is a semistable non-crystalline
p
-adic Galois representation with full monodromy rank, i.e., the monodromy operator
N
on
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho_{L})
satisfies
N^{n-1}\neq 0
. Let
{\mathbf{h}}:=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,1}>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,2}>\cdots>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,n})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
be the Hodge-Tate weights of
\rho_{L}
. There is a unique
{\alpha}\in E^{\times}
such that
{\alpha}
,
{\alpha q_{L}^{1}},\cdots,{\alpha q_{L}^{n-1}}
are
\varphi^{f_{L}}
-eigenvalues of
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho_{L})
. Put
{\rm unr}(\alpha)_{n}:=({\rm unr}({\alpha}),{\rm unr}({\alpha q_{L}^{1}}),\;\cdots,\;{\rm unr}({\alpha q_{L}^{n-1}}))
. Then
D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{L})
admits a triangulation
\mathcal{F}
with parameters
({\rm unr}(\alpha)_{n})\cdot z^{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}})}
for
w_{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathscr{W}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{n,L,\max}\cong\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
. Note that
\rho_{L}
is non-critical if
w_{\mathcal{F}}=w_{0}
.

Denote by
{{\mathbf{h}}}_{i}=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
for
1\leq i\leq n
. For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, we put
x_{w\underline{w}_{0}}:=(\rho_{L},({\rm unr}(\alpha)_{n})\cdot z^{w\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}})})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times\widehat{T}_{L}
. In particular, we write
x:=x_{1}
. The goal of this section is to show that
\{x_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\}_{w\leq w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}
are local companion points of
x
, i.e.,
x_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r}
) for
w\leq w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}
, under some mild hypothesis on trianguline variety.

For
\rho^{\prime}_{L}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
, there exists a unique
a_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}}\in k(\rho^{\prime}_{L})
such that
a_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}},\;\cdots\,\;{a_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}}q_{L}^{i-1}},\;\cdots\,\;,{a_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}}q_{L}^{n-1}}
are
\varphi^{f_{L}}
-eigenvalues of
(\varphi,N)
-module
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho^{\prime}_{L})
. Consider the following morphisms of rigid spaces over
E
:

\displaystyle\iota_{{\mathbf{h}}}:{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times\widehat{T}_{L}

(4.2)

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}_{L}

\displaystyle\mapsto(\rho^{\prime}_{L},z^{{\mathbf{h}}}{\rm unr}(a_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}})_{n}).\;

and

\displaystyle\iota_{w,{\mathbf{h}}}:{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times\widehat{T}_{L}

(4.3)

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}_{L}

\displaystyle\mapsto(\rho^{\prime}_{L},z^{w({\mathbf{h}})}{\rm unr}(a_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}})_{n}).\;

By definition, we have
\iota_{{\mathbf{h}}}=\iota_{1,{\mathbf{h}}}
. Moreover, for
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, let
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})
be the inverse image of
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
via
\iota_{{\mathbf{h}},w}
, which is a closed subspace of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
.

For any
\rho^{\prime}_{L}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
, by the argument in Section
2.2
, the unique
(\varphi,N)
-stable complete flag on
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho^{\prime}_{L})
determine a element
w_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}}\in\mathscr{W}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{n,L,\max}\cong\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
. Then
\rho^{\prime}_{L}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
if and only
w_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}}\underline{w}_{0}=w
. Put
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})={\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})\cap{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
.

Hypothesis 4.5
.

For any
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
,
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})
is equal to
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
.

Remark 4.6
.

This hypothesis is predicted by
[
37
, Conjecture 5.3.13]
and
[
37
, Remark 5.3.5]
. Indeed, for the generic crystalline case (see
[
17
]
), such hypothesis hold automatically (since
\iota_{w,{\mathbf{h}}}(\rho_{L})\in U_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
, for generic
\rho_{L}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\max},w}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
). The author believes that such Hypothesis
4.5
(i.e. Steinberg case) is also true.

Then Proposition
2.4
gives:

Proposition 4.7
.

For any
w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, we put
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},(w,w^{\prime})}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})={\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})\cap{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w^{\prime}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
.

(1)

We have
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},(w,w^{\prime})}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})=0
if
w<w^{\prime}
.

(2)

The Zariski-closure
\overline{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})}\subseteq{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w\underline{w}_{0}}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})
. We have

\overline{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})}=\coprod_{w^{\prime}\leq w}\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},(w,w^{\prime})}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r}),

where the Zariski-closure is taken in
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})
.

(3)

Assume Hypothesis
4.5
. Then we have

\overline{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})}=\coprod_{w^{\prime}\leq w}\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w^{\prime}}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r}),

where the Zariski-closure is taken in
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})
. In particular,
\rho^{\prime}_{L}\in\overline{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})}
if and only
w_{\rho^{\prime}_{L}}\underline{w}_{0}\leq w
.

Proof.

Part
(1)
follows from Proposition
4.3

(d)
. We use the same strategy as in the proof of Proposition
2.4
to prove Part
(2)
. We get that

{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}\times_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}}{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})\rightarrow\big((\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\rm GL}_{n,L}/\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{B}})\times_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E\big)^{{\rm rig}}

is also an open morphism. By the similar argument as in the proof of Proposition
2.4
(i.e. descent along the map
{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
), we deduce from the identity in Part
(2)
. Part
(3)
is a direct consequence of Proposition
2.4
, if Hypothesis
4.5
holds.
∎

Definition 4.8
.

A point
(\rho_{L}^{\prime\prime},(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
is called a local companion point of
(\rho_{L}^{\prime},(\delta^{\prime}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
if
\rho_{L}^{\prime\prime}=\rho_{L}^{\prime}
and
\delta^{\prime\prime}_{i}/\delta^{\prime}_{i}
is algebraic.

Therefore, if
x^{\prime}=(\rho_{L},(\delta^{\prime}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
is a local companion point of
x
, then there exists a
w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
such that
{\rm wt}(\delta)=w^{\prime}({\mathbf{h}})
. Thus each companion point of
x
is of the form
x_{w}:=(\rho_{L},{\rm unr}(\alpha)_{n}z^{w({\mathbf{h}})})
for some
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
. The following proposition is an analogue of
[
17
, Theorem 4.2.3]
(but in our setting).

Proposition 4.9
.

Assume Hypothesis
4.5
(so that
x_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
). Then
x_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
if and only if
w_{\mathcal{F}}\leq w
(in partcular, they are local companion points of
x
and
x_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}
).

Proof.

By Proposition
4.7
, we have an injection
\iota_{w\underline{w}_{0},{\mathbf{h}}}:\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w\underline{w}_{0}}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})\rightarrow X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
of rigid spaces over
L
. By Proposition
4.7
, we see that
\overline{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w\underline{w}_{0}}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})}\subseteq\iota_{w\underline{w}_{0},{\mathbf{h}}}^{-1}(X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r}))
. Since
x_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
and
w_{\mathcal{F}}\leq w
, the result follows.
∎

Remark 4.10
.

If
x_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}\in{X}_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
. Let
\widetilde{X}_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})\subseteq{X}_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
be the subspace defined before
[
15
, Conjeture 2.8]
, then
[
16
, Conjecture 3.23]
(or
[
15
, Conjeture 5.6]
, modular lifting theorem) shows that there exists a point
z\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}
such that
(\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(x_{w\underline{w}_{0}}),z)\in\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\big(X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})\big)\times{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}
are in
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})(E)
. Then
[
15
, Theorem 5.5]
gives the existence of all global companion points, i.e.,
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})(E)
if and only if
w\leq w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}
.

4.3
Galois cycles

We construct certain cycles on the deformation space
X_{\rho_{L}}\cong(\widehat{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}})_{\rho_{L}}
. We follow the notation in
[
12
, Section 6.5]
. We denote by
Z(\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}})
(resp.,
Z^{d}(\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}})
, resp.,
Z^{\leq d}(\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}})
) for the free abelian group generated by the irreducible closed subschemes (resp., the irreducible closed subschemes of codimension
d
, resp., the irreducible closed subschemes of codimension
\leq d
) in
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}}
. If
A
is a noetherian complete local ring which is a quotient of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}}
, we set

[\Spec A]:=\sum_{{\mathfrak{p}}:\text{minimal\;prime\;of\;A}}m({\mathfrak{p}},A)[\Spec A/{\mathfrak{p}}]\in Z(\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}})

the associated cycle in
Z(\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}})
.

In the sequel, we will use the following abusive notation for local formal schemes. Let
\Spf R
be a local formal scheme. Let
\mathcal{P}
be a property of scheme, we say that
\Spf R
satisfies
\mathcal{P}
if
\Spec R
satisfies
\mathcal{P}
. Moreover, for a given irreducible component
\Spec R/{\mathfrak{a}}\subseteq\Spec R
, we will refer to the formal subscheme
\Spf R/{\mathfrak{a}}\subseteq\Spf R
as an irreducible component. Similarity, we also put
Z(\Spf R):=Z(\Spec R)
(resp.,
Z^{d}(\Spf R):=Z^{d}(\Spec R)
, resp.,
Z^{\leq d}(\Spf R)=Z^{\leq d}(\Spec R)
).

Recall that we have fixed a
p
-adic potentially semistable non-crystalline Galois representation
\rho_{L}:{\rm Gal}_{L}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(E)
which admits a special
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration with parameter
({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L}}
(resp., with parameter
(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,{\mathbf{h}}},\widetilde{\bm{\delta}}_{\mathbf{h}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
). By inverting
t
,
\rho_{L}
admits an
\Omega_{r}^{\oplus k}
-filtration
\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}
of
\mathcal{M}
with parameter
({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}^{\otimes k}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{r,\emptyset},L}}
. We refer to Section
3.4
for the discussion on local models. Recall that
y
(resp.,
\widehat{y}
) is the closed point of the
E
-scheme
X_{r,L}
(resp.,
\mathcal{X}_{r,L}
) corresponding to the triple
(\alpha^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}),\alpha^{-1}({\rm Fil}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}},\bullet}),N_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}})
(resp.,
(0,y)
) and
w_{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathscr{W}^{\Delta_{n}^{k},\emptyset}_{n,L,\max}
is the element that encodes the relative localization of two flags.

In this section, we discuss two partially de-Rham Galois cycles with respect to the parabolic group (see
[
61
, Section 3.6]
) and the
\Sigma_{L}
-components (see
[
26
, Section 5.2]
) respectively.

Firstly, we have a commutative diagram of affine formal schemes (and the same for its
\Spec

:

such that

.

Let
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{Z}^{J}_{r,w^{\prime}},\widehat{y}}
be the pullback of
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Z^{J}_{r,w^{\prime}},y}
via the morphism
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\overline{{\mathcal{X}}}^{J}_{r,L},\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{r,L},\widehat{y}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{X}_{r,L},y}
. We denote by

{\mathfrak{Z}}^{J,\flat}_{r,w}\in Z(\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}})

the cycle corresponding via the embedding
\Spec\overline{R}^{J,\flat}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}}
to the cycle
[\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{J,\flat}_{\mathcal{Z}_{r,{w}},\widehat{y}}]
. For

and

, we put

and

, where

are given in

. We put

{\mathfrak{C}}^{J,\flat}_{r,w^{\prime}}:=\sum_{w^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{\Delta_{n}^{k},L}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n,L}}a^{J}_{w^{\prime},w^{\prime\prime}}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{J,\flat}_{r,w^{\prime}}\in Z^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}d_{L}}(\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}}).\;

(4.7)

Then the following statements are equivalent:
(1):{\mathfrak{C}}^{J,\flat}_{r,w^{\prime}}\neq 0
,
(2):{\mathfrak{Z}}^{J,\flat}_{r,w^{\prime}}\neq 0
and

(similar to the proof of

). When

, we drop the superscript

in above notation.

Proposition 4.11
.

We have the following facts.

(a)

If
J\subsetneq\Sigma_{L}
, then
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{Z}^{J}_{r,w^{\prime}},\widehat{y}}
is irreducible. Moreover, the irreducible components of

\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{Z}^{J}_{r,w^{\prime}},\widehat{y}}

(c)

\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{Z}^{\flat}_{\underline{w}_{0}},\widehat{y}}

{\mathfrak{C}}^{\flat}_{r,\underline{w}_{0}}={\mathfrak{Z}}^{\flat}_{r,\underline{w}_{0}}=r_{w_{0}}[\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,\tau,{\mathbf{h}}}},\rho_{L}}]
, where

Proof.

). We say more about the cycles

Proposition 4.15
.

The natural morphsim

\Spec\overline{\overline{R}}^{\Box}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\Spec{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}}_{{{X}}_{L},{y}}
factor through

Proof.

We only need to point out that all the length two subquotient of the deformation

\overline{\overline{X}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
of
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
. It remains to prove all the result for
\overline{\overline{X}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
.
Suppose
y_{A}:=(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}},A},\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet},j_{A})
(resp.,
y_{B}:=(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}},B},\mathcal{M}_{B,\bullet},j_{B})
) is an object in
\overline{\overline{X}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(A)
(resp.,
\overline{\overline{X}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(B)
). Suppose that
x_{A}
is isomorphic to
x_{B}
when modulo
K
. Then we show in Proposition
3.10
the sujectivity of the following map (similar to the sujectivity of
[
17
, (3.23)]
):

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,j})\longrightarrow\;{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR},J}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,j}))\times_{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR},J}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i,j}))}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i,j}).

(4.16)

for
j\leq i-2
(by the definition of
\Spec\overline{\overline{R}}^{\flat,\Box}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
, the contribution of
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i,i-1})
and
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i,i-1})
are zero, so we do not need to consider them) and thus the surjectivity of

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i-2}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\delta}_{A,i}^{-1})[1/t])\longrightarrow

(4.17)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}({\mathcal{M}}_{A,i-2}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\delta}_{A,i}^{-1})[1/t]))

\displaystyle\times_{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i-2}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\delta}_{B,i}^{-1})[1/t]))}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathcal{M}}_{B,i-2}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\delta}_{B,i}^{-1})[1/t]).

This is enough to prove the formally smoothness. By the construction of local models, the last isomorphism
\Spec{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}}_{{{X}}^{0}_{L},{y}}\cong\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\overline{{X}}_{r,L},{y}}
is obvious.
∎

Similar to the argument of (
4.6
), we denote by
\mathcal{Z}^{\flat}_{w^{\prime}}\in Z^{\leq\frac{n(n+3)}{2}d_{L}}(\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}})
the cycle corresponding via the embedding
\Spec\overline{\overline{R}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}}
to the cycle
[\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{Z_{w^{\prime}},y}]
, where
\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{Z_{w^{\prime}},y}:=\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{w^{\prime}},y}\times_{\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{X}_{L,y}}}\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\overline{{\mathcal{X}}}_{L},\widehat{y}}\cong\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Z^{0}_{w^{\prime}},y}
(note that it is not necessary equi-dimensional, see Remark
3.30
). We set as in (
4.7
):

\mathcal{C}^{\flat}_{w^{\prime}}:=\sum_{w^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}}a_{w^{\prime},w^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{Z}^{\flat}_{w^{\prime}}\in Z^{\leq\frac{n(n+3)}{2}d_{L}}(\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}}).\;

(4.18)

In this case, we have by the same arguments as for (
4.8
):

Corollary 4.16
.

Suppose
x=(\rho_{L},\chi)\in X^{\Box}_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
. The following equality holds:

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}\\
w_{\mathcal{F}}\leq w\leq w_{x}\underline{w}_{0}\end{subarray}}b_{w_{x}\underline{w}_{0},w}\mathcal{C}^{\flat}_{w}\in Z^{\leq\frac{n(n+3)}{2}d_{L}}(\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}}).

(4.19)

4.4
Locally analytic “Breuil-Mezard type” conjecture

Put
{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}:=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i}+i-1)_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L},1\leq i\leq n}
, which is a dominant weight of
{\rm GL}_{n,L}
with respect to
{\mathbf{B}}_{n,L}
.

We recall the Orlik-Strauch’s theory
[
52
, Theorem]
. Let
\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,L}}
be the Bernstein-Gelfand-Gelfand (BGG) category (see
[
10
, Section 2]
). The Orlik-Strauch functor (see
[
51
, Theorem]
, see also
[
10
, Section 2]
) associates, to an object
M\in\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,L}}
and a finite length smooth admissible representation
\pi
of
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)
, a locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{{I}}}(M,\pi_{I})
.

Recall that if
\underline{{\lambda}}\in X^{+}_{I}
, then
\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{O}^{\overline{{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{I},L}_{{\rm alg}}
. Let
I^{\prime}
be a subset of
\Delta_{n}
containing
I
, then
\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I^{\prime},L}}
is a full subcategory of
\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,L}}
. Therefore, for any object
M\in\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,L}}
, there is a maximal subset
I^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
such that
M\in\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I^{\prime},L}}
, then we say that
I^{\prime}
is maximal for
M
. In particular, for any
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, let

I(w)
be the subset of
\Delta_{n}
which is maximal for the
\overline{L}(-w\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}})
.

For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
, let
\epsilon_{w}:=|\cdot|^{\frac{1-n}{2}+w(n-1)}\otimes|\cdot|^{\frac{1-n}{2}+w(n-2)}\otimes\cdots\otimes|\cdot|^{\frac{1-n}{2}+w(1)}
be the smooth character of
{\mathbf{T}}(L)
over
E
(note that
\epsilon_{1}
is the character of
{\mathbf{T}}(L)
associated to the Zelevinsky-segment
\Delta_{[n-1,0]}(|\cdot|^{\frac{1-n}{2}})=[|\cdot|^{\frac{1-n}{2}+(n-1)},|\cdot|^{\frac{1-n}{2}+(n-2)}\cdot,\;\cdot,\;|\cdot|^{\frac{1-n}{2}+1},\;|\cdot|^{\frac{1-n}{2}}]
). For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
and
w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
, we put

I_{(w,w^{\prime})}:=\big({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}}(L)}\chi_{w\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}}}^{{1}/{2}}\epsilon_{w^{\prime}}\big)^{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}.

In particular,
\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}}}^{{1}/{2}}\epsilon_{1}
is equal to the trivial representation of
{\mathbf{T}}(L)
.

For
(w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime})\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}\times\mathscr{W}_{n}
, we put
i^{\infty}_{w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime}}:=i^{{\mathbf{L}}_{I(w^{\prime\prime})}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}_{I(w^{\prime\prime})}(L)}\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}}}^{{1}/{2}}\epsilon_{w^{\prime}}
. It is well known that the irreducible components of
i^{\infty}_{w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime}}
are given by the smooth generalized Steinberg representations
\{v^{\infty}_{J,I(w^{\prime\prime})}\}_{J\subseteq I(w^{\prime\prime})}
of
{\mathbf{L}}_{I(w^{\prime\prime})}(L)
over
E
(note that
{\rm St}^{\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{I(w^{\prime\prime})}(L)}:=v^{\infty}_{\emptyset,I(w^{\prime\prime})}
is the smooth Steinberg representation of
{\mathbf{L}}_{I(w^{\prime\prime})}(L)
). By the Orlik-Strauch construction
[
52
, Theorem]
, the irreducible components of
I_{(w,w^{\prime})}
are
\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime},J)}:=\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{I(w^{\prime\prime})}(L)}(\overline{L}(-w^{\prime\prime}\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}),v^{\infty}_{J,I(w^{\prime\prime})})
for
w^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
and
J\subseteq I(w^{\prime\prime})
with multiplicity
M_{w,w^{\prime\prime}}:=[\overline{M}(-w\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}):\overline{L}(-w^{\prime\prime}\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}})]
.

Put
\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}}:=\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}}}^{{1}/{2}}\epsilon_{1}
. We write
K_{0}({\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}})
for the free abelian group generated by the irreducible constituents of the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytically induced representation
I_{(w,w^{\prime})}
for
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
and
w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
. More precisely, write

\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime})}:=\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{I(w^{\prime\prime})}(L)}\Big(\overline{L}(-w^{\prime\prime}\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}),i^{\infty}_{w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime}}\Big).

Then we have
[\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime})}]=\sum_{J\subseteq I(w^{\prime\prime})}[\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime},J)}]
in
K_{0}({\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}})
. Denoted by
