C(w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime}):=\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime},\emptyset)}
the unique quotient of
\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime})}
. Note that
C(w^{\prime\prime},1)\cong\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{I(w^{\prime\prime})}(L)}\Big(\overline{L}(-w^{\prime\prime}\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}),{\rm St}^{\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{I(w^{\prime\prime})}(L)}\Big)
. It is clear that
\{{\Pi}_{(w^{\prime\prime},1,J)}\}_{w^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L},J\subseteq I(w^{\prime\prime})}
actually give a basis of
K_{0}({\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}})
.

For
\beta\in E
, we denote by
I_{(w,w^{\prime})}(\beta)
,
\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime})}(\beta)
,
{\Pi}_{(w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime},J)}(\beta)
and
C(w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime},\beta)
the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation
I_{(w,w^{\prime})}\otimes_{E}{\rm unr}(\beta)\circ\det
,
\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime})}\otimes_{E}{\rm unr}(\beta)\circ\det
,
{\Pi}_{(w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime},J)}\otimes_{E}{\rm unr}(\beta)\circ\det
and
C(w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime})\otimes_{E}{\rm unr}(\beta)\circ\det
.

Conjecture 4.17
.

Put
\widehat{T}_{L}^{\mathrm{spl}}:=\big\{(\delta_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n}\in\widehat{T}_{L}:\delta_{i}\delta_{i+1}^{-1}\text{\;is special}\big\}
(i.e.,
\delta_{i}\delta_{i+1}^{-1}:={\rm unr}(q_{L}^{-1})z^{{\mathbf{k}}}
for some
{\mathbf{k}}:=({\mathbf{k}}_{\tau})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{L}}
). There exists a unique homomorphism

{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\Delta_{n}}:K_{0}({\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}})\rightarrow Z^{\bullet}(\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}}).

For any absolutely irreducible constituent
[\Pi]\in K_{0}({\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}})
, let
\mathcal{C}_{\Pi}\in Z^{\bullet}(\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}})
be the image of
[\Pi]
via
{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\Delta_{n}}
. Then this homomorphism
{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\Delta_{n}}
is uniquely determined by conditions:

[\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})_{\underline{\delta}},(\rho_{L},\delta)}]=\sum_{\Pi\in K_{0}({\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}})}m_{\underline{\delta},\Pi}\mathcal{C}_{\Pi}

(4.20)

in
Z^{\bullet}(\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}})
for all
\underline{\delta}\in\widehat{T}_{L}^{\mathrm{spl}}(E)
.

Remark 4.18
.

We can also consider the homomorphism

{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime\prime}_{{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\Delta_{n}}:K_{0}({\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}})\rightarrow Z^{\frac{n(n+3)}{2}d_{L}}(\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}},\rho_{L}})
by forgetting components of non-maximal dimension.

Proposition 4.19
.

If the cycles
\mathcal{C}_{\Pi}
as in Conjecture
4.17
exist, then they are unique.

Proof.

We first note that
\underline{\delta}\in\widehat{T}_{L}^{\mathrm{spl}}(E)
is necessary condition for the non-vanishing of
[\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})_{\underline{\delta}},(\rho_{L},\delta)}]
. For the uniqueness, it suffices to show that

\mathcal{C}_{\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},1)}}:=\sum_{J\subseteq I(w^{\prime\prime})}\mathcal{C}_{\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},1,J)}}=\mathcal{C}_{C(w^{\prime\prime},1)}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi^{\prime}\neq\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},1,J)},\\
\emptyset\neq J\subseteq I(w^{\prime\prime})\end{subarray}}m_{\underline{\delta}^{\prime},\Pi^{\prime}}\mathcal{C}_{\Pi^{\prime}}

are unique (since
\mathcal{C}_{\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},1,J)}}=0
if
J\neq\emptyset
, see the proof of Theorem
5.20
) for all
w^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
and
J\subseteq I(w^{\prime\prime})
. Replacing
\underline{\delta}
by the unique locally algebraic
\underline{\delta}^{\prime}=\chi_{w^{\prime\prime}\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}
, we have that

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},1)}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Pi^{\prime}\neq\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},1,J)},\\
J\subseteq I(w^{\prime\prime})\end{subarray}}m_{\underline{\delta}^{\prime},\Pi^{\prime}}\mathcal{C}_{\Pi^{\prime}}

(4.21)

If
w^{\prime\prime}\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}
is maximal for the
\uparrow
(where “
\uparrow
” means the strongly linked relation, see
[
41
, Section 5.1]
for the notion of strongly linked and the BGG theorem), then we must have
\mathcal{C}_{\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},1)}}=[\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})_{\underline{\delta}^{\prime}},(\rho_{L},\delta)}]
. Otherwise, for any
\Pi^{\prime}\neq\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},1,J)}
, it has the form
\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime\prime},1,J^{\prime})}
for some
J^{\prime}\subseteq I(w^{\prime\prime\prime})
and
w^{\prime\prime}\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}\uparrow w^{\prime\prime\prime}\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}
(i.e.,
w^{\prime\prime}\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}
is strongly linked to
w^{\prime\prime\prime}\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}
). By induction, we can assume the cycle
\mathcal{C}_{\Pi^{\prime}}
are known, then we must have

\mathcal{C}_{\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},1)}}=\Big([\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})_{\underline{\delta}^{\prime}},(\rho_{L},\delta)}]-\sum_{\Pi^{\prime}\neq\Pi_{(w^{\prime\prime},1,J)}}m_{\underline{\delta}^{\prime},\Pi^{\prime}}\mathcal{C}_{\Pi^{\prime}}\Big).

The result follows.
∎

Theorem 4.20
.

The Conjecture
4.17
is true.

Proof.

Just set
\mathcal{C}_{\Pi_{(w^{\prime},1)}}:=\mathcal{C}^{\flat}_{w^{\prime}}
, then the result follows from the equality (
4.19
).
∎

Remark 4.21
.

We end this section with a remark on the “categorical” approach of Conjecture
4.17
. We keep the notation after
[
37
, Theorem 5.3.36]
. For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, recall that
{\mathfrak{X}}_{n,z^{w\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}}-\mathrm{tri}}\subseteq\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{n}
is the closed substack of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{n}
consisting of all
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules
D
such that
D
admits a triangulation of parameters
\chi_{w\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}}
. Put

{\mathfrak{X}}_{n,({\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}})-\mathrm{tri}}=\bigcup_{w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}}{\mathfrak{X}}_{n,z^{w\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}}-\mathrm{tri}}.\;

Then
{\mathfrak{X}}_{n,({\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}})-\mathrm{tri}}
is the closed substack of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{n}
consisting of all
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules
D
such that
D
admits a triangulation of parameters
\chi_{w\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}}
for some
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
. On the other hand, put

{\mathfrak{X}}_{n,[{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}}]-\mathrm{tri}}=\bigcup_{w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}}{\mathfrak{X}}_{n,({\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},w\cdot\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}})-\mathrm{tri}})

It is conjectured in
[
37
, Conjecture 6.2.34]
that there exists a unique injective group homomorphism:

{\mathfrak{a}}_{{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}}}:K_{0}({\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}})\rightarrow K_{0}(\mathrm{Coh}({\mathfrak{X}}_{n,[{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}}]-\mathrm{tri}})

which are determined by some similar conditions, where
K_{0}(\mathrm{Coh}({\mathfrak{X}}_{d,[{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}}]-\mathrm{tri}}))
is the Grothendieck group of coherent sheaves on the stack
{\mathfrak{X}}_{d,({\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}})-\mathrm{tri}}
. Now our homomorphism
{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\Delta_{n}}
in Conjecture
4.17
is obtained by compositing
{\mathfrak{a}}_{{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}},\underline{\delta}_{\mathrm{sm}}}
with the morphism mapping a coheret sheaf to its support (resp., and forgetting components of non-maximal dimension), together with a completion at our Steinberg point.

5
Global Applications

Under the Taylor-Wiles hypothesis, we show several global results on
p
-adic automorphic representations including a classicality result and the existence of all expected companion constituents. In this section, we assume
L\neq{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
.

5.1
Patched eigenvariety

5.1.1
Patching argument and global setup

We follow the notation of
[
23
, Section 2]
and
[
29
, Section 4.1.1]
(a brief summary of
[
23
, Section 2]
). Suppose that
p\nmid 2n
, and let
\overline{r}:\Gal_{L}{\longrightarrow}\GL_{n}(k_{E})
be a continuous representation such that
\overline{r}
admits a potentially crystalline lift
r_{\mathrm{pot.diag}}:\Gal_{L}{\longrightarrow}\GL_{n}(E)
of regular weight
\xi
which is potentially diagonalisable. We can find a triple
(F,F^{+},\overline{\rho})
, where
F
is an imaginary CM field with maximal totally real subfield
F^{+}
, and
\overline{\rho}:\Gal_{F^{+}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{G}_{n}(k_{E})
is a
suitable globalisation
(cf.
[
23
, Section 2.1]
) of
\overline{r}
. Let
S_{p}
be the set of places of
F^{+}
above
p
. For any
v|p
of
F^{+}
,
v
splits in
F
, and
F^{+}_{v}\cong L
.

We use the setting of
[
23
, Section 2.1]
, and can find the following objects

\{\widetilde{G},v_{1},{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p},\{U_{m}\}_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\},

where
\widetilde{G}
is a certain definite unitary group over
F^{+}
,
v_{1}
is a certain finite place of
F^{+}
prime to
p
, and
\{U_{m}=\prod_{v}U_{m,v}\}_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}
is a tower of certain compact open subgroups of
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+}}^{\infty})
(see also
[
29
, Section 4.1.1]
for a precise description). Write
U_{m}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}=\prod_{v\in S_{p}\backslash{\mathfrak{p}}}U_{m,v}
.

Let
\xi
(resp.,
\tau
) be the inertial type (resp., weight) of
r_{\mathrm{pot.diag}}
. By
[
23
, Section 2.3]
, we can attach a finite free
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-module
L_{\xi,\tau}
, which is a locally algebraic representation of
{\rm GL}_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
. Put
{\mathbb{W}}_{\xi,\tau}:=\bigotimes_{v\in S_{p}\backslash{\mathfrak{p}}}L_{\xi,\tau}
, which is equipped with an action of
U_{m}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}
by the construction. Put
W_{\xi,\tau}:={\mathbb{W}}_{\xi,\tau}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}E
.

Let
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U_{m},\mathcal{O}_{E}/\varpi_{E}^{k})
be locally smooth functions
\widetilde{G}(F^{+})\backslash\widetilde{G}({\mathbf{A}}_{F^{+}}^{\infty})/U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}_{E}/\varpi_{E}^{k}
such that
f(gg_{p}^{\mathfrak{p}})=(g_{p}^{\mathfrak{p}})^{-1}f(g)
for
g\in\widetilde{G}({\mathbf{A}}_{F^{+}}^{\infty})
,
g_{p}^{\mathfrak{p}}\in U_{m}^{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Let
\Sigma
be the set of primes
v
of
F^{+}
such that
v\not\in S_{p}\cup\{v_{1}\}
, and
v
is totally split in
F
. Then the
\mathcal{O}_{E}/\varpi_{E}^{k}
-module
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U_{m},\mathcal{O}_{E}/\varpi_{E}^{k})
is equipped with a natural action of the spherical Hecke operators

T_{w}^{(j)}=\bigg[U_{v}i_{w}^{-1}\bigg(\begin{pmatrix}\varpi_{F_{w}}1_{r,J}&0\\
0&1_{n-1}\end{pmatrix}\bigg)U_{v}\bigg]

where
w
is a place of
F
lying over a place
v\in\Sigma
of
F^{+}
which splits in
F
,
\varpi_{F_{w}}
is a uniformizer of
F_{w}
and
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
. We denote by
{\mathbf{T}}^{S_{p},{\rm univ}}
the (commutative)
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-polynomial algebra generated by such
T_{w}^{(j)}
and the formal variables
T_{\widetilde{v_{1}}}^{(j)}
. By
[
23
, Section 2.3]
, we can associate to
\overline{\rho}
a maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\rho}}
of
{\mathbf{T}}^{S_{p},{\rm univ}}
. Let
{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U_{m},\mathcal{O}_{E}/\varpi_{E}^{k})_{{\overline{\rho}}}
be the
localization of
{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U_{m},\mathcal{O}_{E}/\varpi_{E}^{k})
at
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\rho}}
. We put

\displaystyle\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{E})_{\ast}:=\varprojlim_{m}\varprojlim_{k}{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U_{m},\mathcal{O}_{E}/\varpi_{E}^{k})_{\ast}

(5.1)

\displaystyle\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E):=\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{E})_{\ast}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}E

for
\ast\in\{{\overline{\rho}},\emptyset\}
(roughly speaking, the space of
p
-adic algebraic automorphic forms of fixed type
\sigma(\tau)
(see
[
23
, Theorem 3.7]
, the “inertial local Langlands correspondence”) at the place
S_{p}\setminus\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}
, full level at
p
, and whose weight is
0
at places above
{\mathfrak{p}}
, and given by the regular weight
\xi
at each of the places in
S_{p}\setminus\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}
). Note that
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)
is a Banach space for the supermum norm and is equipped with a continuous (unitary) action of
\GL_{n}(L)
(by right translation on functions). Therefore
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)_{*}
with
*\in\{{\overline{\rho}},\emptyset\}
are admissible unitary Banach representation of
\GL_{n}(L)
with invariant lattice
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{E})_{*}
. Then the action of
{\mathbf{T}}^{S_{p},{\rm univ}}
on the localization
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{E})_{{\overline{\rho}}}
factors through certain Hecke algebra

{\mathbf{T}}_{\xi,\tau}^{S_{p}}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{E})_{{\overline{\rho}}}:=\varprojlim_{m}\varprojlim_{k}{\mathbf{T}}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{E}/\varpi_{E}^{k})_{{\overline{\rho}}},

where
{\mathbf{T}}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{E}/\varpi_{E}^{k})_{{\overline{\rho}}}
denotes the

\mathcal{O}_{E}/\varpi_{E}^{k}
-subalgebra of
{\rm End}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}/\varpi_{E}^{k}}({S}_{\xi,\tau}(U_{m},\mathcal{O}_{E}/\varpi_{E}^{k})_{{\overline{\rho}}})
generated by the operators in
{\mathbf{T}}^{S_{p},{\rm univ}}
.

For
v\in S_{p}
, we denote by
R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square}
the maximal reduced and
p
-torsion free quotient of the universal
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lifting ring of
\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{v}}:=\overline{\rho}|_{\Gal_{F_{\widetilde{v}}}}
(
\cong\overline{r}
, and therefore
R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square}\cong{R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}}
). For
v\in S_{p}\backslash\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}
, we denote by
R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square,\xi,\tau}
for the reduced and
p
-torsion free quotient of
R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square}
corresponding to potentially crystalline lifts of weight
\xi
and inertial type
\tau
. Consider the following global deformation problem (in the terminology
[
25
]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle=\bigg\{{F}/{F}^{+},T^{+},T,\mathcal{O}_{E},\overline{\rho},\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}^{1-n}\delta_{{F}/{F}^{+}}^{n},\{R_{\widetilde{v}_{1}}^{\square}\}\cup\{R_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\square}\}\cup\{R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square,\xi,\tau}\}_{v\in S_{p}\backslash\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}}\bigg\}

They by
[
25
, Proposition 2.2,9]
, this deformation problem is represented by a universal deformation ring
R_{\mathcal{S}}^{{\rm univ}}
. Note that we have a natural morphism
R_{\mathcal{S}}^{{\rm univ}}\rightarrow{\mathbf{T}}_{\xi,\tau}^{S_{p}}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{E})_{{\overline{\rho}}}
.

Following
[
23
, Section 2.8]
(or
[
29
, Section 4.1.1]
) we put

\displaystyle R^{{\rm loc}}:=R_{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\square}\widehat{\otimes}\Big(\widehat{\otimes}_{S_{p}\backslash\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}}R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square,\xi,\tau}\Big)\widehat{\otimes}R_{\widetilde{v_{1}}}^{\square},

where all completed tensor products are taken over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
. We put
g:=q-[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}]\frac{n(n-1)}{2}
, where
q
is a certain integer as in
[
23
, Section 2.8]
(or
[
29
, Section 4.1.1]
). We now put

\displaystyle R_{\infty}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle R^{{\rm loc}}\llbracket x_{1},\cdots,x_{g}\rrbracket,

\displaystyle S_{\infty}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{E}\llbracket z_{1},\cdots,z_{n^{2}(|S_{p}|+1)},y_{1},\cdots,y_{q}\rrbracket,

where
x_{i}
,
y_{i}
,
z_{i}
are formal variables. By
[
23
, Section 2.8]
(or
[
29
, Section 4.1.1]
), we get the following objects:

(1)

a continuous
R_{\infty}
-admissible unitary representation
\Pi_{\infty}
of
G=\GL_{n}(L)
over
E
together with a
G
-stable and
R_{\infty}
-stable unit ball
\Pi_{\infty}^{o}\subset\Pi_{\infty}
;

(2)

a morphism of local
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-algebras
S_{\infty}{\longrightarrow}R_{\infty}
such that
M_{\infty}:=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{o},\mathcal{O}_{E})
is finite projective as
S_{\infty}\llbracket\GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{L})\rrbracket
-module;

(3)

a closed ideal
{\mathfrak{a}}
of
R_{\infty}
, a surjection
R_{\infty}/{\mathfrak{a}}R_{\infty}\twoheadrightarrow R_{\mathcal{S}}^{{\rm univ}}
and a
G\times R_{\infty}/{\mathfrak{a}}R_{\infty}
-invariant isomorphism
\Pi_{\infty}[{\mathfrak{a}}]\cong\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\rho}}}
, where
R_{\infty}
acts on
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\rho}}}
via
R_{\infty}/{\mathfrak{a}}R_{\infty}\twoheadrightarrow R_{\mathcal{S}}^{{\rm univ}}
.

5.1.2
Patched eigenvariety and Hecke eigenvariety

We briefly recall the Hecke eigenvariety and Patched eigenvariety of
[
17
]
. Indeed, our input as in previous section is slightly different from that in
[
17
]
, but it is clear that all of the arguments in
[
17
]
apply in our case.

Put
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho},U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}=\big({\rm Spf}\;R_{\mathcal{S}}^{{\rm univ}}\big)^{{\rm rig}}
and
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\overline{\rho},U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}:=\big({\rm Spf}\;{\mathbf{T}}_{\xi,\tau}^{S_{p}}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{E})_{{\overline{\rho}}}\big)^{{\rm rig}}
. Then the natural surjective morphism
R_{\mathcal{S}}^{{\rm univ}}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbf{T}}_{\xi,\tau}^{S_{p}}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{E})_{{\overline{\rho}}}
shows that
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\overline{\rho},U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
is a closed subspace of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho},U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
. Let Hecke eigenvariety
Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})
be the schematic support of the coherent
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{\overline{\rho},U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\times\widehat{T}_{L}}
-module (equivalently,
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho},U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\times\widehat{T}_{L}}
-module)
\big(J_{{\mathbf{B}}}(\widehat{S}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},W^{\mathfrak{p}})^{{\rm an}}_{\overline{\rho}})\big)^{\vee}
on
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\overline{\rho},U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\times\widehat{T}_{L}
(equivalently,
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho},U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\times\widehat{T}_{L}
), where
J_{{\mathbf{B}}}
is the Jacquet-Emerton functor (see
[
31
]
) with respect to
{\mathbf{B}}
. This is a reduced rigid analytic variety over
E
of dimension
n[F^{+}:{\mathbf{Q}}]
, which admits (or factors through) an injections of rigid spaces over
E
:

Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{\overline{\rho},U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\times\widehat{T}_{L}\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho},U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\times\widehat{T}_{L}.

For
x=(\rho,{\delta})\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho},U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\times\widehat{T}_{L}
, it belongs to
Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})
if and only if

{\rm Hom}_{T(L)}(\delta,J_{{\mathbf{B}}}(\widehat{S}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},W^{\mathfrak{p}})^{{\rm an}}_{\overline{\rho}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}]\otimes_{k(\rho)}k(x)\neq 0,

where
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}\subset R_{\mathcal{S}}^{{\rm univ}}[1/p]
denotes the maximal ideal corresponding to the point
\rho\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho},U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
.

We next briefly recall the following version of patched eigenvariety, given in
[
29
, Section 4.1.1]
. Let
R^{{\mathfrak{p}}}=\Big(\widehat{\otimes}_{S_{p}\backslash\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}}R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square,\xi,\tau}\Big)\widehat{\otimes}R_{\widetilde{v_{1}}}^{\square}
and
R_{\infty}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}:=R^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\llbracket x_{1},\cdots,x_{g}\rrbracket
. Then we have
R^{{\rm loc}}=R^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\widehat{\otimes}{R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}}
(recall that
R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square}\cong{R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}}
) and
R_{\infty}=R_{\infty}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\widehat{\otimes}{R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}}
. Let
{\mathbb{U}}
be the open unit ball in
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
. We put
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}:=(\Spf R^{{\mathfrak{p}}})^{{\rm rig}}
and
\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}=({\rm Spf}\;{R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}})^{{\rm rig}}
. Then
(\Spf R^{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{\infty})^{{\rm rig}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}
. We have thus

{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}:=(\Spf R_{\infty})^{{\rm rig}}\cong(\Spf R^{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{\infty})^{{\rm rig}}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\cong{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}
. By
[
29
, Section 4.1.2]
, we see that
J_{{\mathbf{B}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})^{\vee}
is a coadmissible module over
\mathcal{O}({\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times\widehat{T}_{L})
, which corresponds to a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}
over
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times\widehat{T}_{L}
such that

\Gamma\Big({\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times\widehat{T}_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\infty}\Big)\cong J_{{\mathbf{B}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})^{\vee}.

Let
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times\widehat{T}_{L}
be the Zariski-closed support of
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}
. We call
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
the patched eigenvariety. By
[
29
, Theorem 4.1]
, we have

Proposition 5.1
.

(1)

For
x=({\mathfrak{m}}_{x},\chi_{x})\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times\widehat{T}_{L}
,
x\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
if and only if

J_{{\mathbf{B}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y},{\mathbf{T}}(L)=\chi_{x}]\neq 0
.

(2)

The rigid space
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
is reduced and equidimensional of dimension

g+nd_{L}+n^{2}(|S_{p}|+1)+[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}]\frac{n(n-1)}{2}.

(3)

The coherent sheaf
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}
is Cohen-Macaulay over
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
.

(4)

The set of very classical non-critical generic points is Zarisiki-dense in
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
and is an accumulation set. The set of very classical non-critical generic points accumulates at point
x=({\mathfrak{m}}_{x},\chi_{x})
with
\chi_{x}
locally algebraic.

(5)

The Hecke variety
Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})
is the reduced Zariski-closed subspace of
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
underlying the vanishing locus of
{\mathfrak{a}}\Gamma({\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}})
.

The Hecke eigenvariety
Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})
and patched eigenvariety
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
are related to the trianguline variety
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
as follows. Let
\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}:\widehat{T}_{L}\rightarrow\widehat{T}_{L}
be the automorphism defined by

\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\delta_{1},\cdots,\delta_{n}):=\delta_{B}\cdot(\delta_{1},\delta_{2}{\rm unr}(q_{L}^{-1}),\cdots,\delta_{n}{\rm unr}(q_{L}^{-(n-1)})).

Note that
\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\delta_{1},\cdots,\delta_{n})=(\delta_{1},\cdots,\delta_{n})\cdot\zeta
, where

\zeta:=\Big({\rm unr}(q_{L}^{1-n}),\cdots,{\rm unr}(q_{L}^{i-n})\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\tau^{i-1},\cdots,\;\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\tau^{n-1}\Big).

Then
\mathrm{id}\times\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
induces an isomorphism of rigid spaces
\mathrm{id}\times\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}:\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{\rho}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times\widehat{T}_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{\rho}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times\widehat{T}_{L}
. Let
\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\big(X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})\big)
be the image of
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
via this automorphism. Then the natural embedding

X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times\widehat{T}_{L}\cong(\Spf R_{\infty})^{{\rm rig}}\times\widehat{T}_{L}\cong{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times\widehat{T}_{L}

factors through

Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})\hookrightarrow X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\times\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\big(X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})\big).

(5.2)

Therefore,
\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}
induces morphisms

\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}:Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})\hookrightarrow X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})\rightarrow X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r}).

(5.3)

For each irreducible component
{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}
of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}
, there is a (possibly empty) union
X^{{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
of irreducible components of
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
such that we have an isomorphism of closed analytic subsets of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times\widehat{T}_{L}
:

X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})\cong\bigcup_{{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\big(X^{{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{{\mathfrak{p}}})\big)\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}.

(5.4)

5.2
Classicality

We fix a Galois representation
\rho\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho},U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
. We make the following Hypothesis.

Hypothesis 5.2
.

(a)

\rho
comes from a point
y\in Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})
of the form
y=(\rho,\underline{\delta})
.

(b)

\rho_{L}:=\rho_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
is a semistable non-crystalline
p
-adic Galois representation with full monodromy rank., i.e., the monodromy operator
N
on
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho_{L})
satisfies
N^{n-1}\neq 0
.

(c)

Let
{\mathbf{h}}:=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,1}>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,2}>\cdots>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,n})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
be the distinct Hodge-Tate weights of
\rho_{L}
. Let
{\alpha}\in E
such that
{\alpha},{\alpha q_{L}^{1}},\;\cdots,\;{\alpha q_{L}^{n-1}}
are
\varphi^{f_{L}}
-eigenvalues of
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho_{L})
. Then
D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{L})
admits a triangulation
\mathcal{F}
with parameters
({\rm unr}(\alpha)_{n})\cdot z^{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}})}
for
w_{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathscr{W}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{n,L,\max}\cong\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, where
{\rm unr}(\alpha)_{n}:=({\rm unr}({\alpha}),{\rm unr}({\alpha q_{L}^{1}}),\;\cdots,\;{\rm unr}({\alpha q_{L}^{n-1}}))
.

Recall that
{{\mathbf{h}}}_{i}=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
for
1\leq i\leq n
. For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, we put
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}:=(\rho,\delta_{B}\chi_{w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot{\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}}{\rm unr}(\alpha)\circ\det)\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho},U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\times\widehat{T}_{L}
(so
y_{1}=y_{\underline{w}_{0}\underline{w}_{0}}
). It is easy to see that
x_{1}:=(\rho_{L},({\rm unr}(\alpha)_{n})\cdot z^{{\mathbf{h}}})
(resp.,
x_{w\underline{w}_{0}}:=(\rho_{L},({\rm unr}(\alpha)_{n})\cdot z^{w\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}})})
) is the image of
y_{1}
(resp.,
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}
) via the morphism (
5.3
). Then
y=y_{w_{y}\underline{w}_{0}}
for some
w_{y}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
.

Theorem 5.3
.

(Classicality) Assume Hypothesis
5.2
. Suppose
x_{w_{\mathcal{F}}w_{0}}\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
, then
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm lalg}}_{\overline{\rho}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\rho}}]\neq 0
, i.e.,
\rho
is associated to a classical automorphic representation of
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+}}^{\infty})
.

Proof.

By applying Proposition
4.3
to
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
and
x_{w_{y}\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}})}
, there is a unique irreducible component
Z
of
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
passing through
x_{w_{y}\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}})}
. From (
5.4
) we thus necessary have
x\subseteq\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\big(Z\big)\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\subseteq{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\big(X^{{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})\big)\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}
for some irreducible component
{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}
of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}
. In particular, for
V\subseteq X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
a sufficiently small open neighbourhood of
x_{w_{y}\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}})}
in
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
, we have
V\subseteq Z\subseteq X^{{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
. We modify the proof of
[
15
, Theorem 3.9]
to our case.

Keep the argument and notation in the
[
53
, Section 5]
, the
R_{\infty}\otimes_{R_{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\square}}R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
-module
(\Pi_{\infty}(\sigma^{\circ}_{\min})[1/p])^{\vee}
is supported on a union of irreducible components of
\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}
, and we have to prove that
r_{y}
ia a point on one of these irreducible components. Recall that
Z(\rho_{L})
is the unique irreducible component of
\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}
containing
\rho_{L}
. It is enough to prove that
\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times Z(\rho_{L})\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}
is one of the irreducible components in the support of
(\Pi_{\infty}(\sigma^{\circ}_{\min})[1/p])^{\vee}
, or equivalently that
\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times Z(\rho_{L})\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}
contains at least one point which is in the support of
(\Pi_{\infty}(\sigma^{\circ}_{\min})[1/p])^{\vee}
. By Hypothesis
4.5
, we have a closed immersion
\iota_{{\mathbf{h}}}:{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}\hookrightarrow X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
. For any point
x^{\prime}=(r_{x^{\prime}},\delta^{\prime})\in\iota_{{\mathbf{h}}}(Z(\rho_{L}))\cap V\subseteq X^{{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
, by the choice of
V
, we may choose point
x^{\prime}
in
\iota_{{\mathbf{h}}}(Z(\rho_{L}))\cap V
such that the associated semistable non-crystalline Galois representation
r_{x^{\prime},{\mathfrak{p}}}
belongs to subspace
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min},\underline{w}_{0}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
(non-critical points in
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}
). Since non-critical point are classical (by the global triangulation theory, see
[
29
, Proposition 4.7]
), we see that
r_{x^{\prime}}
is in the support of
(\Pi_{\infty}(\sigma^{\circ}_{\min})[1/p])^{\vee}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 5.4
.

Apply the above proof to
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
case, together with the results in
[
60
, Theroem 5.6.5]
, the classicality theorem still holds for
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
case.

5.3
Patching functor

5.3.1
Recollections of variants of the BGG-category

Given
I\subseteq\Delta_{n}
, we apply the argument in
[
44
, Section 2]
to
{\rm GL}_{n,L}
, and get the Bernstein-Gelfand-Gelfand (BGG) category and its variants (deformations of BGG category)

\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,L}}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,L},\infty}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,L}}.

We modify the notation of
[
44
, Section 2]
by replacing the superscript
I
in
[
44
, Section 2]
by
\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,L}
.

For
J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
, let
{\mathfrak{m}}_{I,J}
(resp.,
{\mathfrak{m}}_{J}
) be the augmentation ideal of the envelope algebra
U({\mathfrak{z}}_{I,J})
(resp.,
U({\mathfrak{t}}_{J})
). We set
A_{I,J}:=U({\mathfrak{z}}_{I,J})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{I,J}}
and
A_{\emptyset,J}=U({\mathfrak{t}}_{J})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{J}}
the localizations. The canonical Lie algebra decomposition
{\mathfrak{z}}_{I,L}={\mathfrak{z}}_{I,J}\oplus{\mathfrak{z}}_{I,L\backslash J}
defines a surjective morphisms
U({\mathfrak{z}}_{I,L})\twoheadrightarrow U({\mathfrak{z}}_{I,J})
and
A_{I,\Sigma_{L}}\twoheadrightarrow A_{I,J}
of
A_{I,L}
-modules.

For
\mu\in X_{I}^{+}
, we define the
J
-deformed generalized Verma module of weight
\mu
as

\widetilde{M}^{J}_{I}(\mu):=U({\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{U({\mathfrak{p}}_{I,L})}(L_{I}(\mu)\otimes_{E}A_{I,J})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,L}},

where
U({\mathfrak{p}}_{I,L})
acts on
A_{I,J}
via the composition
U({\mathfrak{p}}_{I,L})\rightarrow U({\mathfrak{l}}_{I,L})\rightarrow U({\mathfrak{z}}_{I,L})\rightarrow A_{I,L}\twoheadrightarrow A_{I,J}
. If
J=\Sigma_{L}
, we drop the superscript
J
in
\widetilde{M}^{J}_{I}(\mu)
. Moreover, by
[
44
, Section 2.3]
, we have an isomorphism of
U({\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}})_{A_{I,L}}
-modules
\widetilde{M}^{J}_{I}(\mu)\otimes_{A_{I,J}}A_{I,J}/{\mathfrak{m}}_{I,J}={M}_{I}(\mu)
. The surjective morphism
A_{I,L}\twoheadrightarrow A_{I,J}
induces a surjective morphism
\widetilde{M}_{I}(\mu)\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{M}^{J}_{I}(\mu)
.

5.3.2
Locally analytic patching functor

For
y\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times\widehat{T}_{L}\cong{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times\widehat{T}_{L}
, denoted by
r_{y}
(resp.,
{\mathfrak{m}}_{r_{y}}
) its image in
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}={\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}
(resp., the corresponding maximal ideal of
R_{\infty}[1/p]
). Denoted by
r_{y,{\mathfrak{p}}}
(resp.,
r_{y}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}
, resp.,
z
) its image in
\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}=({\rm Spf}\;{R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}})^{{\rm rig}}
(resp.,
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}
, resp.,
{\mathbb{U}}^{g}
), and by
\underline{\epsilon}_{y}
its image in
\widehat{T}_{L}
(so
y=(r_{y}^{{\mathfrak{p}}},r_{y,{\mathfrak{p}}},z,\underline{\epsilon}_{y})=(r_{y},\underline{\epsilon}_{y})
).

We follow the argument and notation in
[
44
, Section 6.1]
. For
M\in\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{alg}}
, we have the functor

\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(M):={\rm Hom}_{U({\mathfrak{g}})}\Big(M,(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})^{U_{0}}\Big)_{\mathrm{fs}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{r_{y}}^{\infty}][{\mathfrak{m}}^{\infty}_{\underline{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{sm}}}]^{\vee},\;

(5.5)

where
U_{0}
is a compact open subgroup of
{\mathbf{N}}(L)
. In
[
44
, Section 6.1]
, the authors extend
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}
to larger category
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{alg}}
by putting

\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(M):=\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(M/{\mathfrak{m}}_{I}^{n}).\;

(5.6)

for
M\in\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{alg}}
. By
[
44
, Proposition 6.5]
, the functor
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}
is exact on
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{alg}}
. For each
M\in\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{alg}}
,
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(M)
is a finitely generated and Cohen-Macaulay
\widehat{R}_{\infty,r_{y}}
-module with dimension
t+\dim_{L}{\mathfrak{z}}_{I}
, where
t=g+\dim R^{\mathrm{loc}}
(see Section
5.1.1
).

Let
\mu
be a dominant weight. Assume that
\underline{\epsilon}_{y}=\underline{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{sm}}z^{w\cdot\mu}
with
\underline{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{sm}}
a smooth character of
{\mathbf{T}}(L)
and
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
. For any
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, denote by
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\cdot\mu}
the fiber at
w\cdot\mu\in{\mathfrak{t}}^{{\rm rig}}(E)
of the composition
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})\rightarrow\widehat{T}_{L}\xrightarrow{{\rm wt}}{\mathfrak{t}}^{{\rm rig}}
, where
{\mathfrak{t}}^{{\rm rig}}
denotes the rigid space associated with
{\rm Hom}_{E}({\mathfrak{t}},E)
and the map
{\rm wt}
sends a character of
\widehat{T}_{L}
to its weight. Consider

\mathcal{M}_{w\cdot\mu}:=\mathcal{M}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\cdot\mu}}.\;

By the argument in
[
61
, Section 4.4, (4.3)]
, we see that
the vector space of compact type
\Gamma(X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\cdot\mu},\mathcal{M}_{w\cdot\mu})^{\vee}
is topologically isomorphic to the following vector spaces of compact type:

{\rm Hom}_{U({\mathfrak{g}})}\big(M(w\cdot\mu),(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})^{U_{0}}\big)_{\mathrm{fs}}\cong{\rm Hom}_{U({\mathfrak{t}})}\big(w\cdot\mu,J_{{\mathbf{B}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})\big),

where
(-)_{\mathrm{fs}}
denotes Emerton’s finite slope part functor
[
31
, Definition 3.2.1]
. On the other hand, the quotient
M(w\cdot\mu)\twoheadrightarrow L(w\cdot\mu)
induces a closed immersion

{\rm Hom}_{U({\mathfrak{g}})}\big(L(w\cdot\mu),(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})^{U_{0}}\big)_{\mathrm{fs}}\hookrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{U({\mathfrak{t}})}\big(w\cdot\mu,J_{{\mathbf{B}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})\big).

Then the continuous dual
{\rm Hom}_{U({\mathfrak{g}})}\big(L(w\cdot\mu),(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})^{U_{0}}\big)_{\mathrm{fs}}^{\vee}
corresponds to a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{L}_{w\cdot\mu}
on
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\cdot\mu}
(so
\Gamma(X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\cdot\mu},\mathcal{L}_{w\cdot\mu}^{\vee})\cong{\rm Hom}_{U({\mathfrak{g}})}\big(L(w\cdot\mu),(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})^{U_{0}}\big)_{\mathrm{fs}}
). The schematic support of
\mathcal{L}_{w\cdot\mu}
defines a Zariski-closed rigid subspace
Y_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\cdot\mu}
in
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\cdot\mu}
. Let
Y_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\cdot\mu}^{{\rm red}}
be the underlying reduced analytic subvariety of
Y_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\cdot\mu}
. Then we have

\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w\cdot\mu))\neq 0
if and only if
y\in Y_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\cdot\mu}
, by
[
61
, (4.4)]
or
[
17
, (5.16), (5.18)]
. If non-zero, the schematic support of
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w\cdot\mu))
is supported in
\widehat{Y_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})}_{w\cdot\mu,y}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(M(w\cdot\mu))=\mathcal{M}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\cdot\mu},y},

(5.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w\cdot\mu))=\mathcal{L}_{w\cdot\mu}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\cdot\mu}}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\cdot\mu},y}\cong\mathcal{L}_{w\cdot\mu}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\cdot\mu}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\cdot\mu},y}.

5.4
Global companion points and companion constituents

We now state the main results on the appearance of companion constituents in the completed cohomology and the existences of global companion points. See Proposition
4.9
, Proposition
5.14
, Proposition
5.15
and Theorem
5.18
. We prove our main theorems by using the same strategy as in the proof of
[
61
, Proposition 4.7, Proposition 4.9, Theorem 4.10, Theorem 4.12]
and
[
44
, Proposition 7.2]
.

Hypothesis 5.5
.

Assume
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm lalg}}_{\overline{\rho}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\rho}}]\neq 0
and
(b),(c)
in Hypothesis
5.2
.

The first goal of this section is to show that
\{y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\}_{w\leq w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}
are global companion points of
y
, i.e.,
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in Y(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\overline{\rho})
for
w\leq w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}
. Put
{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}:=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i}+i-1)_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L},1\leq i\leq n}
, which is a dominant weight of
{\rm GL}_{n,L}
with respect to
{\mathbf{B}}_{n,L}
. Recall that
r_{y_{1}}:=(r_{y_{1}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\rho_{L},z)\in{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty}={\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}
is the image of
y_{1}
in
{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty}
. We have

\widehat{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,r_{y_{1}}}=\widehat{({\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box})}_{r_{y_{1}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\times\widehat{({\mathbb{U}}^{g})}_{z}\times\widehat{(\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box})}_{\rho_{L}}\cong\widehat{({\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box})}_{r_{y_{1}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\times\widehat{({\mathbb{U}}^{g})}_{z}\times X_{\rho_{L}}.

In the sequel, let
y
be a possible companion point of
y_{1}
(so that
r_{y}=r_{y_{1}}
). We rewrite
{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\infty,y}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}:=\widehat{({\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box})}_{r_{y_{1}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\times\widehat{({\mathbb{U}}^{g})}_{z}
for simplicity (so that
\widehat{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,r_{y}}={{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\infty,y}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times X_{\rho_{L}}
). We define

{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}={{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\infty,y}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\subset\widehat{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,r_{y}}.

We define some subspaces of
{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\infty,y}
(using partially de-Rham Galois cycles), which are related to the supports of the patching functor
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(-)
. For
J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
,
{I}\subset\Delta_{n}
and
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, we put

\displaystyle{{\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}}}^{\underline{I}^{J}}_{\infty,y}={{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\infty,y}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times X^{J,\underline{I}^{J}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}},\;\text{and\;}{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}^{\underline{I}^{J},w}_{\infty,y}={{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\infty,y}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times X^{J,\underline{I}^{J},w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}},

(5.8)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}^{\underline{I}^{J}}_{\infty,y}={{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\infty,y}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\overline{X}^{J,\underline{I}^{J}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}},\;\text{and\;}{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}^{\underline{I}^{J},w}_{\infty,y}={{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\infty,y}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\overline{X}^{J,\underline{I}^{J},w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}},

where
\underline{I}^{J}=\prod_{\tau\in J}I
, and we drop the superscript
J
if
J=\Sigma_{L}
. Let
Y
be a subspace of
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,\widehat{y}}
. Let
X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(Y)
be the essential image of
X^{\Box}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\times_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,\widehat{y}}}Y
via the formally smooth morphism
X^{\Box}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
. We put (recall the diagram (
4.12
))

\displaystyle\overline{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}={{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\infty,y}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\Spec\overline{\overline{R}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\cong{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\infty,y}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(\Spf\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\overline{{X}}_{L,{y}}}),

(5.9)

\displaystyle\overline{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}^{w}_{\infty,y}={{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\infty,y}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\Spec\overline{\overline{R}}^{w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\cong{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\infty,y}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(\Spf\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\overline{{X}}_{w,{y}}}).

We further put

\displaystyle{{{\mathfrak{Z}}}}^{w}_{\infty,y}={{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\infty,y}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(\Spf\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{Z}_{w},\widehat{y}}),\;\text{resp.,\;}\overline{{{\mathfrak{Z}}}}^{w}_{\infty,y}={{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\infty,y}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(\Spf\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{Z_{w},y}),

(5.10)

which is a closed subspace of
{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}
(resp.,
\overline{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}
).

5.4.1
General discussion on coherent sheaves and cycles

Assume
L\neq{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. We begin with a computation of the schematic supports
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash J}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
and
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash J}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee})
when
J\subsetneq\Sigma_{L}
.

Proposition 5.6
.

Fix
{I}\subset\Delta_{n}
,
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
and
J\subsetneq\Sigma_{L}
or
w=1
and
J=\Sigma_{L}
, we have

(a)

For
w^{\min}\underline{w}_{0}\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
, the formal scheme
{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{\underline{I},w^{\min}\underline{w}_{0}}
is reduced and is an irreducible component of
{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{\underline{I}}
.

(b)

The schematic supports of
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
and
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee})
are generically free of rank
m_{y,I,w}
over their support (it equal to
{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{\underline{I},w^{\min}\underline{w}_{0}}
or empty) for some integer
m_{y,I,w}
. If no empty, then
w^{\min}\underline{w}_{0}\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
. We obmit the subscript
I
in
m_{y,I,w}
if
I=\emptyset
.

(c)

For
w\underline{w}_{0}\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
, the schematic supports of the sheaves
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash J}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
and
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash J}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee})
are either
{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w\underline{w}_{0}}
or empty. If non empty, their schematic support is
{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w\underline{w}_{0}}
. These sheaves are generically free over its support
{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w\underline{w}_{0}}
. Thus we have
[\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash J}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))]=m_{y,w\underline{w}_{0}}[{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w\underline{w}_{0}}]\in Z^{0}({\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y})
.

Proof.

Part
(a)
is obvious. The proof of Part
(b)
is the same as
[
44
, Theorem 6.16]
and
[
44
, Proposition 7.2,(1)]
. As the sheaves,
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
and
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee})
are Cohen-Macaulay of the same dimension as
{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,r_{y}}^{\underline{I},w^{\min}\underline{w}_{0}}
(which is generically smooth and irreducible), the last assertion in Part
(b)
follows. For general
J\subsetneq\Sigma_{L}
, Part
(c)
follows by quoting the regular sequence generating the maximal ideal of
U(t_{J})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{J}}
. Note that
this sequence is regular for
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
and
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee})
by
[
44
, Proposition 6.5]
, and is also regular for
{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w^{\min}\underline{w}_{0}}
(since
X^{\Box}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
is formally smooth over
X^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{F}}_{\bullet},J}
). For
J=\Sigma_{L}
and
w=1
, this sequence is regular for
{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w^{\min}\underline{w}_{0}}
by Proposition
3.28
. As coherent sheaves,
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash J}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
and
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash J}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee})
are Cohen-Macaulay of the same dimension as
{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,r_{y}}^{w\underline{w}_{0}}
(which is generically smooth), the last assertion in Part
(c)
also follows.
∎

Recall in the proof of
[
17
, Theorem 2.4.7 (iii)]
,
(a_{w_{\tau},w^{\prime}_{\tau}})_{(w,w^{\prime})\in\mathscr{W}_{n}\times\mathscr{W}_{n}}
is the product of two upper triangular matrices
B_{\tau}P_{\tau}
with
1
on the diagonal. Let
M_{\tau}
be the diagonal matrix
(m_{y,w})_{(w,w)}
. Write
(a^{\prime}_{w_{\tau},w^{\prime}_{\tau}})_{(w,w^{\prime})\in\mathscr{W}_{n}\times\mathscr{W}_{n}}=B_{\tau}M_{\tau}P_{\tau}
and put
a^{\prime}_{w,w^{\prime}}:=\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}a_{w_{\tau},w^{\prime}_{\tau}}
.

Corollary 5.7
.

Assume Conjecture
3.27
holds. Then the Part
(c)
in Proposition
5.6
also holds for every
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
and
J=\Sigma_{L}
. In particular, we have equality
[\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}({M}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))]=[\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}({M}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee})]=m_{y,w}[{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w^{\min}\underline{w}_{0}}]\in Z^{0}({\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y})
. Moreover, by the exactness of the patching functor
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(-)
, we have

[\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}({L}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))]=\sum_{w^{\prime}\geq w}a^{\prime}_{w,w^{\prime}}[{{{\mathfrak{Z}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w^{\prime}\underline{w}_{0}}]=m_{y,w}[{{{\mathfrak{Z}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w\underline{w}_{0}}]+\sum_{w^{\prime}>w}a^{\prime}_{w,w^{\prime}}[{{{\mathfrak{Z}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w^{\prime}\underline{w}_{0}}]\in Z^{0}({\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}).

Proof.

The first assertion follows by quoting the regular sequence generating the maximal ideal of
U(t_{L})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{L}}
. Note that
this sequence is regular for
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
and
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee})
by
[
44
, Proposition 6.5]
, and is also regular for
{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w^{\min}\underline{w}_{0}}
if Conjecture
3.27
holds. The remainder of this corollary is now obvious.
∎

The following proposition compare
m_{y,I,w}
with
m_{y,w}
and study the sheaves
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
and
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee})
, if Conjecture
3.27
holds.

Proposition 5.8
.

Suppose that Conjecture
3.27
holds. For
{I}\subset\Delta_{n}
and
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, the sheaves
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
and
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee})
are generally free of rank
m_{y,w}
over their support. In particular, the integer
m_{y,I,w}
appeared in Proposition
5.6
is equal to
m_{y,w}
.

Proof.

As
{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}^{\underline{I},w}_{\infty,y}
is generically smooth for any
w
, we see that
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(M)
is generically free of rank
r
over its support, where
M
equals to
\widetilde{M}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
or
\widetilde{M}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee}
. As in the proof of
[
44
, Proposition 7.2,(7)]
, there exists an open subset
U
in the regular locus of
{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}^{\underline{I},w}_{\infty,y}
(as a scheme) such that
the intersection of
U
and the support of
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}({L}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
is non-empty. Then
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
and thus
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}({M}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
is locally free of rank
r
over its support intersected with
U
. By Corollary
5.7
,
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}({L}(w^{\prime}\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
is not supported at every generic points of
{{{\mathfrak{Z}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w\underline{w}_{0}}
if
w^{\prime}>w
, and
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}({L}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
has length
m_{y,w}
at each generic point of
{{{\mathfrak{Z}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w\underline{w}_{0}}
. Since
{L}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
has multiplicity one in
{M}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
, we have
m_{y,I,w}=m_{y,w}
.
∎

We let
m_{y}=\dim_{k(y)}\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L({\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))\otimes k(y)=m_{y,1}
. Since we have an injection
M(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow M({\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
, we have
m_{y,w}\leq m_{y}
for
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
. We will show that
m_{y,w}=m_{y}
in Proposition
5.19
, if Conjecture
3.27
holds.

Proposition 5.9
.

If
w_{\mathcal{F}}=\underline{w}_{0}
(i.e.,
y
is non-critical). Then
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}({M}(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))\neq 0
if and only if
w=\underline{w}_{0}
, and
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}({M}({\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))=\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L({\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))=m_{y}r_{w_{0}}[{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}_{\infty,y}]\in Z^{0}({\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,r_{y}})
, where
{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}_{\infty,y}:={\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\times\widehat{({\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box,{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}})}_{\rho_{L}}
.

Proof.

By
[
29
, Corollary 4.4, Lemma 4.6]
,
y=y_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}=y_{1}
does not have any other companion point (so that
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}({M}(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))\neq 0
if and only if
w=\underline{w}_{0}
) and is a smooth point on
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
. Therefore, the sheaf
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}({M}({\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))=\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L({\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
is free over its support of rank
m_{y}
. By
[
53
, Section 5]
, the schematic support of
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L({\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
is contained in the
{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}_{\infty,y}\subseteq{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}
, which is smooth and irreducible of the same dimension as the support of
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L({\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
. Thus we get the last equality by Proposition
4.11

(3)
. This completes the proof.
∎

Recall the Bezrukavnikov’s functor recollected in
[
44
, Section 7.2]
, which
associates, to an object
M
in BGG category
\mathcal{O}_{\chi_{\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}}
, a Cohen-Macaulay coherent sheaf
\mathcal{B}(M)
on
X_{L}^{\wedge}
, where
{X}_{L}^{\wedge}
is the completion of
X_{L}
along the preimage of
(0,0)\in\mathcal{T}_{L}:={\mathfrak{t}}_{L}\times_{{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}/\mathscr{W}_{n,L}}{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
in
X_{L}
. It satisfies the following properties:

(a)

For all
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, there is an isomorphism
\mathcal{B}({M}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee})\cong\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}_{w}}
;

(b)

For all
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, there is an isomorphism
\mathcal{B}({M}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))\cong\omega_{\overline{X}_{w}}
, where
\omega_{\overline{X}_{w}}
is the dualizing sheaf of
\overline{X}_{w}
;

(c)

Recall
P(\underline{w}_{0}\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
is the anti-dominant projective envelope. Then
\mathcal{B}(P(\underline{w}_{0}\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))
is equal to the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}
.

As in the proof of
[
44
, Corollary 7.5]
, we have the diagram

\textstyle{{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}^{\Box}_{\infty,y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{W^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{W}
\textstyle{{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}}
\textstyle{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,\widehat{y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\flat}}
\textstyle{\widehat{X}_{L,\widehat{y}}.}

(5.11)

By the proof of
[
44
, Corollary 7.5]
, the Bezrukavnikov’s functor
\mathcal{B}
induces a functor

\mathcal{B}_{y}:\mathcal{O}_{\chi_{\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Coh}({{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y})

such that for all
M\in\mathcal{O}_{\chi_{\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}}
, the sheaf
\mathcal{B}_{y}(M)
is a Cohen-Macaulay sheaf. In precise, for any
M\in\mathcal{O}_{\chi_{\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}}
, the sheaf
\mathcal{B}(M)
gives rise to a
G
-equivariant sheaf
\mathcal{B}(M)^{\widehat{}}_{\widehat{y}}
on
\widehat{X}_{L,\widehat{y}}
, the pullback of
\mathcal{B}(M)^{\widehat{}}_{\widehat{y}}
along
W
is a
G
-equivariant sheaf on
{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}^{\Box}_{\infty,y}
, and hence descends to a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{B}_{y}(M)\in\mathrm{Coh}({{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y})
. In particular, we have

Lemma 5.10
.

For all
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, we have isomorphisms
\mathcal{B}({M}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee})\cong\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{L,w},\widehat{y}}
and
\mathcal{B}(P(\underline{w}_{0}\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))\cong\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{X}_{L},\widehat{y}}
.

Remark 5.11
.

This functor is not exact in our case since
W
is no longer flat. Note that

W^{\ast}\mathcal{B}(M)^{\widehat{}}_{x_{{\rm pdR}}}=W^{\flat,\ast}(\iota^{\flat})^{\ast}\mathcal{B}(M)^{\widehat{}}_{\widehat{y}}
. Since the local model map
W^{\flat}
is formally smooth, we just need to treat the
G
-equivariant sheaf
(\iota^{\flat})^{\ast}\mathcal{B}(M)^{\widehat{}}_{\widehat{y}}
on
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{\flat}_{L,\widehat{y}}
.

Remark 5.12
.

In general, the right functor
R\mathcal{B}_{y}:\mathcal{O}_{\chi_{\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}}\rightarrow D^{b}_{\mathrm{Coh}}({{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y})
should
given by

R{\mathfrak{B}}_{y}({M})=RW^{!}\mathcal{B}(M)^{\widehat{}}_{\widehat{y}}=W^{\flat,\ast}R(\iota^{\flat})^{!}\mathcal{B}(M)^{\widehat{}}_{\widehat{y}},\;

where
D^{b}_{\mathrm{Coh}}({{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y})
is the (bounded) derived category of coherent sheaves on
{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}
, and
(-)^{!}
is the upper shriek functor (see
[
58
, Tag 0A9Y]
).

For
M\in\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{alg}}
, we also consider the following functor (i.e., we fix the smooth part
{\underline{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{sm}}}
of
\underline{\epsilon}
)

\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y,\underline{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{sm}}}(M):={\rm Hom}_{U({\mathfrak{g}})}\Big(M,(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})^{U_{0}}\Big)[{\mathfrak{m}}_{r_{y}}^{\infty}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\underline{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{sm}}}]^{\vee},\;

(5.12)

where
U_{0}
is a compact open subgroup of
{\mathbf{N}}(L)
. For
M\in\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{alg}}
, we define

\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y,\underline{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{sm}}}(M):=\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(M/{\mathfrak{m}}_{I}^{n}).\;

(5.13)

In this case, the functor
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y,\underline{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{sm}}}(-)
is only left exact on
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{alg}}
. The support of the coherent sheaf
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y,\underline{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{sm}}}(-)
is contained in
\overline{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}
.

Remark 5.13
.

Let
g:\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat}_{\overline{X}_{L},y}\cong\Spec{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}}_{\overline{X}^{0}_{L},{y}}\rightarrow\Spec\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{X}_{L},y}
be the natural closed embedding given in Proposition
4.15
. Define
R\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{y}:\mathcal{O}_{\chi_{\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}}\rightarrow D^{b}_{\mathrm{Coh}}({{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y})
by
R\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{y}({M})=R(g\circ W)^{!}\mathcal{B}(M)^{\widehat{}}_{y}
. In spirit of the categorical approach
p
-adic Langlands correspondence, the functor
R\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y,\underline{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{sm}}}(-)
given by
R\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y,\underline{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{sm}}}(M):=R{\rm Hom}_{U({\mathfrak{g}})}\Big(M,(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})^{U_{0}}\Big)[{\mathfrak{m}}_{r_{y}}^{\infty}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\underline{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{sm}}}]^{\vee}
should closely related to the “local” functor
R\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{y}(-)
.

5.4.2
Proofs of the existences of companion points (constituents) for
L\neq{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}

In this section, we will show that
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))\neq 0
, equivalently,
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash J}_{{I}}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))\neq 0
for any
J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
(in particular,
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}({M}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))\neq 0
) if and only if
w\underline{w}_{0}\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
.

The key step is the following proposition, which is an analogue of
[
61
, Proposition 4.7]
(but in our setting).

Proposition 5.14
.

Assume that
x_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
for any
w_{\mathcal{F}}<w
and
w_{\mathcal{F}}\neq\underline{w}_{0}
. Suppose that there exists a point
z\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}
such that
(\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(x_{w\underline{w}_{0}}),z)\in\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\big(X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})\big)\times{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}
are in
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})(E)
. Then
(\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(x_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}),z)\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})(E)
.

Proof.

We adapt the proof of
[
61
, Proposition 4.7]
to our case. For
w
such that
w_{\mathcal{F}}\leq w
, we write
z_{w\underline{w}_{0}}:=(\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(x_{w\underline{w}_{0}}),z)
. By
[
61
, Lemma 2.26]
, there exists a simple root
\alpha_{\tau}
(for one
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
) of
{\rm GL}_{n,L}
and a standard parabolic subgroup
{\mathbf{P}}_{L}
of
{\rm GL}_{n,L}
containing
{\mathbf{B}}_{L}
such that
w\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}})
is strictly
{\mathbf{P}}_{L}
-dominant (more precisely, strictly
{\mathbf{P}}_{\tau}
-dominant) and
w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}})
is not strictly
{\mathbf{P}}_{L}
-dominant (not strictly
{\mathbf{P}}_{\tau}
-dominant), where
w:=s_{\alpha_{\tau}}w_{\mathcal{F}}
and
\lg(w)=\lg(w_{\mathcal{F}})+1
. By assumption, we have

\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(M(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))\neq 0
(and equivalently,
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))\neq 0
). Similar to the proof of
[
61
, Proposition 4.7]
, we get an exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}(w_{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}\underline{w}_{0,\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}

\displaystyle\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}})\otimes_{E}L(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))

(5.14)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}(w_{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}\underline{w}_{0,\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}})\otimes_{E}L(w_{\tau}{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))\rightarrow 0.

Therefore, to show that
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))\neq 0
, it suffices to show that the natural surjection

\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}(w_{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}\underline{w}_{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}})\otimes_{E}L(w_{\tau}{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))

(equivalently,
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(M(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))
) is not an isomorphism. We prove it by contradiction. Assume that the above surjection is an isomorphism. Then this isomorphism shows that (the same as in the proof of
[
61
, Proposition 4.7]
)

{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,r_{y}}^{\{\tau\},w\underline{w}_{0}}=\Supp\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(\widetilde{M}^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}(w_{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}\underline{w}_{0,\Sigma_{L}\backslash\tau}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}})\otimes_{E}L(w_{\tau}{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))\subseteq{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,r_{y}}^{I_{\tau}}\;(\text{viewed as schemes}),

(5.15)

where
{\mathbf{P}}_{\tau}={\mathbf{P}}_{I_{\tau}}
for some
I_{\tau}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
. We show that this is impossible. The underlying topological space of
{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{\{\tau\},w\underline{w}_{0}}
is equal to the union of non-empty cycles denoted by
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{\{\tau\},w\underline{w}_{0}}_{\infty,y}
and
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{\{\tau\},w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}_{\infty,y}
. But by Lemma
4.14
,
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{\{\tau\},w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}_{\infty,y}
is not contained in
{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,r_{y}}^{I_{\tau}}
since
w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}})
is not strictly
{\mathbf{P}}_{L}
-dominant (not strictly
{\mathbf{P}}_{\tau}
-dominant), which lead a contradiction to (
5.15
).
∎

We are ready to prove the main theorem on global companion points. From now on, we set
\beta:=\alpha q_{L}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}
.

Proposition 5.15
.

Assume
x_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
. If
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})(E)
for some
w\leq w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}
, then

{\rm Hom}_{G}\Big(\Pi_{(w\underline{w}_{0},1)}(\beta),\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{r_{y}}^{\infty}]\Big)\neq 0.

By
[
61
, (4.1)]
, we see that

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{G}(\Pi_{(w\underline{w}_{0},1)}(\beta),\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{r_{y}}^{\infty}])\neq 0\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))\neq 0\Leftrightarrow y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in Y_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}.

(5.16)

Therefore Proposition
5.15
is equivalent to

Proposition 5.16
.

Assume
x_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
. If
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})(E)
, then
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))\neq 0
.

Remark 5.17
.

Note that
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))\neq 0
implies
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
and
x_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
. Therefore this proposition is stronger than predicting the set of companion points. But in our Steinberg case,
\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))\neq 0
is still slightly weaker than the locally analytic scole conejecture (see Theorem
5.20
, which is not far from Proposition
5.15
).

Proof of Proposition
5.16
.

We follow the route of the proof in
[
61
, Proposition 4.9]
. This proposition holds clearly in the case when
w=w_{\mathcal{F}}
. Suppose that
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\times{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min}}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}
for some irreducible component
{\mathfrak{X}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\subset{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}
. Recall that we have a closed immersion:

\iota_{{\mathbf{h}},w\underline{w}_{0}}:\overline{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})}\hookrightarrow X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r}).

Then the point
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}
is in the image of
\iota_{{\mathbf{h}},w\underline{w}_{0}}
since
w\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
. We can take an affinoid neighbourhood
U
of
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}
in
X
. Let
V
be a sufficiently mall open affinoid
V\subset\iota_{{\mathbf{h}},w\underline{w}_{0}}^{-1}(U)
such that
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in\iota_{{\mathbf{h}},w\underline{w}_{0}}(V)
. Note that
V\cap\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w\underline{w}_{0}}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})
is Zariski open dense in
V
. Since the any point
z
in
\big(\mathrm{id}\times(\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\circ\iota_{{\mathbf{h}},w\underline{w}_{0}})\times\mathrm{id}\big)({\mathfrak{U}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g})
satisfies that
w_{z}=w_{\mathcal{F}}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{U}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\subset\big(\mathrm{id}\times(\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\circ\iota_{{\mathbf{h}},w\underline{w}_{0}})\times\mathrm{id}\big)^{-1}\big(Y_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}\big).

(5.17)

for any
l\leq\lg(w)
. Therefore, we deduce:

{\mathfrak{U}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\overline{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\subset\big(\mathrm{id}\times(\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\circ\iota_{{\mathbf{h}},w\underline{w}_{0}})\times\mathrm{id}\big)^{-1}\big(Y_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}\big).\;

This shows that the companion point
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}
is in
Y_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}
.
∎

Theorem 5.18
.

Assume Hypothesis
1.1
, Hypothesis
5.5
and Hypothesis
4.5
and
x_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
. Then
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})(E)
if and only if
w\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
.

Proof.

The ”only if” part follows from Proposition
4.9
. We prove ”if” part by descending induction on the integer integer
l\leq\lg(\underline{w}_{0})
for the following hypothesis
\mathcal{H}_{l}
: if
y_{w_{0}}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})(E)
is a Steinberg point which is
w_{\mathcal{F}}
-critical, then for any
w_{\mathcal{F}}\leq w
and
l\leq\lg(w)
,
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})(E)
. For
l=\lg(\underline{w}_{0})
, there is nothing to prove. It suffices to prove
\mathcal{H}_{l-1}
when
\mathcal{H}_{l}
holds.

If
\lg(w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0})\geq l-1
, then the Hypothesis
\mathcal{H}_{l}
and Proposition
5.14
imply
y_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})(E)
, and hence
y_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}\in Y_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}
. Now we assume that
\lg(w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0})<l-1
. We need to prove that for any
w
such that
w\leq w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}
and
\lg(w\underline{w}_{0})=l-1
, we have
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})(E)
. The point
y=y_{1}=y_{\underline{w}_{0}\underline{w}_{0}}
is in the image of
\iota_{{\mathbf{h}},1}(\overline{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})})
since
w\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
. We can take an affinoid neighbourhood
U
of
y
in
X
. Then
V:=\iota_{{\mathbf{h}},1}^{-1}(U)\cap\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})
, which is Zariski open dense in the affinoid
\overline{V}:=\iota_{{\mathbf{h}},1}^{-1}(U)\cap\overline{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})}
. Since any point
z
in
\big(\mathrm{id}\times(\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\circ\iota_{{\mathbf{h}},1})\times\mathrm{id}\big)({\mathfrak{U}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g})
satisfies the condition in
\mathcal{H}_{l-1}
, and
w_{z}=w_{\mathcal{F}}
,
\lg(w_{z})=l-1
. Hence their companion points are contained in
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
. We hence deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{U}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\subset\big(\mathrm{id}\times(\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\circ\iota_{{\mathbf{h}},w})\times\mathrm{id}\big)^{-1}\big(X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})\big).

(5.18)

for any
l\leq\lg(w)
. Therefore, we deduce:

{\mathfrak{U}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\overline{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{X}}}_{\mathrm{tri},\mathcal{P}_{\min},w}^{{\mathbf{h}}-\mathrm{st}}(\overline{r})}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\subset\big(\mathrm{id}\times(\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\circ\iota_{{\mathbf{h}},w\underline{w}_{0}})\times\mathrm{id}\big)^{-1}\big(X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})\big).\;

This show that the companion point
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}
is in
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
. This completes the proof of
\mathcal{H}_{l-1}
.
∎

Proposition 5.19
.

Assume Conjecture
3.27
, then
m_{y,w}=m_{y}
for any
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
. Furhermore, we have
[\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}({M}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee})]=m_{y}[{\mathfrak{B}}_{y}({M}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))^{\vee}]=m_{y}[\overline{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w{w}_{0}}]
for all
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
.

Proof.

The proof is just a copy of the Step
10
in the proof of
[
17
, Theorem 5.3.3]
. We prove this Proposition by descending induction on the integer integer
l\leq\lg(\underline{w}_{0})
for the following hypothesis
\mathcal{H}_{l}^{\prime}
: for any
l\leq\lg(w)
, we have
m_{y,w}=m_{y}
. For
l=\lg(\underline{w}_{0})
, there is nothing to prove. It suffices to prove
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{l-1}
when
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{l}
holds. Choose
w_{1},w_{2},w_{3}
as in
[
17
, Lemma 5.2.7]
applied to
w
. Then we have the equality:

[\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(M(w_{i}\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))]=m_{y}[{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w_{i}\underline{w}_{0}}]\in Z^{0}({\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}),\;i\in\{1,2,3\}.

In particular, we have the equalities in
Z^{0}({\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y})
:

\displaystyle[{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w_{i}\underline{w}_{0}}]=[{{{\mathfrak{Z}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w_{i}}]+[{{{\mathfrak{Z}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w}],\;i\in\{1,2\}

(5.19)

\displaystyle[{\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w_{3}\underline{w}_{0}}]=[{{{\mathfrak{Z}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w_{3}}]+[{{{\mathfrak{Z}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w_{1}}]+[{{{\mathfrak{Z}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w_{1}}]+[{{{\mathfrak{Z}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w}].\;

On the other hand, we have the following equalities in
Z^{0}({\overline{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y})
:

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w_{i}\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))]+[\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))],\;i\in\{1,2\}

(5.20)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(M(w_{3}\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))]=

\displaystyle[\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w_{3}\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))]+[\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w_{1}\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))]

\displaystyle+[\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w_{2}\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))]+[\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))]

By the argument below
[
17
, (5.37)]
and Corollary
5.7
, we have
[\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w_{i}\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))]=m_{y}[{{{\mathfrak{Z}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w_{i}}]
for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
. Compare the equations in (
5.20
) and (
5.20
), we see that
[\mathcal{M}_{\infty,y}(L(w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}))]=m_{y}[{{{\mathfrak{Z}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w}]
and thus
m_{y,w}=m_{y}
(using Corollary
5.7
). This completes the proof of the first assertion. By Lemma
5.10
, we have
{\mathfrak{B}}_{y}({M}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\vee})=W^{\flat,\ast}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{w}},x_{{\rm pdR}}}
. This implies
[{\mathfrak{B}}_{y}({M}(w\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))]=[\overline{{{\mathfrak{X}}}}_{\infty,y}^{w{w}_{0}}]
by definition. The last assertion then follows from Corollary
5.7
.
∎

5.5
Locally analytic socle conjecture

For any
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,L}
, recall that the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic irreducible admissible representation
C(w\underline{w}_{0},1)\cong\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{w\underline{w}_{0}}(L)}\Big(\overline{L}(-w\underline{w}_{0}\cdot{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{h}}}),{\rm St}^{\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{w\underline{w}_{0}}(L)}\Big)
is the unique quotient of
\Pi_{(w\underline{w}_{0},1)}
.

Theorem 5.20
.

Assume Hypothesis
1.1
, Hypothesis
5.5
and Hypothesis
4.5
and
x_{w_{\mathcal{F}}\underline{w}_{0}}\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
. Then
C(w\underline{w}_{0},1,\beta)
is a subrepresentation of
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm an}}_{\overline{\rho}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{r_{y}}]
if and only if
w\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
\Pi_{(w\underline{w}_{0},1,J)}(\beta)
for any
\emptyset\neq J\subseteq I(w\underline{w}_{0})
cannot be embedded into the space
\widehat{S}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},W^{{\mathfrak{p}}})_{\overline{\rho}}^{{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{r_{y}}]
. Indeed, there exists an
w^{\prime}\neq 1
such that
\Pi_{(w\underline{w}_{0},1,J)}(\beta)
becomes the unique quotient of
{\Pi}_{(w\underline{w}_{0},w^{\prime})}(\beta)
. If
\Pi_{(w\underline{w}_{0},1)}(W)\hookrightarrow\widehat{S}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},W^{{\mathfrak{p}}})_{\overline{\rho}}^{{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{r_{y}}]
. Then we see that

{\rm Hom}_{G}\Big({\Pi}_{(w\underline{w}_{0},w^{\prime})}(\beta),\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)^{{\rm an}}_{\overline{\rho}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{r_{y}}]\Big)\neq 0.

This gives a companion point
(\rho,z^{w\underline{w}_{0}({\mathbf{h}})}\eta)\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
of
y
with
\eta
a
w^{\prime}
-twist of
{\rm unr}(\alpha)_{n}
, thus this point is not equal to
y_{w\underline{w}_{0}}
for any
w\geq w_{\mathcal{F}}
, a contradiction.
∎

. Let

. Firstly, we assume that:

Hypothesis 6.1
.

Assume

\rho

Suppose that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\delta_{1}:={\rm unr}(\alpha q_{L}^{-1})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{1,\Sigma^{+}(\delta)}}z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{2,\Sigma^{-}(\delta)}}\\
\delta_{2}:={\rm unr}(\alpha q_{L})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{1,\Sigma^{-}(\delta)}}z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{2,\Sigma^{+}(\delta)}}.\end{array}\right.

(6.2)

Theorem 6.2
.

Keep the above situation. Let
J^{\prime}\subset J
and
J^{\prime}\cap\Sigma(x)\neq\Sigma(x)

(1)

\dim_{E}T_{X,x}=4+3d_{L}

(2)

Remark 6.3
.

The fist two results are analogue of
[
26
, Theorem 2.2]
, and
(3),(4)
are analogue of
[

Proof.

Let
W:=\{(d_{1,\tau},d_{2,\tau})|\;d_{1,\tau}=d_{2,\tau},\tau\in\Sigma(x)\}
and
W_{J}:=\{(d_{1,\tau},d_{2,\tau})|\;d_{1,\tau}=d_{2,\tau}=0,\tau\in J\}
. As in

, (9)]
, one has an exact sequence

0\rightarrow K(\rho_{L})\cap T_{X,x}\rightarrow T_{X,x}\xrightarrow{f}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L}}(\rho_{L},\rho_{L}).\;

(6.4)

We need to control the
\mathrm{Im}(f)
. For
t\in T_{X,x}:\Spec E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}\rightarrow X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
, we get the following map. The composition
\Spec E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}\rightarrow X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})\rightarrow\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}
gives a continuous representation
\widetilde{\rho_{L}}
(we view it as an element in

). We define a

-linear map
\nabla:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L}}(\rho_{L},\rho_{L})\rightarrow E^{2d_{L}}
by sending

to

such that

equals to the Sen’s weights of
\widetilde{\rho_{L}}
. Secondly, the composition
\Spec E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}\rightarrow X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})\rightarrow\widehat{T}_{L}
also gives a deformation
\widetilde{\delta}=\widetilde{\delta}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{\delta}_{2}
of
{\delta}={\delta}_{1}\otimes{\delta}_{2}
. The two properties in
[
26
, (12)]
also hold in semistable non-crystalline case. Let
V_{1}
be the kernel of the composition:

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D,D)\rightarrow

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\delta^{\prime}_{1}),D)

(6.5)

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\delta^{\prime}_{1}),\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\delta^{\prime}_{2}))\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\delta_{1}),\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\delta^{\prime}_{2})).\;

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow K(\rho_{L})\rightarrow T_{X_{J-{\rm dR}}(\underline{{\mathbf{h}}}_{J}),x}\xrightarrow{f}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L},g,J}(\rho_{L},\rho_{L})\cap V_{1}\cap\nabla^{-1}(W)\rightarrow 0,

(6.6)

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow K(\rho_{L})\rightarrow T_{X_{J^{\prime}-{\rm dR}}(\underline{{\mathbf{h}}}_{J}),x}\xrightarrow{f}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L},g,J^{\prime}}(\rho_{L},\rho_{L})\cap V_{1}\cap\nabla^{-1}(W\cap W_{J})\rightarrow 0.

We claim that:

(a)

We instead of considering the cohomology of the corresponding
