Gal}_{L},B_{E}(\delta^{\prime}_{2}(\delta_{1})^{-1}))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

We then have
\mathrm{Im}{\mathfrak{j}}_{1}={\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g,J}({\rm Gal}_{L},W(\rho_{L})\otimes W(\rho_{L})^{\vee})
,
\mathrm{Im}{\mathfrak{j}}_{2}={\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g,J}({\rm Gal}_{L},B_{E}(\delta^{\prime}_{2})\otimes W(\rho_{L})^{\vee})
, and
\mathrm{Im}{\mathfrak{j}}_{3}={\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g,J}({\rm Gal}_{L},B_{E}(\delta^{\prime}_{2}(\delta^{\prime}_{1})^{-1}))
,
\mathrm{Im}{\mathfrak{j}}_{4}={\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g,J}({\rm Gal}_{L},B_{E}(\delta^{\prime}_{2}(\delta_{1})^{-1}))
. By
[
28
, (1.7)]
, we have

\mathrm{Im}(f^{\prime})={\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g,\Sigma^{-}(\delta)\cup\Sigma(x)}({\rm Gal}_{L},B_{E}(\delta^{\prime}_{2})\otimes W(\rho_{L})^{\vee}\otimes B_{E}(\eta^{\#}_{J})).\;

Therefore, we deduce that the composition (
6.7
) restricts to a surjective map:

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g,J}({\rm Gal}_{L},W(\rho_{L})\otimes W(\rho_{L})^{\vee})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g,J\cup\Sigma^{-}(\delta)}({\rm Gal}_{L},B_{E}(\delta^{\prime}_{2}(\delta_{1})^{-1})).

Note that
\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g,J\cup\Sigma^{-}(\delta)}({\rm Gal}_{L},B_{E}(\delta^{\prime}_{2}(\delta_{1})^{-1}))=1+d_{L}-|J|
(the
\Sigma^{-}(\delta)
-component has no effect). In this case, we get that
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L},g,J}(\rho_{L},\rho_{L})\cap V_{1}=\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L}}(\rho_{L},\rho_{L})-(2|J|+d_{L})
. We complete the proof of the claim. Note that
W_{J}\subset W
since
\Sigma(x)\subseteq J
. The same argument as in the proof of
[
26
, Proposition 2.8]
show
(3)
and
(4)
. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L},g,J}(\rho_{L},\rho_{L})\cap V_{1}\cap\nabla^{-1}(W)=

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L},g,J}(\rho_{L},\rho_{L})\cap V_{1}

(6.9)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L}}(\rho_{L},\rho_{L})-(2|J|+d_{L}).\;

Applying the above formula to
J^{\prime}
. Since
J^{\prime}\cap\Sigma(x)\neq\Sigma(x)
, then
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L},g,J^{\prime}}(\rho_{L},\rho_{L})\cap V_{1}\cap\nabla^{-1}(W\cap W_{J})
is the preimage of
W\cap W_{J}
via the surjective map
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L},g,J^{\prime}}(\rho_{L},\rho_{L})\cap V_{1}\cap\nabla^{-1}(W)\rightarrow W\cap W_{J^{\prime}}
. This implies
(4)
.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

If
\Sigma(x)\subseteq J^{\prime}
, then this computation is not clear since we do not know the image of
\nabla:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L},g,J^{\prime}}(\rho_{L},\rho_{L})\cap V_{1}\rightarrow W
explicitly. Note that
W_{J}\subset W
since
\Sigma(x)\subseteq J
. We claim that
W_{J\cup\Sigma^{-}(\delta)}\subset\mathrm{Im}(\nabla)
. The Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens formula
[
28
, Theorem 2.1]
describes the obstructions of liftings to
E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
. We show that the induced map
\nabla:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L},g,J}(\rho_{L},\rho_{L})\cap V_{1}\rightarrow W_{J\cup\Sigma^{-}(\delta)}
is surjective. Let
\widetilde{\delta_{1}^{\prime}}:L^{\times}\rightarrow E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
be a continuous character with
\widetilde{\delta_{1}^{\prime}}\equiv{\delta_{1}^{\prime}}{\textrm{mod}}\;\epsilon
and
{\rm wt}_{\tau}(\widetilde{\delta_{1}^{\prime}})={\rm wt}_{\tau}({\delta_{1}^{\prime}})
for
\tau\in J\cup\Sigma^{-}(\delta)
. Then
[
28
, Theorem 2.1]
and its proof show that there exists a deformation
\widetilde{W}
of
W(\rho_{L})
over
E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
and a deformation
\widetilde{\delta_{2}^{\prime}}
of
{\delta_{2}^{\prime}}
over
E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
such that
\widetilde{W}
comes from
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm Gal}_{L},g,J\cup\Sigma^{-}(\delta)}(\rho_{L},\rho_{L})\cap V_{1}
with parameter
(\widetilde{\delta_{1}^{\prime}},\widetilde{\delta_{2}^{\prime}})
if and only if
(\widetilde{\delta_{2}^{\prime}}\widetilde{\delta_{2}^{\prime}}^{-1}(p)-1)/\epsilon+\sum_{\tau\in\Sigma^{+}(\delta)\backslash\Sigma(x)}\mathscr{L}_{\tau}(d_{1,\tau}-d_{2,\tau})=0
(so the value
\widetilde{\delta_{2}^{\prime}}(p)
is uniquely determined), where
\mathscr{L}_{\tau}
are the
\mathscr{L}
-invariants defined in
[
28
, Definition 1.20]
, and
({\rm wt}_{\tau}(\delta_{1})+\epsilon d_{1,\tau},{\rm wt}_{\tau}(\delta_{1})+\epsilon d_{2,\tau})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
equals to the Sen’s weights of
\widetilde{W}
. Thus, by choosing the value of
\widetilde{\delta_{2}^{\prime}}\widetilde{\delta_{2}^{\prime}}^{-1}(p)
carefully, the above assertion follows.

As a corollary, we immediately get:

Corollary 6.5
.

If Let
J^{\prime}\subset J
such that
J^{\prime}\cap\Sigma(x)\neq\Sigma(x)
and
(J\backslash J^{\prime})\cap\Sigma(x)\neq\emptyset
, then
X_{J-{\rm dR}}({{\mathbf{h}}}_{J})
is a proper closed subspace of
X_{J^{\prime}-{\rm dR}}({{\mathbf{h}}}_{J})
.

Put
\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}=({\mathbf{h}}_{1},{\mathbf{h}}_{2}+1)
. In
[
26
, Section 3.3.2]
, the author introduces some stratifications on patched eigenvariety. For any
J\subset\Sigma_{L}
, we get a reduced closed subspace
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J})
of
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}})
such that
(\rho,\delta^{\prime})\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J})
if and only if the eigenspace
J_{{\mathbf{B}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}(\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J}))[{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho},{\mathbf{T}}(L)=\delta^{\prime}]\neq 0
, where
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}(\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J}):=(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}\otimes_{E}L(\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J}^{\vee}))^{\Sigma_{L}\backslash J}\otimes L(\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J})
. We have a natural morphism
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J})\rightarrow\widehat{T}_{L}(\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J})
. For
J^{\prime}\subseteq J
, we put
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J},J^{\prime}):=X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J^{\prime}}){\times}_{\widehat{T}_{L}(\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J^{\prime}})}\widehat{T}_{L}(\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J})
. Such subspaces fit into the following commutative diagram (by
[
26
, (22)]
):

\textstyle{X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J},J^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J^{\prime}})^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{T}_{L}(\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{T}_{L}(\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{T}_{L}(\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{T}_{L}(\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{T}_{L},}

(6.10)

where the horizontal maps are closed embedding, and the second and fourth square are cartesian. The injection (
5.2
) induces a closed embedding (by
[
26
, (34)]
):

X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J})^{\mathrm{red}}\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\times\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\big(X_{J-{\rm dR}}({{\mathbf{h}}}_{J})).\;

(6.11)

By the same argument as in the proof of
[
26
, Theorem 3.21]
, we show that

Theorem 6.6
.

(Infinitesimal “
R=T
” results)
Let
y=(r_{y},\delta)\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J})(E)
such that
r_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
is isomorphic to
\rho_{L}
. Suppose
x
is spherical (i.e.,
\delta
is locally algebraic and
\delta z^{-{\rm wt}(\delta)}
is unramified). Then
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J})
is smooth at
x
, and we have a natural isomorphism of complete regular noetherian local
E
-algebras:

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J}),{x}}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\big(X_{J-{\rm dR}}(\underline{{\mathbf{h}}}_{J})\big)\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g},x}.

By Theorem
6.6
and Corollary
6.5
, we get

Corollary 6.7
.

Keep the situation of Theorem
6.6
. Let
J^{\prime}\subset J
and
J^{\prime}\cap\Sigma(x)\neq\Sigma(x)
. The following statements are equivalent:

(i)

The natural projection
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J},J^{\prime}),{x}}\twoheadrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J}),{x}}
is an isomorphism;

(ii)

X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J},J^{\prime})
is smooth at
x
;

(iii)

(J\backslash J^{\prime})\cap\Sigma(x)=\emptyset
.

We now state the locally analytic socle conjecture for
{\rm GL}_{2}(L)
case. For
J\subset\Sigma_{L}
, we denote

I^{c}_{S}(\alpha,{\mathbf{h}}):=C(1,s_{J})=\mathcal{F}^{{\rm GL}_{2}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}}(L)}(\underline{L}(-s_{J}\cdot\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}})},1).\;

Conjecture 6.8
.

Assume Hypothesis
6.1
. Then
I^{c}_{S}(\alpha,{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\widehat{S}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)_{\overline{\rho}}^{{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}]
if and only if
S\subseteq\Sigma(y)
.

In
{\rm GL}_{2}(L)
-case, this conjecture is in fact equivalent to the following conjecture on companion points on
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}})
. For
J\subset\Sigma_{L}
, we put
\chi_{J}^{c}=({\rm unr}(\alpha q_{L}^{-1})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{1,\Sigma_{L}\backslash J}}z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{2,J}})\otimes({\rm unr}(\alpha q_{L}^{-1})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{1,\Sigma_{L}\backslash J}+1}z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{2,J}+1})
.

Conjecture 6.9
.

Assume Hypothesis
6.1
. Then
(r_{y},\chi)\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}})
if and only if
\chi=\chi_{J}^{c}
for some
J\subseteq\Sigma(x)
. In particular, the point
y_{J}:=(r_{y},\chi_{J}^{c})
lie in
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J})
.

Theorem 6.10
.

Let
y=(r_{y},\delta)\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},J})(E)
such that
r_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
is isomorphic to
\rho_{L}
. Suppose
y
is spherical and
r_{y,\widetilde{v}}
is generic for
v\in\Sigma(U^{p})\backslash S_{p}
. Let
x
be the associated point in
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r},{{\mathbf{h}}}_{J})
. Suppose
\Sigma(x)\neq\emptyset
. Then for all
\tau\in\Sigma(x)
,
y_{\tau}^{c}=(r_{y},\delta_{\tau}^{c})\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{{\rho}},\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}},\Sigma^{+}(\delta)\backslash\tau})(E)
.

Proof.

We put
J=\Sigma^{+}(\delta)
and
J^{\prime}=\Sigma^{+}(\delta)\backslash\tau
. Then we have
J^{\prime}\cap\Sigma(x)\neq\Sigma(x)
. Then the claim, i.e.,
(37)
in the proof of

[
26
, Theorem 4.4]
also holds in our case, instead by using our Theorem
6.6
and Corollary
6.7
. Thus this theorem follows from the Breuil’s adjunction formula, as in the proof of
[
26
, Theorem 4.4, Page 94]
.
∎

Finally, similar to the proof of
[
26
, Corollary 46]
, we actually get

Theorem 6.11
.

The Conjecture
6.9
(and hence Conjecture
6.8
) is true.

Remark 6.12
.

The above theorem gives a complete version of
[
28
, Theorem 4.22]
, which only proves Conjecture
6.9
(and hence Conjecture
6.8
) if
|S|=1
.
